# BasaltDB contracts

Normative contract for the surfaces BasaltDB exposes: **MCP tools** served by the engine (through the per-agent shim), the **`basalt` CLI**, the **approval and revert pages**, and the **`basalt_meta` schema** the guard installs in the person's Postgres. This file fixes the wire rules. There is no REST API, no OpenAPI/AsyncAPI and no event stream. The HTTP surfaces are the engine's loopback [local pages](#local-pages) and, from M4.1, its [direct listener](#direct-transport) behind the person's own tunnel, which serves only a probe and the encrypted link session. The local pages are the pages plus a same-origin relay for them, not an API for other clients. From M3.5 the same pages API also runs inside the end-to-end encrypted [Vault channel](#vault-channel), the one hosted piece before M4 (owner decision VC-1).

## Process: contract first

- A new MCP tool, a change to a tool's input or output schema, a new error code or a new `basalt_meta` table or function lands in a **contract-only PR** first: tool schemas under `api/mcp/`, this file if a convention changes, and the contract test that checks the served tool list against the schemas. No behaviour change in the same PR unless CI needs it.
- Every tool in `api/mcp/` declares a status, `IMPLEMENTED` or `PLANNED`. A contract test fails if a served tool is missing from the schemas, or an `IMPLEMENTED` tool is not served.
- One file per tool, `api/mcp/tools/<name>.json`: `name`, `status`, `milestone`, `description`, `inputSchema` (JSON Schema, `additionalProperties: false`), `outputSchema`, and `errors` (codes from the table below). The engine embeds these files and lists each tool's `inputSchema` as written, so the schema has one source. The contract test (`api/mcp/contract_test.go`) checks camelCase property names, SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE enum values, closed input schemas, and that every declared error code is in this file.
- **Planned parts of a served tool.** A change to an `IMPLEMENTED` tool lands in its contract before the engine serves it. An input property whose `description` starts with `Planned (` is in the contract but not served: the engine leaves it out of the tool's listed `inputSchema` and refuses it as an unknown argument (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`) until the change that serves it removes the marker (`mcpcontract.Load` serves, `mcpcontract.LoadAll` is the contract as written). Likewise a [next-steps](#next-steps) entry with `"planned": true` is contracted but not returned yet; its row in the tables below starts its Steps with `Planned (`, and when it lands it replaces the served entry of the same key, if there is one. Output properties and enum values marked `Planned` in their description are not returned yet. A planned input property may not appear in its schema's `required`, `dependentRequired` or `oneOf`/`anyOf`/`allOf` (loading the contracts fails), so leaving it out never changes what the served schema accepts otherwise. **Version-scoped payload schemas are outside this convention**: a schema under `api/mcp/revert/` whose description names a `basalt_meta` version (`revert-binding.schema.json`, "the shape from basalt_meta version 20") describes that version's format only, as a whole, and the guards before it emit the older format its description names.
- Breaking changes are allowed until M4 (nothing has shipped). Record each one in the PR description.

## Wire format

- JSON keys we define (MCP arguments and results, the change record, CLI `--json` output, config) are **camelCase**. Exception: column names and values from the person's tables pass through exactly as Postgres returns them, except a `timestamptz` value, which follows the Timestamps rule below.
- **Enum values are SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE** everywhere (Go consts, JSON, `validate` `oneof`, `basalt_meta` values, docs). Go identifiers stay PascalCase (`StatusApplied = "APPLIED"`).
- **IDs:** UUIDv7 for every id BasaltDB generates (`changeId`, run, task, approval), canonical text on the wire, `uuid` in Postgres. Row versions are `"xmin:<n>"`.
- **Timestamps:** RFC 3339 UTC with `Z` (`2026-11-10T06:02:11Z`), fractional seconds kept when present (`2026-11-10T06:02:11.513958Z`). This holds for the values of the person's tables too: a `timestamptz` column is reformatted to it wherever the engine returns row values to an agent or the person's CLI (`query`, `run_history`, `basalt log`, the lines of `preview_revert` and `revert_plan` and of `basalt revert`), whatever `TimeZone` the session or the writing session had. In `query` the column's type decides (Postgres prints it in `DateStyle` ISO, which the engine sets; see below). Inside stored row values (the ledger, revert lines) there is no type, so any string at any depth in the exact shape of a `timestamptz` in JSON (`2026-11-10T08:02:11+02:00`: date, `T`, time with an optional fraction of 1 to 6 digits, numeric offset `+HH:MM`, with `:SS` when the zone has seconds) is reformatted, a text column holding such a string and the elements of a `timestamptz[]` column included. In `query` an array, json, jsonb or text column is returned as Postgres prints it, so the same array shows as Z strings in `run_history` and in Postgres form in `query`. A `timestamp` without time zone, a `date`, `infinity` and any value RFC 3339 cannot write (BC, year over 9999) are not touched. What the guard stores and the person signs (held and revert lines, `rowsHash`, the ledger's row values) is not reformatted: it keeps Postgres's JSON form, so the signing pages show `2026-11-10T06:02:11+00:00`. It does not depend on the session either: every transaction in which the engine writes, reads for an agent, previews, holds or reverts, and those of `basalt revert` (on a direct connection or through the person socket), first sets `TimeZone` to `UTC`, `DateStyle` to `ISO, MDY`, `IntervalStyle` to `postgres`, `extra_float_digits` to `1`, `lc_monetary` to `C` and `bytea_output` to `hex` for that transaction only (`set_config(name, value, true)`), whatever the client, the role or the database set. The agent's own SQL runs in it too: a `timestamptz` literal without an offset is read as UTC, `now()::date` or a `::text` cast prints in UTC, a `money` value prints and is read in the C locale's form (`$1,234.50`), and a `bytea` value prints in hex (`\x00ff`). The guard's row trigger sets the same six values for itself ([version 34](#version-34-the-row-trigger-stores-row-images-in-fixed-settings), [version 35](#version-35-bytea_output-and-the-revert-functions)), so a write that skips the engine (the agent's credential used directly, as in psql) stores the same row images as one through it, and its own SQL keeps its session's settings. The revert's own read of the row now and its restore set them too, so a person calling the revert functions by hand from any session finds no false conflict. For every write, a stored `timestamptz` reads `+00:00`, an `interval` `01:30:00` and a `float8` its shortest exact form (`0.30000000000000004`) and a `bytea` `\x00ff`, the lines and `rowsHash` are the same from any client, and a revert's conflict check (the row now against the row the agent left, compared as JSON) finds no false conflict when the write and the revert ran in different sessions. A row value stored before this by a session in another zone (`+07:00`) no longer matches the row now: its line is skipped `CHANGED_AFTER`, never written (lane decision, 14 Nov 2026; owner decision on the returned values, 13 Nov 2026).
- Named Go types for anything (de)serialized; DTOs separate from domain structs.

## The change record

One record per action an agent asks for, whether applied, held or refused. Shape:

```json
{
  "changeId": "0193a4c2-…",
  "at": "2026-11-10T06:02:11Z",
  "actor": { "kind": "AGENT", "name": "tracker-bot", "runId": "…", "taskId": "…" },
  "intent": "Merge duplicate case reports from yesterday",
  "target": { "table": "public.cases", "key": { "id": 4211 } },
  "op": "UPDATE",
  "statement": "UPDATE cases SET status = $1 WHERE id = $2",
  "before": { "status": "open" },
  "after": { "status": "merged" },
  "basedOn": { "version": "xmin:88123" },
  "durationMs": 12,
  "rows": 1,
  "policy": { "decision": "ALLOW", "rule": "default.single-row-update" },
  "status": "APPLIED"
}
```

| Field | Values |
|---|---|
| `status` | `APPLIED`, `APPROVAL_PENDING`, `REFUSED`, `APPROVAL_EXPIRED`, `REVERTED` |
| `op` | `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `OTHER` (a refused statement that is none of these), `REVERT` (M3: a person's revert) |
| `actor.kind` | `AGENT`, `PERSON` |
| `policy.decision` | `ALLOW`, `HOLD`, `REFUSE` |

As served by `run_history` and `basalt log --json`, a record also carries `revertId` (on a revert's own record) and `revertedBy` (the revert changes that restored rows of it), `params`, `cascadedRows` and `rowChanges` (one entry per row written, cascades included, with `key`, `before`, `after`, and `redacted` or `pruned` when values are hidden). On `run_history`, a value or image too large for the agent is left out and named in `largeValues` ([Large values](#large-values)); `basalt log` prints it in full. `before`, `after` and `policy` at the top level arrive with M2.

Reads (M1.5): every `query` the engine runs is a change record with `op: SELECT`, `status: APPLIED`, `rows` = rows returned, its statement, parameters and duration, and no `rowChanges`; it carries the run's `intent` when it ran in a run. The rows it saw are kept by version in `basalt_meta` (see `reads` and the `READ` ledger row).

`basedOn` (M1.5): a `rowChanges` entry for an `UPDATE` or `DELETE` of a row the run had read carries `basedOn: { "version": "xmin:<n>", "changeId": "<the read>" }`; one the run never read carries `blindWrite: true` (allowed, owner decision #15). Cascaded rows and inserts carry neither. A change with exactly one non-cascaded row that was read also carries that `basedOn` at the top level, as in the shape above. A `REFUSED` record carries `reason: { code, message }` with an error code from the table below. `before` values are kept 30 days; other ledger metadata 1 year.

## Stores

A store is the system an agent's actions land in. The engine records every action in a ledger kept next to the store, and checks a few things itself before it calls the store. Each agent is bound to exactly one store, and tool names carry no store prefix.

| Kind | Status |
|---|---|
| `POSTGRES` | Built. This is the only kind the engine serves. |
| `MYSQL`, `S3`, `DRIVE` | Designed, not served. An `engine.json` that names one is refused. |

**Binding.** `engine.json` binds an agent to its store with `agents[].store`:

```json
{ "agents": [ { "name": "tracker-bot", "osUser": "basalt-tracker-bot", "store": { "kind": "POSTGRES", "credentialFile": "/etc/basalt/agents/tracker-bot.url" } } ] }
```

`store.credentialFile` is the file that holds the agent's credential for that store (for `POSTGRES`, the connection string of its own `<prefix>_agent_<name>` role, as described under "One engine, many agents" in [CLI](#cli)). It replaces the `credentialFile` that used to sit directly on the agent; the old place is not read, and an `engine.json` that still has it is refused, whichever command loads it, with the edit that fixes it: `agent "<name>": "credentialFile" now goes inside "store"; replace "credentialFile": "<path>" with "store": {"kind": "POSTGRES", "credentialFile": "<path>"}`. An unknown `kind` is a config error that names the agent.

**Guarantee levels.** A store is not always able to refuse what the engine would refuse, so each property has a level that says what holds when the engine is wrong or bypassed (for example, an attacker holds the engine's credential):

| Level | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `STORE` | The store itself refuses an action that breaks the rule, whatever path it takes. |
| `STORE_HISTORY` | The store does not refuse it, but keeps history the agent's credential cannot destroy, so a bypass is caught afterwards and can be reverted. |
| `ENGINE` | Only the engine enforces it. A bypass is neither refused nor necessarily seen. |

For `POSTGRES`: requiring an intent, recording changes with their old and new rows, row limits (cascades counted), approval and revert are `STORE` (the guard triggers and the signature checks in `basalt_meta`). Recording reads is `ENGINE`, and in same-user mode reads are not recorded at all. Parsing and classifying the statement, the function allowlist and the repeatable-read check are done by the engine first.

**Capabilities.** What the engine may rely on, per store. `POSTGRES` has these values:

| Capability | `POSTGRES` |
|---|---|
| `InStoreEnforcement`: the store refuses an unguarded agent write | yes |
| `InStoreLedger`: the ledger is written in the action's own transaction | yes |
| `InStoreSignatureCheck`: the store verifies an approval's signature | yes |
| `TransactionalPreview`: a trial run that rolls back | yes |
| `ConditionalWrites`: compare-and-set on the version | yes (`xmin`, checked in the trigger) |
| `TargetConflicts`: the unit a person's change wins over an agent's | row |
| `Cascades`: a write reaches targets the action did not name | yes |
| `RetainsHistory`: the store itself keeps before values | no (the ledger does) |

**Versions.** A target's version is a string the store defines. `POSTGRES` uses `xmin:<n>`, as `basedOn.version` in the [change record](#the-change-record) already shows. The designed kinds would use `hash:<sha256>` (`MYSQL`), `s3:<versionId>` and `drive:<revisionId>`.

**The ledger.** The ledger lives with the store it records: for `POSTGRES`, in `basalt_meta` in the same database. An agent can never write the ledger's location. For a `POSTGRES` agent that is the grants: no direct access to `basalt_meta`, only the functions listed under [`basalt_meta`](#basalt_meta). A store whose credential could write its ledger location would have to say so in its capabilities and rely on the hash chain and the head the engine pins.

**Tools.** `query` and `write` are SQL-store tools: they are served only for a store that speaks SQL (`POSTGRES` today), and the dialect is the store's. The other tools keep their schemas whatever the store. Because an agent has one store, no tool takes a `store` argument; an agent bound to more than one would need one, and that is a later contract change.

## Error codes

Tool errors return an MCP tool result with `isError: true` and a JSON body `{ "code": "<CODE>", "message": "…", "reason": "…", "sqlState": "…" }`. `reason` is set for `STATEMENT_REFUSED` and `QUERY_TIMEOUT`; `sqlState` is set when Postgres raised the error. Codes are stable once `IMPLEMENTED`.

Every tool error body also carries `docsUrl`, the link to the code's section on the public docs: `https://docs.basaltvault.app/reference/errors/#<anchor>`, where the anchor is the code in lowercase with underscores as hyphens (`APPROVAL_REQUIRED` is `#approval-required`). The server adds it when it writes the error, from the code alone, including the errors `basalt mcp` answers itself while the engine is down. The docs site's errors page has one anchor for every code in this table (and `UNKNOWN_TOKEN`, planned); the contract test checks each code's anchor against the page's anchors, kept in [`api/mcp/errors/docs-anchors.json`](api/mcp/errors/docs-anchors.json). Warnings and `status` problems are not error codes and carry no `docsUrl`.

Planned (actionable errors, owner decisions AE-1 to AE-14; not emitted yet): the body also carries `nextSteps` (required, at least one step), `personMessage` (when the person must know or act), `errorId` (on `UNAVAILABLE` and `INTERNAL`), and `reason` for every code with more than one case. Schema: [`api/mcp/errors/tool-error.schema.json`](api/mcp/errors/tool-error.schema.json); rules in [Next steps](#next-steps).

From M2, an `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` body also carries `approval: { "approvalId", "expiresAt", "rows", "headline", "approvalUrl", "approveCommand", "personMessage" }` (see [Approvals](#approvals) and [Handing a held action to the person](#handing-a-held-action-to-the-person)):

- `headline`: the held write in one line, cascade stated (`Delete 1 row in public.leads + 2 cascaded rows (public.lead_notes 2)`), as `basalt approve --show` prints it.
- `approvalUrl`: the **short link** `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>` when the engine serves the [local pages](#local-pages); otherwise the long link `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#1.<request>` (below, [Page links](#page-links)), or, when that is over the link budget, the hash link `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#h=<h>` with `requestFile` beside it ([The request file and pasted blocks](#the-request-file-and-pasted-blocks)). Clients that render links may show it.
- `requestFile`: only with a hash link: the request as a file and as numbered text blocks for the agent to send the person ([The request file and pasted blocks](#the-request-file-and-pasted-blocks)).
- `approveCommand`: `basalt approve --agent NAME [--config PATH] --open <approvalId>` (`--config` only when the engine runs from a non-default config; planned, AE-3: then with the absolute path). The person runs it in a terminal and it opens the approval page in their browser. A first-class path, not only a fallback.
- `personMessage`: the message the agent shows the person verbatim: what is held, the headline, the link, the equivalent command (with a long `--config` path, a pointer to `approveCommand` in its place, see [Showing links](#showing-links)) and the expiry, with the link on its own line ([Showing links](#showing-links), which has an example). Planned (M3.5): `approval.links` and the body's `links` carry the link with a label, as there.

`headline` and `personMessage` are new (M4.1); `approvalUrl` changes from always-long to short-when-served (M4.1, a breaking change). `list_pending` items carry the same four fields, so an agent that lost the link gets it again there.

| Code | When |
|---|---|
| `INTENT_REQUIRED` | A write without an intent (guard SQLSTATE `BS001`) |
| `STALE_READ` | An `UPDATE` or `DELETE` of a row whose version changed since the run last read or wrote it (`BS004`). A row the run never read is a blind write, not refused |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | DDL, multiple statements, data-changing `WITH`, `COPY`, or a function outside the allowlist (engine policy, or `BS007` from the guard) |
| `ROW_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` | More than 50 rows in one action, cascades included (`BS003`) |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | A `SELECT` hit the row or time limit (time limit: SQLSTATE `57014`), or the result went over the 16 MiB budget (size limit: the reads of the whole call), or one row of a `query` or `RETURNING` is over the budget alone (reason `ROW_TOO_LARGE`), or a revert's request is too large for any link the engine has (`request_revert` reason `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE`); also `request_revert` when preparing the revert hit its 100-second limit (reason `TIME_LIMIT`; the message gives the person's `basalt revert ... --open` command) |
| `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | The action is held; the result carries the approval id and expiry (`BS002`), or reason `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` when no link the engine has can carry the request ([Large values](#large-values)) |
| `APPROVAL_EXPIRED` | The approval was not given within 24 hours (`BS005`) |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | The approval does not exist, is used, belongs to another agent, or no longer matches the rows (`BS005`). HR-1: also a reveal Postgres refused (reason `REVEAL_REFUSED`, one reason for every proof, key, nonce or ticket failure) or whose hidden rows changed (reason `ROWS_CHANGED`), on the pages API only ([Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent)) |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | M3. The revert authorisation does not exist, is already applied, carries a bad signature, or its rows changed since the preview (`BS005`). For a batch of a large plan: the plan is not this agent's, not signed or already finished, or a line is not in the plan, repeated, or already applied. Revert scope (`basalt_meta` version 20, rescope and reset in version 21, reject in version 23): also a revert the person rejected (`REJECTED`), a rescope or reset after the signature, a binding whose `select` is not the revert's stored scope, and a widen Postgres refused (detail `WIDEN_REFUSED`, one detail for every proof or token failure). HR-1: a reveal refused (reason `REVEAL_REFUSED`) or whose hidden rows changed (reason `ROWS_CHANGED`), on the pages API only ([Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent)) |
| `REVERT_EXPIRED` | M3. The revert was not signed within 1 hour of `prepare_revert`, or a signed large plan was not finished within 24 hours of the signature (`BS005`) |
| `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` | M3. Every row in the scope would be skipped, or the scope wrote no rows (`BS009`). Served (AE-8): also `preview_revert` and `request_revert`, in place of `NOT_FOUND`, with reason `READS_ONLY` (the scope wrote no row a revert could restore: only reads, or rows it both inserted and deleted), `ALL_CHANGED_BY_PEOPLE` (every row would be skipped, not all as `ALREADY_REVERTED`) or `ALREADY_REVERTED` (every row was last written by a revert of the scope; the next steps name that revert's `changeId` and when it ran) |
| `PERMISSION_DENIED` | The agent's role lacks the privilege; the database refused (`42501`) |
| `NOT_FOUND` | The run named in the arguments does not exist for this agent; for `preview_revert` and `request_revert`, also a change or run that is not this agent's (before any preview or prepare) and a task the agent never ran in (reason `TASK`, RS-6); also an approval or revert that is not this agent's, and a page link without its secret or replaced (reasons in [Next steps per code](#next-steps-per-code)). For `describe` (M4), a resource that does not exist and one the agent cannot read: the same answer for both, so the agent cannot probe for names. Unknown ids and other agents' ids only: an own scope with nothing to undo is `NOTHING_TO_REVERT`. Planned (revert scope, RS-2): a revert link the agent reset is `NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK_RESET` (pages only) |
| `SQL_ERROR` | Any other error Postgres raised for the agent's statement (syntax, unknown column, constraint); `sqlState` says which |
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | Arguments fail schema validation, or an unknown argument was passed. Served (revert scope, RS-1): a revert scope filter outside the agent's own work (reason `SCOPE_FILTER`) or with more than 20 entries (reason `SCOPE_FILTER_CAP`). Served (admin credential): a string in the arguments, a key included, holds a `postgres://` or `postgresql://` URL with a password (reason `ADMIN_CREDENTIAL`, [Agents and the admin credential](#agents-and-the-admin-credential)) |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | The database or the guard cannot be reached; planned (chat-only operator): also the agent's [setup from the laptop](#setup-from-your-laptop) has not run yet (reasons `SETUP_PENDING`, `SETUP_EXPIRED`) |
| `INTERNAL` | A defect in the engine or the guard (`BS006`, `BS008`) |
| `PLUGIN_REFUSED` | Planned ([Plugins](#plugins)). Never returned by an MCP tool: a `basalt plugin` command refuses with it (exit `1`, and this error body with `--json`) and changes nothing. Reasons `AGENT_OPERATED` (an agent marker is set or the engine is `operatedBy: AGENT`, and the command is `add`, `enable` or `trust`, other than an `add` that only removes agents from an existing entry: [Consent](#consent)), `BAD_MANIFEST` (the manifest breaks a rule of [Manifest](#manifest); the message names the rule), `NOT_TRUSTED` (no trusted key signed the manifest, or a file's hash or permissions failed) and `UNKNOWN_AGENT` (an agent in `--agents` is not one of the engine's agents) |

`STATEMENT_REFUSED` reasons: `PARSE_ERROR`, `MULTIPLE_STATEMENTS`, `WRONG_TOOL` (a write sent to `query` or a read sent to `write`), `DDL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `STATEMENT_NOT_ALLOWED` (`SET`, `DO`, `COPY`, `EXPLAIN`, transaction control and other utility statements), `SELECT_INTO`, `LOCKING_READ`, `MERGE_NOT_ALLOWED`, `DATA_MODIFYING_WITH`, `FUNCTION_NOT_ALLOWED`, `HOLD_TOO_LARGE` (a write that would be held has preview lines over what a hold keeps, [Large values](#large-values) rule 7), and from M2 `NOT_REPEATABLE` (a write that would be held contains a value that changes between transactions: a function such as `now()` or `random()`, `DEFAULT`, or a string that is a date/time word, `now`, `today`, `tomorrow` or `yesterday` in any case, alone or with a time of day, a day name or a zone (`tomorrow 10:00`, `today 09:00 Europe/Berlin`; a zone is an offset, one of Postgres's default zone abbreviations or an `Area/City` name), as a literal anywhere in the statement or as a parameter; pass a fixed value, a timestamp, as a parameter. A string that holds such a word among other words (`call back tomorrow`) is plain text and is not refused, but a string that is only a time word cannot be held, whatever the column's type: the check reads the statement, not the column). `QUERY_TIMEOUT` reasons: `TIME_LIMIT`, `ROW_LIMIT`, `SIZE_LIMIT`: the result costs more than 16 MiB (16 777 216 bytes) as the engine counts it, summed over its rows: each value's bytes in text form, what JSON adds to escape them, a fixed cost per value (NULLs included) and each column's name per row. A single row over 16 MiB on the wire is refused before it is read. The engine stops reading at either point, so one large value, or many small or NULL ones, cannot grow its memory without bound. **One budget per call.** Every read the engine makes on the agent's connection for one tool call is charged against the same 16 MiB, with the same accounting: the agent's own statement in `query` and a write's `RETURNING`, the held write's preview, and the reads behind `run_history`, `list_pending`, `preview_revert`, `revert_plan`, `request_revert` and `describe`. Reads the person's CLI makes are not budgeted. The ledger tools never fail on one large item: they replace large values with a marker ([Large values](#large-values)), so their pages always fit at `limit` 1 and a smaller `limit` always ends a `SIZE_LIMIT`. The reads of `list_runs`, `run_start`, `apply_revert_batch` and `status` are charged too but are bounded far below the budget (short fields only), so those tools do not declare `QUERY_TIMEOUT`; `run_history`, `list_pending` and `revert_plan` declare it. Reason `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` (`request_revert`; and `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` for a held write): the request is over what a page can decode and the engine has no short link that can carry it ([Large values](#large-values)). Reason `ROW_TOO_LARGE` (`query` and a write's `RETURNING`): one row alone costs more than the budget. `query` never shortens a value, so it refuses the row: the message names the row's size and its largest columns with their sizes (when the row was over 16 MiB on the wire and could not be read at all, the row's size and the result's columns, largest unknown), and the agent selects fewer columns, a size (`length(col)`, `octet_length(col)`) or a slice (`substr(col, 1, 100000)`). The agent never holds a shortened value it could write back. `SIZE_LIMIT` stays the reason when no single row is over the budget but the rows together are. Planned (AE-5): `STATEMENT_REFUSED` gains `NO_PRIMARY_KEY` and `NOT_ALLOWED` (the guard's `BS007`, which carries no reason today), and the other codes with more than one case get reasons; [Next steps per code](#next-steps-per-code) lists them all.

New codes and reasons go through the contract-first process. Each one needs its entry in [Next steps per code](#next-steps-per-code) and [`api/mcp/errors/next-steps.json`](api/mcp/errors/next-steps.json).

## Next steps

Planned (owner decisions AE-1 to AE-14). Every error body and every result the agent must act on tells it what to do next and what to tell the person. No bare error strings to agents.

- **`nextSteps`**: an ordered array of steps, first step first ([`next-step.schema.json`](api/mcp/errors/next-step.schema.json)). Each step has `actor` (`AGENT` or `PERSON`), `action`, and `text` (one plain sentence, at most 200 characters), plus what the action needs: `tool` and `arguments` (filled in by the engine: complete for `CALL_TOOL`, where `repeatCall: true` merges them over the arguments of the call that returned the step and `null` removes one; the ids it knows for `REVISE_CALL`), `command`, `url`, `maxRows`, `waitSeconds`.
- **Actions.** `AGENT`: `CALL_TOOL` (call `tool` with `arguments`), `REVISE_CALL` (change the statement or arguments as `text` says, then call `tool`), `RETRY_SMALLER_BATCHES` (split into writes of at most `maxRows` rows, same intent and run), `ASK_PERSON`, `END_TURN` (end the turn; the person acts next), `STOP` (do not retry, do not work around). `PERSON`: `OPEN_LINK` (`url`, optionally `command` that opens the same page), `RUN_COMMAND` (`command`), `TELL_AGENT`. No step is conditional: each result carries its own steps. `AGENT` `SEND_REQUEST` (send the person the request file, or its text blocks, of the `requestFile` beside the link; [The request file and pasted blocks](#the-request-file-and-pasted-blocks)) is not in the catalog's sequences: the engine puts it right before the first step of an entry exactly when the result, or the `list_pending` item, carries `requestFile`, and the catalog checks run on the steps without it. Chat-only operator (CO-1): `AGENT` also `RUN_COMMAND` (`command`), only when the engine's `operatedBy` is `AGENT`.
- **Who runs machine steps** (chat-only operator). A machine step runs a command on the engine's machine: start or restart the engine, install or upgrade `basalt`, `basalt init --remote`. When `operatedBy` is `PERSON` it is a `PERSON` step, as above; when it is `AGENT` it is an `AGENT` `RUN_COMMAND`, with `command` as complete as a person's and `text` written to the agent. A step that needs the admin connection string is always a `PERSON` step, whatever `operatedBy` says: on an agent-operated engine it runs on the person's own computer (`text` says so) and is never addressed to the agent (CO-9). The catalog writes this two ways: a machine step whose entry reads the same in both modes has actor `OPERATOR`, a catalog-only value the engine resolves to `AGENT` or `PERSON` from `operatedBy` (the shim, when it cannot read the config, resolves it to `PERSON`); an entry whose steps differ on an agent-operated engine has a variant in `agentOperated` ([Next steps on an agent-operated engine](#next-steps-on-an-agent-operated-engine)) that replaces its steps there. On the wire the actor is always `AGENT` or `PERSON`.
- **Setup code steps.** An `AGENT` `RUN_COMMAND` of `basalt init --remote ... --json` makes a [setup code](#the-setup-code). Its output carries its own `personMessage` and `nextSteps` (the person runs `basalt init --setup-code <code>` on their own computer, then tells the agent; [`remote-init-result.schema.json`](api/mcp/setup/remote-init-result.schema.json)): the agent shows them to the person and follows them in place of the rest of the entry's steps. An agent runs a machine step at most once for one problem; when `status` reports the same problem right after, it asks the person instead (`ASK_PERSON`), never loops.
- **`personMessage`**: plain text, at most 500 characters not counting its URL lines, that the agent shows the person verbatim. Set whenever the person must know or act. Each URL in it stands on a line of its own, and `links` lists them with labels ([Showing links](#showing-links); until M3.5 the engine writes one line). The agent also shows each `PERSON` step's `command` and `url` unchanged.
- **Commands** are complete and copy-pasteable: every `basalt` command names `--agent <name>` and its ids, and `--config <absolute path>` unless the config is the default the CLI finds by itself (`/etc/basalt/engine.json` on Linux, `/Library/Application Support/Basalt/engine.json` on macOS). No placeholders. Never a secret (connection string, password, token); a command that needs the admin connection string asks for it itself. A short link's `#s=` secret appears only in `url` and `personMessage`. "Run `basalt init` again" steps use the flags init saved in `engine.json` ([CLI](#cli)), plus the one change the step is for.
- **Steps never suggest getting around a limit**: no split below the hold threshold to avoid an approval, no other path around a refusal, no retry loop on a refusal. Over 50 rows the agent stops and asks; only on the person's explicit yes does it split into held batches of up to 50 rows, each needing the person's approval.
- **Results.** `preview_revert`, `request_revert`, `revert_plan`, `apply_revert_batch` and each `list_pending` item carry `nextSteps` and `personMessage` when there is something to do. `query` and `write` carry `warnings: [{ code, message, table, personMessage, nextSteps }]` ([`warning.schema.json`](api/mcp/errors/warning.schema.json)), absent when empty, with `RLS_HIDES_ROWS` (row-level security hides every row of a table in the statement from the agent role and the statement returned or changed 0 rows); `run_start` carries `GUARD_UPGRADE_AVAILABLE`.
- **`status`.** The MCP tool `status` (below) reports what is ready and lists `problems`, each with `code`, `severity` (`ERROR` or `WARNING`), `personMessage` and `nextSteps`. The agent calls it at the start of a session, after the person says they fixed something, and after a failure that looks like setup. When `operatedBy` is `PERSON` the agent never starts, stops, restarts or upgrades anything itself.
- **Pages.** The local pages' JSON errors carry `personMessage` and the entry's `PERSON` steps.
- **Startup failures.** `basalt mcp` that cannot reach the engine serves `status` and the tool list from its embedded contracts and answers every other call `UNAVAILABLE` reason `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE` (it holds no credential and reaches no database). An engine whose guard misses a **breaking** migration serves the agent degraded: `status` works, every other tool answers `UNAVAILABLE` reason `GUARD_OUTDATED` with the `basalt upgrade` steps; it checks again on each call and serves normally once upgraded, with no restart. Missing only **non-breaking** migrations: served normally, with `GUARD_UPGRADE_AVAILABLE` (see [`basalt_meta`](#basalt_meta)). A connection to an agent's socket from any OS user but that agent's `osUser` runs nothing: the engine writes one JSON-RPC error line with a `null` id and code `-32001`, whose message names the agent and says only its `osUser` may use the socket and how to change it, reads and drops what the peer sent for up to 2 seconds (at most 8 such connections per agent at a time; past that, and once the engine stops, it closes right after the line), and closes. An accept that fails for lack of file descriptors or memory is retried with a backoff (5 ms doubling to 1 s); any other accept error ends that agent's socket. `basalt mcp` does not pass that line on: it answers each request it had sent on that connection, or tried to send after the engine had already refused and closed it, with a JSON-RPC error (`-32000`) carrying the message, and exits with it when that connection is its last one (a later connection the engine admits decides instead).
- **Engine restarts (upgrades).** `basalt mcp` is the thin, long-lived side: it holds no state and no credential, so it needs no restart when `basalt` is upgraded; only the engine restarts. When an engine that was serving this `basalt mcp` goes away, the next message waits up to 15 seconds for the engine's socket to answer again before it is answered as in the paragraph above; after one wait that fails, later messages do not wait until the engine has answered once more. A request the engine had already taken and drops unanswered is sent once more to the engine that comes back only when it is a call of a read-only tool (`query`, `describe`, `list_pending`, `list_runs`, `run_history`, `status`). Every other request (`write`, `run_start`, the revert tools, `initialize`) is answered as dropped (`UNAVAILABLE` `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE`, "may or may not have run") and never sent twice, so no write, approval or revert runs twice or is lost silently across a restart. A retried `query` is recorded as a second read. A request the engine answers with JSON-RPC error `-32003` (below: it did not run it because it is restarting) is different: nothing ran, so `basalt mcp` sends it once more, whatever the tool, to the engine that comes back, after the same wait, and sends nothing more on that connection; a second `-32003` for the same request, or no engine within the wait, is answered as usual (the `-32003` error is passed to the client as it is; with no engine, `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE` as for a request the engine never received).
- **Restart onto an upgraded binary.** `basalt engine`, with or without a service manager and with or without `--user`, restarts itself when a new `basalt` binary is installed where it was started from, so an upgrade needs no restart by hand:
  - **What it watches.** The path it was started from: `argv[0]`, looked up on `PATH` when it has no slash, so a package manager's symlink that now points at a new version is followed; when that path leads to another file than the running binary (`argv[0]` can be anything), the running binary's own path. It looks every 5 seconds. A different file there (another device and inode, size, modification time or mode: a rename over it, a copy over it and a new symlink target all count) is used once it was the same on two looks in a row, so a file still being copied is never run. A path the engine cannot read at start turns the restart off, with a warning in the log.
  - **Checks on the new binary.** When the engine runs as root, or the binary it runs (its own executable, whatever path started it) passed the root-only check of `basalt service install` ([CLI](#cli)), the new one must pass that check too, again right before it is run: a restart never runs a binary less protected than the first. In that case a watched path that fails the check itself (a link in a directory others can change) is not used: the engine watches its own executable's path instead. Then `<path> version`, run with `BASALT_UPDATE_CHECK=off` for at most 10 seconds, must exit `0` and print `basalt <version>`. A binary that fails a check is logged once (`a new basalt binary is installed but the engine does not restart onto it`, with the reason) and skipped until it changes again.
  - **Between calls.** The engine logs `a new basalt binary is installed` with its version, then waits up to 2 minutes for a moment when nothing runs: no agent call on any of its sockets, no person socket request and no page-session request through the Vault or the direct listener (a call waits at most 110 seconds for a person, so only requests that keep overlapping hold it off). From that moment it admits none of them. When no such moment comes, it admits none after the 2 minutes and lets those still running finish for up to 2 more minutes; past that it cancels them: their transactions roll back, and `basalt mcp` answers them as dropped (above), so a cancelled write, approval or revert is never sent again. A person socket request it does not admit is answered `REFUSED` (`the basalt engine is restarting onto the upgraded basalt binary and did not run this request; nothing was run: run the command again in a few seconds`), and a page-session request `503` `UNAVAILABLE` with the same meaning; neither runs anything. A stop (`SIGTERM`, `SIGINT`) during the wait or the drain is a stop: the engine exits as on any stop and does not run the new binary.
  - **Stopping.** It closes every agent socket at once, so a `basalt mcp` that dials waits for the new engine. Each open connection is read for 250 milliseconds more, counted from when the engine stopped admitting calls or from the connection's last answer, whichever is later: every request read then is answered with JSON-RPC error `-32003` and nothing is run for it (message: the engine is restarting onto the upgraded binary and did not run this call; nothing was run; call again in a few seconds). The hello and notifications are not refused. Then it closes the connection, the pages, the person socket and the Vault connection as on a stop, and releases its lock.
  - **Running the new binary.** It replaces itself with the new binary at that path (`execve`), with the same arguments and environment: the same process id, OS user, groups, umask and, on Linux, `no_new_privs`, which a process cannot undo. A service manager sees no restart. `--user NAME` finds the process already running as `NAME` and still runs its binary check and its verification (`basalt engine: already running as NAME ...`), so the privilege drop and the owner of every file it makes are the same after the restart. The new engine reads the config again and serves; a `basalt mcp` waiting for it sends its next message there, with no client restart. When the new binary cannot be run (`execve` fails), this binary serves on with a new start of the engine, and restarts only when the binary changes again. A new binary that runs but then stops (a config it refuses) leaves no engine running: a service manager starts it again, and without one the person does.
- **Version negotiation.** The shim and the engine agree on an integer protocol version for the link between them. It is raised only when a change would make an older shim or an older engine misbehave; this build speaks version 1, serves every shim from version 0 and works with every engine from version 0, so nothing is refused today. A peer from before the negotiation is version 0. On every connection the shim's first line is the request `basalt/hello` (id `basalt-hello`) with params `{ "protocol", "build" }`; the engine answers `{ "protocol", "build", "minShimProtocol" }`, and the shim passes neither to the client. The shim holds the client's first request until the answer arrives (one round trip per connection; an engine that stays silent for 3 seconds is treated as if it had answered), so no request reaches an engine the shim refuses. A shim that sends no hello counts as version 0, and an engine that answers the hello with method not found (`-32601`) is version 0.
  - **Shim too old for the engine.** When the shim's version is below the engine's `minShimProtocol`, the engine runs no request of that connection: it answers the hello and every request after it with a JSON-RPC error, code `-32002`, whose message names both builds and the step: reconnect the MCP server so the client starts the upgraded `basalt mcp` (in Claude Code run `/mcp` and reconnect `basalt`, or restart the agent client), then call again; nothing was run for the call. The shim passes the error on as it is, and does not judge the engine's version from it. A shim that never says hello gets the same errors.
  - **Engine too old for the shim.** When the engine's version is below the oldest the shim works with, the shim closes the connection without sending the client's request and answers every request with a JSON-RPC error (`-32000`) saying the engine was not restarted after the upgrade and asking the person to restart the engine (or its service), then call again; nothing was run for the call. It checks again each time it connects, so the next call after the restart works with no client restart.
- **The catalog.** [`api/mcp/errors/next-steps.json`](api/mcp/errors/next-steps.json) fixes, per code and reason (and per tool where a tool differs), per result case, per warning and per `status` problem, the `(actor, action)` sequence the engine returns and whether `personMessage` is set. The tables below are the same list. The contract test checks both agree and that every code in [Error codes](#error-codes), every warning and every `status` problem has an entry; the engine's test checks every returned `nextSteps` against it. Entries marked `planned` (rows whose Steps start with `Planned (`) are left out of the engine's catalog until they land ([Planned parts of a served tool](#process-contract-first)).

### Next steps per code

`—` in Reason: the code's only or default case (no `reason`). Steps: `A` agent, `P` person, `O` operator (a machine step, resolved by `operatedBy`); `+pm`: `personMessage` set. These are the steps of a person-operated engine; [the next table](#next-steps-on-an-agent-operated-engine) lists the entries an agent-operated engine replaces.

| Code | Reason | Tool | Steps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `INTENT_REQUIRED` | — | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STALE_READ` | — | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`query` of the row), A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `PARSE_ERROR` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `MULTIPLE_STATEMENTS` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `WRONG_TOOL` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `DDL_NOT_ALLOWED` | — | A `STOP`, A `ASK_PERSON`; +pm |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `STATEMENT_NOT_ALLOWED` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `SELECT_INTO` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `LOCKING_READ` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `MERGE_NOT_ALLOWED` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `DATA_MODIFYING_WITH` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `FUNCTION_NOT_ALLOWED` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `NOT_REPEATABLE` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `NO_PRIMARY_KEY` | — | A `STOP`; +pm |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `NOT_ALLOWED` | — | A `STOP` |
| `STATEMENT_REFUSED` | `HOLD_TOO_LARGE` | — | A `STOP`, A `ASK_PERSON` (the person makes the change, or the agent writes fewer rows per write, each held; on a guard before version 37, the person upgrades the guard and the agent sends the write again); +pm |
| `ROW_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` | — | — | A `STOP`, A `ASK_PERSON`, A `RETRY_SMALLER_BATCHES` (`maxRows` 50, only on an explicit yes); +pm |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | `ROW_LIMIT` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | `SIZE_LIMIT` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` (select fewer or shorter columns, for example `left(col, 200)`, or fewer rows; for `run_history`, `list_pending`, `preview_revert`, `revert_plan` and `describe`, a smaller `limit`, down to 1) |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` | `request_revert` | ([Large values](#large-values)): A `STOP`, A `ASK_PERSON` (the setup change by setup, or a narrower revert with `runs` or `tables`), A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`request_revert` with the `revertId`, and `reissueLink`, once the person says they changed the setup); +pm |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | `ROW_TOO_LARGE` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` (select fewer columns, the size with `length(col)`, or a slice with `substr(col, 1, 100000)`; the message names the large columns and their sizes) |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | `TIME_LIMIT` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | `TIME_LIMIT` | `preview_revert` | A `STOP`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --dry-run`), P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --open`), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `QUERY_TIMEOUT` | `TIME_LIMIT` | `request_revert` | A `STOP`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --open`), P `TELL_AGENT` (the revert id it prints), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `HELD` | — | P `OPEN_LINK`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`write` with `approvalId`, waits once); +pm |
| `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `HELD_NO_PAGES` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt approve ... --open`), A `CALL_TOOL`; +pm |
| `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` | — | ([Large values](#large-values)): A `ASK_PERSON` (the setup change by setup: local pages, or the Vault for the portal; the held write waits until it expires), A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`list_pending` with `kind` `APPROVAL`, `status` `PENDING`, the hold's `runId` and `reissueLink`, once the person says they changed the setup); +pm |
| `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `NO_PASSKEY` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ... --add-passkey`), P `OPEN_LINK`, A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `WAIT_ENDED` | — | P `OPEN_LINK` (the short link the agent gave earlier, no `url`, while the engine serves the pages; else the link the hold handed over, with `requestFile` beside a hash link), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`write` with `approvalId`, no wait); +pm |
| `APPROVAL_EXPIRED` | — | — | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `REVISE_CALL`; +pm |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `NOT_SIGNED` | — | P `OPEN_LINK` (the page again, as for `WAIT_ENDED`: no `url` while the engine serves the pages, the person opens the short link the agent gave earlier; else the link the hold handed over, never a long link over the budget, with no `requestFile` again), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `ALREADY_USED` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`run_history`), A `STOP` |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `DIFFERENT_WRITE` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`write` with the held arguments) |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `NOT_THIS_AGENT` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`list_pending`) |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `ROWS_CHANGED` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` (`query`), A `REVISE_CALL` (`write`, held anew); +pm |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `BAD_SIGNATURE` | — | P `OPEN_LINK` (as for `NOT_SIGNED`), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `REVEAL_REFUSED` | — | HR-1, pages API only. P `OPEN_LINK` (the page's own short link, then Show values with passkey once more); +pm: Your passkey didn't confirm. Nothing was shown; try again. |
| `APPROVAL_INVALID` | `PASSKEY_REVOKED` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt passkey list`), P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ... --add-passkey`), P `OPEN_LINK` (as for `NOT_SIGNED`), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `LINE_INVALID` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`revert_plan`, pending lines) |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `PLAN_NOT_SIGNED` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`revert_plan`), A `END_TURN` |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `PLAN_FINISHED` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`revert_plan`), A `STOP` |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `NOT_THIS_AGENT` | — | A `STOP` |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `ROWS_CHANGED` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` (`request_revert` again, same scope); +pm |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `BAD_SIGNATURE` | — | P `OPEN_LINK` (the revert link: no `url` while the engine serves the pages, the person opens the short link the agent gave earlier; else the link the request handed over, the long link within the budget or the hash link, with no `revertUrl` and no `requestFile` again; no `url` for a widened revert, signed on the page the person widened on), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `REVEAL_REFUSED` | — | HR-1, pages API only. P `OPEN_LINK` (the page's own short link, then Show values with passkey once more); +pm: as for `APPROVAL_INVALID` |
| `REVERT_INVALID` | `PASSKEY_REVOKED` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt passkey list`), P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ... --add-passkey`), P `OPEN_LINK` (as for `BAD_SIGNATURE`), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `REVERT_EXPIRED` | `NOT_SIGNED` | — | A `ASK_PERSON`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --open`); +pm |
| `REVERT_EXPIRED` | `NOT_FINISHED` | — | A `ASK_PERSON`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --open`); +pm |
| `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` | `READS_ONLY` | — | A `STOP`; +pm |
| `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` | `ALL_CHANGED_BY_PEOPLE` | — | A `STOP`; +pm |
| `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` | `ALREADY_REVERTED` | — | A `ASK_PERSON` (undo that revert?), A `REVISE_CALL` (`request_revert` with the revert's `changeId`); +pm |
| `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` | `READS_ONLY` | — | Task scope: A `STOP`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --task ID --open`: choose other agents' changes on the page); +pm |
| `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` | `ALL_CHANGED_BY_PEOPLE` | — | Task scope: A `STOP`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --task ID --open`); +pm |
| `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` | `ALREADY_REVERTED` | — | Task scope: A `ASK_PERSON`, A `REVISE_CALL`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --task ID --open`); +pm |
| `PERMISSION_DENIED` | — | — | A `STOP`, A `ASK_PERSON`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ...` with the table added), P `TELL_AGENT`; +pm |
| `NOT_FOUND` | `RUN` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`list_runs`) |
| `NOT_FOUND` | `CHANGE` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`list_runs`) |
| `NOT_FOUND` | `APPROVAL` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`list_pending`) |
| `NOT_FOUND` | `REVERT` | — | A `ASK_PERSON` (the revert id `basalt revert` printed) |
| `NOT_FOUND` | `LINK` | — | P `TELL_AGENT` (pages only); +pm |
| `NOT_FOUND` | `TASK` | — | RS-6. A `CALL_TOOL` (`list_runs`); +pm: this agent has no run in task X; to revert work in that task the person runs `basalt revert --agent <agent> --task <id> --open` with an agent that ran in it, or asks that agent to request it. The task id is filled in; the agent name is left to the person, because this agent cannot see who ran in the task (N12-R1): a stated exception to AE-3's filled-in commands |
| `NOT_FOUND` | `LINK_RESET` | — | RS-2. P `TELL_AGENT` (pages only: the agent reset this revert to its own work; ask it for the new link, and widen again to include other agents); +pm |
| `NOT_FOUND` | — | `describe` | A `CALL_TOOL` (`describe` with `query`), A `ASK_PERSON`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt access --agent <agent>`; always a `PERSON` step, never resolved through `OPERATOR`: the output names tables the agent cannot read), P `TELL_AGENT`; +pm |
| `SQL_ERROR` | — | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `SQL_ERROR` | `NO_ROWS_NOW` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` (`query`) |
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | — | — | A `REVISE_CALL` |
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | `STALE_CURSOR` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (same call, no `cursor`) |
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | `SCOPE_FILTER` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` (the same call without the refused entries), A `CALL_TOOL` (`run_history` of the run, or `list_runs` for a task, to see its own runs and tables) |
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | `SCOPE_FILTER_CAP` | — | A `REVISE_CALL` (at most 20 entries, or no filter: the person can narrow on the page) |
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | `ADMIN_CREDENTIAL` | — | A `ASK_PERSON` (tell the person a connection string with a password was in the call and was not kept; ask them to change that database password now), A `REVISE_CALL` (the same call without it); +pm: a database connection string with a password was sent through the agent, Basalt refused the call and kept no copy, change that password now |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `DATABASE_UNREACHABLE` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (same call after 10 s), A `ASK_PERSON`; +pm |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `TOO_MANY_CONNECTIONS` | — | SQLSTATE `53300` on a new connection: the agent role is at its `CONNECTION LIMIT` of 8. A `CALL_TOOL` (same call), A `ASK_PERSON`; +pm: "The agent <name> has used all 8 database connections Basalt allows it. Another engine for the same agent may be running, or a stopped engine left sessions open. Stop any second engine; stale sessions close on their own within a few minutes." |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `CREDENTIAL_REJECTED` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (restart the engine), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP`; +pm |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `GUARD_MISSING` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ...`, saved flags), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP`; +pm |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `GUARD_OUTDATED` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt upgrade ...`), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP`; +pm |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (start the engine), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP`; +pm |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `PAGES_OFF` | — | A `CALL_TOOL` (`list_pending`, hand over `approveCommand`) |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `SETUP_PENDING` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init --setup-code <the pending code>`, on their own computer), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`status`); +pm |
| `UNAVAILABLE` | `SETUP_EXPIRED` | — | O `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init --remote --new-code ...`, saved flags), A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `INTERNAL` | — | — | A `STOP`; +pm |
| `PLUGIN_REFUSED` | `AGENT_OPERATED` | — | Planned (plugins). A `STOP`, A `ASK_PERSON` (an agent never adds or widens a plugin; on a person-operated engine the person runs the command in a terminal of their own, and on an agent-operated engine plugins cannot be added yet); +pm |
| `PLUGIN_REFUSED` | `BAD_MANIFEST` | — | Planned (plugins). A `STOP` (the plugin's publisher fixes the manifest; the message names the rule); +pm |
| `PLUGIN_REFUSED` | `NOT_TRUSTED` | — | Planned (plugins). P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt plugin trust <publisher key file>`, only when the person trusts that publisher), P `RUN_COMMAND` (the refused command again); +pm |
| `PLUGIN_REFUSED` | `UNKNOWN_AGENT` | — | Planned (plugins). P `RUN_COMMAND` (the refused command with `--agents` naming only the engine's agents); +pm |

Results and warnings:

| Tool or warning | Case | Steps |
|---|---|---|
| `preview_revert` | `PREVIEW` (a change or run of this agent) | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `REVISE_CALL` (`request_revert` with the scope; the agent adds the person's reason as `intent`); +pm |
| `preview_revert` | `PREVIEW_TASK` (a task this agent ran in, its own lines) | RS-1, RS-7. A `ASK_PERSON`, A `REVISE_CALL` (`request_revert` with `taskId`); +pm, which always adds "You can include other agents' changes in this task on the signing page." whether or not other agents wrote in it |
| `request_revert` | `REQUESTED` (request form) | P `OPEN_LINK`, A `CALL_TOOL` (wait form, once); +pm |
| `request_revert` | `WAIT_ENDED` (`PENDING` after a wait) | P `OPEN_LINK` (the short link the agent gave earlier, no `url`; when the engine serves no pages, the link the request handed over: the long link within the budget, else the hash link, with no `requestFile`: the agent sent the file with the request), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (wait form, no wait), A `CALL_TOOL` (`reissueLink: true`, only if the link is lost); +pm |
| `request_revert` | `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` (`PENDING` after a wait or a reissue, no link can carry it) | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`request_revert` with the `revertId`, and `reissueLink`, once the person says they changed the setup); no `OPEN_LINK`, since no link was handed over; +pm |
| `request_revert` | `APPLYING` | A `CALL_TOOL` (`revert_plan`, `pending: true`, `limit: 200`) |
| `request_revert` | `APPLIED` | A `STOP`; +pm |
| `request_revert` | `EXPIRED` | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `REVISE_CALL` (request form again); +pm |
| `request_revert` | `REJECTED` | A `STOP` (do not request it again unless the person asks); +pm: "The revert was rejected on the signing page. Nothing was reverted.", with the person's note when they wrote one |
| `describe` | `OVERVIEW_LARGE` (more than 50 visible tables) | A `CALL_TOOL` (`describe` with `query`) |
| `describe` | `NOT_GRANTED` (overview with `counts.notGranted` above 0) | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt access --agent <agent>`, always a `PERSON` step), P `TELL_AGENT`; +pm: the count and the command, and no `ASK_PERSON` (platform schemas such as Supabase's `auth` are ungranted on every project, so asking on every overview would nag) |
| `list_pending` item | `PENDING` | P `OPEN_LINK`; +pm |
| `list_pending` item | `REVERT_PENDING` (a revert waiting for the person's signature) | P `OPEN_LINK` (the revert link; no URL for a widened revert, signed on the page the person widened on), A `CALL_TOOL` (`request_revert` with `revertId` and `waitForSignature: 0`, once the person says they signed); +pm |
| `list_pending` item | `REVERT_APPLYING` (a signed large plan) | A `CALL_TOOL` (`revert_plan`, `pending: true`, `limit: 200`); +pm |
| `list_pending` item | `EXPIRED` | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `REVISE_CALL`; +pm |
| `list_pending` item | `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` (a held write or pending revert no link the engine has can carry, [Large values](#large-values)) | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (once the person changed the setup: for a revert item `request_revert` with its `revertId`, and `reissueLink`; for a held write `list_pending` with `kind` `APPROVAL`, `status` `PENDING`, its `runId` and `reissueLink`; filled in per item); +pm |
| `revert_plan` | `PENDING` | P `OPEN_LINK`, P `TELL_AGENT`, A `END_TURN`; +pm |
| `revert_plan` | `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` (`PENDING`, no link can carry it) | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`request_revert` with the `revertId`, and `reissueLink`, once the person says they changed the setup); +pm |
| `revert_plan` | `APPLYING` | A `CALL_TOOL` (`apply_revert_batch` with the pending line numbers) |
| `revert_plan` | `APPLIED` | A `STOP`; +pm |
| `revert_plan` | `EXPIRED` | A `ASK_PERSON`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt revert ... --open`); +pm |
| `revert_plan` | `REJECTED` | A `STOP` (do not request it again unless the person asks); +pm: as `request_revert` `REJECTED` |
| `apply_revert_batch` | `APPLYING` | A `CALL_TOOL` (`revert_plan`, `pending: true`, `limit: 200`) |
| `apply_revert_batch` | `APPLIED` | A `STOP`; +pm |
| `RLS_HIDES_ROWS` | — | A `ASK_PERSON`, P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ... --agent-rls=all-rows`), P `TELL_AGENT`; +pm |
| `GUARD_UPGRADE_AVAILABLE` | — | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt upgrade ...`); +pm |

Status problems (`status` tool, every one +pm):

| Problem | Severity | Steps |
|---|---|---|
| `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE` | `ERROR` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (start the engine), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP` |
| `DATABASE_UNREACHABLE` | `ERROR` | A `ASK_PERSON` (check the database is up) |
| `TOO_MANY_CONNECTIONS` | `ERROR` | A `ASK_PERSON` (stop a second engine for the agent, or wait for stale sessions to close; the error's person message) |
| `CREDENTIAL_REJECTED` | `ERROR` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (restart the engine), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP` |
| `GUARD_MISSING` | `ERROR` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ...`, saved flags), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP` |
| `GUARD_OUTDATED` | `ERROR` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt upgrade ...`), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `STOP` |
| `GUARD_UPGRADE_AVAILABLE` | `WARNING` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt upgrade ...`) |
| `NO_PASSKEY` | `WARNING` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ... --add-passkey`) |
| `SETUP_NOT_RUN` | `ERROR` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init --setup-code <the pending code>`, on their own computer), P `TELL_AGENT`, A `END_TURN`, A `CALL_TOOL` (`status`) |
| `SETUP_EXPIRED` | `ERROR` | O `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init --remote --new-code ...`, saved flags), A `END_TURN` |
| `SETUP_CHANGE_PENDING` | `WARNING` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init --setup-code <the pending code>`, on their own computer), P `TELL_AGENT` |
| `LOCK_FILE_UNUSABLE` | `WARNING` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`sudo chown <engine user> <lock file>`, on the engine's machine), P `TELL_AGENT` |
| `PORTAL_MISMATCH` | `WARNING` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt portal verify`: the engine's daily check found the portal serving pages other than this build embeds; no `--agent` or `--config`, it reads public pages only) |
| `DEV_LOCAL_PAGES` | `WARNING` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init ... --add-passkey`, saved flags without `--dev-local-pages` and `--passkey-origin`, so the portal's pages and a passkey for them) |
| `ENGINE_REVOKED` | `WARNING` | A `ASK_PERSON` (whether they revoked this engine on purpose; until a new key is recorded the agent hands over long links, and a replacement means `basalt engine rotate-key` and a new setup code), A `END_TURN` |
| `DIRECT_NOT_SERVED` | `WARNING` | O `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt init --remote ... --direct-addr 127.0.0.1:8787`, the saved flags `--remote` takes, then restart the engine), so the tunnel has a listener to forward to |
| `LEDGER_REINSTALLED` | `WARNING` | [Version 36](#version-36-reinstalls-on-the-record). A `ASK_PERSON` (show the notice and ask whether someone on their team reinstalled Basalt; the tools keep working, and the notice stays until an admin acknowledges it) |
| `PLUGIN_UNAVAILABLE` | `ERROR` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt plugin check <name>`), P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt plugin disable <name>`) |
| `PLUGIN_NOT_TRUSTED` | `ERROR` | P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt plugin check <name>`: it names the check that failed), P `RUN_COMMAND` (`basalt plugin disable <name>`) |

### Next steps on an agent-operated engine

Chat-only operator. When `operatedBy` is `AGENT`, each entry below replaces the steps of the entry of the same kind, code and reason above (`agentOperated` in [`next-steps.json`](api/mcp/errors/next-steps.json)); every other entry keeps its steps, with `O` resolved to `A`. Each replaces a `PERSON` step that would run a command with the admin connection string, or start the engine, on the agent's machine. `A RUN_COMMAND (init --remote)` is `basalt init --remote ... --json` with the saved flags plus the one change the step is for; it prints a setup code whose steps the agent follows next ([Setup code steps](#next-steps)). The saved flags it carries are the ones `basalt init --remote` takes (`--agent`, `--write`, `--read`, `--prefix`, `--statement-timeout`, `--operated-by`, and `--config` when not the default); `--os-user` and init's other flags are left out. A variant's `personMessage` names no command on the engine's machine: it tells the person the agent gives them a setup code to run on their own computer.

| Entry | Code | Reason | Steps |
|---|---|---|---|
| error | `PERMISSION_DENIED` | — | A `STOP`, A `ASK_PERSON` (whether to add the table), P `TELL_AGENT` (to add it), A `END_TURN`, A `RUN_COMMAND` (when the person told it to: init --remote with the table added, an `AGENT` code); +pm |
| error | `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` | `NO_PASSKEY` | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `AGENT` code with no change; the person's computer registers a portal passkey); +pm |
| error | `UNAVAILABLE` | `CREDENTIAL_REJECTED` | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote --new-code: an `AGENT` code with a new password); +pm |
| error | `UNAVAILABLE` | `GUARD_MISSING` | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `INSTALL` code); +pm |
| error | `UNAVAILABLE` | `GUARD_OUTDATED` | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `UPGRADE` code); +pm |
| error | `UNAVAILABLE` | `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE` | A `RUN_COMMAND` (start the engine), A `CALL_TOOL` (same call, `repeatCall`) |
| warning | `RLS_HIDES_ROWS` | — | A `ASK_PERSON`, A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `AGENT` code; the person answers the row-level security question on their computer); +pm |
| warning | `GUARD_UPGRADE_AVAILABLE` | — | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `UPGRADE` code); +pm |
| problem | `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE` | — | A `RUN_COMMAND` (start the engine), A `CALL_TOOL` (`status`) |
| problem | `CREDENTIAL_REJECTED` | — | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote --new-code: an `AGENT` code with a new password); +pm |
| problem | `GUARD_MISSING` | — | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `INSTALL` code); +pm |
| problem | `GUARD_OUTDATED` | — | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `UPGRADE` code); +pm |
| problem | `GUARD_UPGRADE_AVAILABLE` | — | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `UPGRADE` code); +pm |
| problem | `NO_PASSKEY` | — | A `RUN_COMMAND` (init --remote: an `AGENT` code with no change); +pm |

Not covered here: entries whose `PERSON` step opens a page or reads the ledger on the engine's machine (`basalt approve ... --open`, `basalt revert ... --open`, `basalt passkey list`: `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` `HELD_NO_PAGES`, `APPROVAL_INVALID` and `REVERT_INVALID` `PASSKEY_REVOKED`, `REVERT_EXPIRED`, `QUERY_TIMEOUT` `TIME_LIMIT` for `preview_revert` and `request_revert`, `revert_plan` `EXPIRED`). On an agent-operated engine there is no person at that machine; their variants come with the links fallback contract (chat-only operator design, PR 7, and the vault channel's transports), and until then these entries keep the steps above, which assume a person at the engine's machine.

## MCP tools

The first release serves exactly these tools (plus the two large-revert tools of owner decision N12-1), and the agent-assisted revert tools `list_runs` (owner decisions N12-R1 and N12-R2) and `request_revert` (N12-R3 to N12-R9), both forms.

| Tool | Milestone | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `run_start` | M1 | Start a run with an intent and optional shared `taskId` |
| `query` | M1 | Run one `SELECT`; the engine records the read (M1.5: as a change record, with the rows it saw by version) |
| `write` | M1 | Run one `INSERT`, `UPDATE` or `DELETE` with an intent |
| `run_history` | M1 | The change records of a run |
| `list_pending` | M2 | Held writes waiting for approval, and (LP-1) reverts waiting for the person's signature or for the agent's batches |
| `preview_revert` | M3 | What reverting a change, run or task would restore, and its conflicts |
| `revert_plan` | M3 | Nov 12: the lines of a large revert plan and its progress |
| `apply_revert_batch` | M3 | Nov 12: apply a batch of lines of a large revert plan the person signed |
| `describe` | M4 | The agent's map of the database: overview, search, one table in full |
| `list_runs` | M4.1 | This agent's own runs, newest first, with what each did |
| `request_revert` | M4.1 | Prepare a revert for the person to sign and hand them the link; then wait once for their signature |
| `status` | M4.1 | AE-9. Whether Basalt is ready for this agent, and each problem's next steps; served by the shim when the engine is down |

`status` (AE-9): no arguments. Returns `agent`, `operatedBy` (`PERSON` or `AGENT`), `ready` (no `ERROR` problem), `engine` (`reachable`, `build`), `guard` (`state`: `OK`, `UPGRADE_AVAILABLE`, `OUTDATED`, `NEWER`, `MISSING`, `UNKNOWN`; `installed`, `build`, `missingBreaking`, `missingNonBreaking`), `pages` (`served`, `url`), `passkeys` (`registered`, `active`), `ledger` (`bytes`, `valuesKeptDays`, `recordsKeptDays`; see [Configuration and status](#configuration-and-status)), `engineRecord` (`state`, `url`, `recordedAt`, `revokedAt`; same section) and `problems`, each with `code`, `severity`, `message`, `personMessage` and `nextSteps` ([Next steps](#next-steps)). Served by the engine, and by `basalt mcp` itself when the engine does not answer (then `engine.reachable` is false, `guard.state` `UNKNOWN`, and `operatedBy` comes from the config the shim can read, else `PERSON`). Also served while the guard is outdated. Read-only; never a secret, and unlike `basalt status` it never names the credential file. Errors: `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `INTERNAL`; it never answers `UNAVAILABLE`, problems are its result. With the chat-only operator (design section 9) it also returns `enforcement` and `setup`. `enforcement` is `GUARD_ONLY` when `operatedBy` is `AGENT`, when the engine runs in same-user mode, or when the shim cannot read the config (the agent could use its credential directly; the guard still enforces intent, row limits, holds and signatures), else `ENGINE_AND_GUARD`. `setup` is `{ "state", "setupId", "kind", "expiresAt", "fingerprint" }` (`fingerprint` while pending) with `state` `PENDING` (a [setup code](#the-setup-code) was made and its setup is not recorded yet), `EXPIRED` (the same, past `expiresAt`), `DONE` (no setup pending; `setupId` and `kind` name the agent's latest recorded setup) or `NONE` (no setup pending and none recorded, or the engine is down and none is pending). A pending `INSTALL` code is the problem `SETUP_NOT_RUN` and an expired one `SETUP_EXPIRED`, both `ERROR`; a pending `AGENT` or `UPGRADE` code is `SETUP_CHANGE_PENDING`, a `WARNING` (the tools keep working); zero active passkeys stays `NO_PASSKEY`. While an `INSTALL` code is pending or expired and the database refuses the agent's role or has no guard yet, those two problems are left out: they are what the code fixes, and its setup problem is the one to fix. When the database cannot be reached, `status` cannot tell whether the code was used: it reports `DATABASE_UNREACHABLE` and no setup problem (with `setup` still showing the config's code), and the tools answer `DATABASE_UNREACHABLE`, not `SETUP_PENDING`.

`preview_revert` (M3): exactly one of `changeId`, `runId`, `taskId`, plus `limit` and `cursor` (no `sort`: lines come in their signed order). The scope is the agent's own: its change, its run, or its own lines in a task it ran in (RS-1); anything else is `NOT_FOUND` before any preview (revert scope, below). Returns `scope`, `scopeId`, the `rows` and `skipped` totals of the whole preview, the `command` the person runs (`basalt revert --agent <name> --run <id>`), and one page of lines as objects (see [Revert](#revert)). It runs the revert on trial and rolls it back; it never reverts. The cursor names the last line of the page by its hash, so a preview whose rows changed between pages is refused with `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (start again). An own scope with nothing to revert is `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` with its AE-8 reason ([Error codes](#error-codes)). A scope of any size can be previewed, within the agent role's statement timeout: a preview that runs out is `QUERY_TIMEOUT` `TIME_LIMIT`, and its next steps give the person `basalt revert ... --dry-run` and `--open`, whose prepare may take 10 minutes.

`revert_plan` (large reverts, N12): `revertId`, optional `pending` (only lines not yet applied), `limit` and `cursor` (keyset on the line number; no `sort`: lines come in execution order). Returns the plan's `status` (`PENDING` until the person signs, `APPLYING`, `APPLIED`, `EXPIRED`), `scope`, `scopeId`, totals `lines`, `restored`, `skipped`, `pending`, `expiresAt` (sign by), `applyBy` (after the signature), `maxBatch` (200), and one page of `items`: `{line, outcome, batch, conflict, sqlState, plan}` with `outcome` `PENDING`, `RESTORED` or `SKIPPED` and `plan` the signed revert line as an object. Only the agent that prepared the revert sees it (`NOT_FOUND` otherwise). A revert of up to 1000 lines is applied in one call and has no plan to page (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`). Revert scope (RS-2, RS-5): `status` also takes `REJECTED` (the person rejected the plan on the signing page; nothing was reverted). For a plan the person widened, the result carries `widened: true` and no `scope`, `scopeId`, `revertUrl` or `links`; its `personMessage` starts "You changed the scope on the signing page; the agent cannot see what you added."; a `PENDING` plan's `OPEN_LINK` step carries no URL (the person signs on the page they widened on, and the call issues no link); and an item's `plan` is absent when its line names a change the agent did not make (`agent_revert_lines.line` is `NULL`). Line numbers, `outcome`, `batch`, `conflict` and `sqlState` stay on every item, so the agent applies a signed widened plan's batches by number.

`apply_revert_batch` (large reverts, N12): `revertId` and `lines`, 1 to 200 unique line numbers from `revert_plan`. Postgres applies those lines only if the person signed the plan, the caller prepared it, it has not expired, and every line is in the plan and still pending; otherwise nothing is written (`REVERT_INVALID`, `REVERT_EXPIRED`). Each line is re-checked with its row locked: a row someone changed after the plan was built is skipped (people win), never overwritten. Returns `revertId`, `batch`, `status`, the plan's totals `restored`, `skipped`, `pending`, and this batch's `items: [{line, outcome, conflict, sqlState}]`. The loop: `revert_plan` with `pending: true` and `limit: 200`, then `apply_revert_batch` with those line numbers, until `pending` is 0. Apply lines in number order: it is the order that worked on trial.

`list_runs` (N12-R1, N12-R2): the calling agent's own runs, read from `agent_runs` and `agent_changes` (no other agent's runs, ever). Arguments `limit`, `cursor` (id-only keyset on `runId`, UUIDv7 so time-ordered), `sort` (`id` or `-id`, default `-id`), and optional `startTime` and `endTime` (RFC 3339) filtering on the run's start: `startTime` inclusive, `endTime` exclusive; `endTime` not after `startTime` is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Without them the newest runs come first by `limit` and `cursor`; the agent does not have to ask the person for a period. Each item: `runId`, `taskId`, `intent`, `startedAt`, `lastChangeAt` (absent when the run has no change record), counts `applied` (writes `APPLIED`), `held` (`APPROVAL_PENDING`), `refused`, `reverted` (writes `REVERTED`; a partly reverted write stays `APPLIED`), `reads`, `rowsWritten` (rows of applied and reverted writes, cascades included), and `tables` (the tables it wrote to, at most 10 in name order, `tablesTruncated` when there are more). Counts and tables come from one aggregate per run over `agent_changes`; a revert's own change record (`op` `REVERT`) belongs to no run and is not counted. Paging by `runId` uses the `runs_agent` index (`basalt_meta` version 14). Listing every agent's runs is not part of this tool: it belongs to the hosted Vault, later.

`request_revert` (N12-R3 to N12-R9): the agent hands the person a revert to sign, with no terminal. An agent can never sign a revert; it can only deliver the person's signature. Two forms, told apart by their arguments:

- **Request.** Exactly one of `changeId`, `runId` or `taskId`, plus `intent` (1 to 500 characters: why the person reverts, as the agent heard it; it is in the binding and on the page). The scope must be the agent's own (RS-1, RS-7): a `changeId` or `runId` of another agent is `NOT_FOUND`, and a `taskId` names a task the agent ran in (it has a run with that task id), of which the revert holds only the agent's own lines; a task it never ran in is `NOT_FOUND` reason `TASK` (revert scope, below). An agent that thinks another agent's run is wrong asks that agent, which decides from its own context and requests the revert of its own run. The engine prepares the revert exactly as `basalt revert` does (`basalt_meta.prepare_revert` with the agent credential, with the statement timeout lifted to 100 seconds for that call only, so the MCP call stays in the client's foreground; a prepare that runs out is `QUERY_TIMEOUT` reason `TIME_LIMIT`, and its message gives the person's `basalt revert --agent <name> --<scope> <id> --open` command, whose prepare may take 10 minutes; there is no background prepare), issues the revert's short link ([Link secrets](#link-secrets)), and returns at once with `status` `PENDING`, `revertId`, `scope`, `scopeId`, `intent`, `batched`, the totals `rows` (restored) and `skipped`, `expiresAt`, `revertUrl` and `personMessage`. `waitForSignature`, `reissueLink` and `signature` are refused in this form: the person cannot sign a link before the agent has shown it. `NOTHING_TO_REVERT` when every row would be skipped or the scope wrote none (reasons in [Error codes](#error-codes)). A `changeId` may be a revert's own change of this agent's work: reverting it brings the agent's values back (a redo; [Revert](#revert), owner decision AE-14), with people still winning and the person still signing.
- **Wait.** Only `revertId` (a revert this agent requested; `NOT_FOUND` otherwise), optional `waitForSignature` (0 to 110 seconds, default 90), `reissueLink` and `signature`. The engine reads the revert's state about once a second until it is no longer `PENDING` or the time is up, and returns the same fields: `APPLIED` with `changeId` (a revert of up to 1000 lines is applied when the person's page relays the signature, M3-2); `APPLYING` for a signed large plan, with `changeId`, `applyBy`, `progress` (`restored`, `skipped`, `pending`, `batches`) and `nextSteps` (the `revert_plan` and `apply_revert_batch` loop of [Large plans](#large-plans), arguments filled in; [Next steps](#next-steps)); `PENDING` when the wait ran out, with nothing reverted; `EXPIRED`. Without `signature` the wait changes nothing: it only reads `agent_reverts`. Without `reissueLink` it issues no link and `revertUrl` is absent (the link already given keeps working); with `reissueLink` on a `PENDING` revert it issues a new one, the old one stops working, and the call returns at once with the new `revertUrl` and `personMessage` (no wait: the person needs the link before anyone can sign), so `reissueLink: true` with `waitForSignature` is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. A `PENDING` answer after a wait carries the no-repeat `nextSteps` (end the turn, then call once more when the person says they signed), with `waitForSignature: 0` too. It never hands out the long link while the engine serves the pages: the person signs at the short link the agent gave them earlier, which the agent still has (the engine keeps only its hash); if it is lost or the person asks for a new one, the agent calls `request_revert` with `revertId` and `reissueLink: true` for a fresh short link.
