A config with a `notify` key, which configured the parked Telegram notifier, is refused, as every unknown key is, with a message that names it.
- **Waiting write (M4.1).** `write` takes `waitForApproval` (integer seconds, 0 to 110). When the write is held, or the call names an `approvalId` whose approval is not signed yet, the engine keeps the call open up to that long, checking about once a second. As soon as the approval is signed (by the page's relay, `basalt approve` or the `approval` argument) it applies the held write exactly as a repeat with `approvalId` would, and returns `APPLIED` in the same call. On timeout it returns `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` with the same `approval` body, and nothing is applied; its message tells the agent not to wait again but to end its turn, telling the person the write waits for their approval (link, expiry) and that it applies the write when they say they approved (a later `write` with `approvalId` applies at once). One wait per turn: a review can take longer than any wait, and MCP cannot start a client turn when the person approves. Default: **0 on a new write**, so the agent gets the link at once and can hand it over; **90 when `approvalId` is given**. The intended loop: the agent shows `personMessage`, then, in the same turn, repeats the write once with `approvalId` (also after a `list_pending` that handed over a link), and the call returns `APPLIED` once the person signs, with no "I approved it" from the person. Bound: Claude Code moves an MCP call still running after 2 minutes to a background task (`CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS`, default 120000), and its hard tool timeout (`MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT`) is about 28 hours, so 110 seconds keeps a wait in the foreground. A client with a 60 second request timeout should pass a smaller value.

### What an approval binds (decision M2-1)

Bound: the statement, its parameters, and for every affected row, cascades included: table, operation, cascade flag, primary key, full old row, and the new values of the columns the statement assigns (`UPDATE … SET` targets; the `INSERT` column list, or every column when it is omitted). Not bound: defaults, identity and sequence values, and values set by the app's triggers. A write that would be held and contains a value that changes between transactions is refused with `STATEMENT_REFUSED` reason `NOT_REPEATABLE`. Postgres reads the strings `now`, `today`, `tomorrow` and `yesterday` as the time the write runs, so they count as such values when they are a literal or a parameter (see [Error codes](#error-codes)).

**Preview line.** One per affected row: the `jsonb` text Postgres returns for

```json
{"op": "UPDATE", "key": {"id": 7}, "old": {"id": 7, "status": "open"}, "set": {"status": "merged"}, "table": "public.leads", "cascaded": false}
```

`key` and `old` are `null` for an `INSERT`; `set` is `null` for a `DELETE` and for cascaded rows. A row of a table the agent role cannot `SELECT` is redacted to `{"op", "table", "rowHash", "cascaded", "redacted": true}` with `rowHash` = lowercase hex of `sha256(jsonb_build_array(key, old, set)::text)`. Lines are ordered by their text `COLLATE "C"` (byte order). Clients hash and parse the text as given and never re-serialise it. Planned: on the approval page the engine serves, the person can reveal a redacted line's values with their passkey ([Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent)).

**Binding text.** The `jsonb` text Postgres returns for

```json
{"kind": "APPROVE", "rows": 15, "agent": "bot", "runId": "…", "intent": "…", "params": ["merged"], "taskId": "…", "database": "app", "rowsHash": "<hex>", "expiresAt": "2026-11-12T06:02:11.123456Z", "statement": "…", "approvalId": "…", "assignedColumns": ["status"]}
```

`rowsHash` = lowercase hex of `sha256(utf8(lines joined by "\n"))`. `expiresAt` is RFC 3339 UTC with microseconds. Keys appear in `jsonb` order (shorter first, then bytewise); clients parse the text and never rebuild it. `kind` is `APPROVE` (M3 adds `REVERT`). **Challenge** = `sha256(utf8(binding text))`, in `clientDataJSON.challenge` as base64url without padding.

### Codes

All base64url is RFC 4648 §5 without padding. Byte fields inside JSON are base64url strings.

| Payload | Carried as | JSON |
|---|---|---|
| Approval request | URL `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#2.<b64url(deflate-raw(json))>` ([Format 2](#format-2-the-compact-request)) | `[shapes, texts]` or `[shapes, texts, credentialIds]`: the binding text, then the preview lines in order, then the database's active credential ids |
| Approval request, format 1 (read only) | URL `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#1.<b64url(deflate-raw(json))>` | `{ "approvalId", "binding", "lines", "credentialIds" }`: `binding` the binding text, `lines` the preview lines in order, `credentialIds` the registered passkeys (may be empty). The pages still read it; the engine no longer writes it. The engine's local pages serve this JSON at `GET /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}`, with (planned) `revealNonce` ([Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent)), which a long link never carries |
| Approval response | Text `basalt-approval.1.<b64url(json)>` | `{ "approvalId", "credentialId", "authenticatorData", "clientDataJson", "signature" }` from `navigator.credentials.get` (`signature` DER) |
| Passkey registration request | URL `<base>/passkeys/register#1.<b64url(json)>` | `{ "challenge", "userId", "userName", "displayName" }`: 32 and 16 random bytes; names are labels for the passkey picker. `handBack` (optional): the hand-back secret, only in links of the pages `basalt init` serves itself ([CLI](#cli)); the page then posts the passkey to init and shows the pairing code instead of the long code |
| Passkey registration response | Text `basalt-passkey.1.<b64url(json)>` | `{ "credentialId", "clientDataJson", "authenticatorData", "publicKey", "publicKeyAlgorithm" }`: `publicKey` the SubjectPublicKeyInfo DER from `getPublicKey()`, algorithm `-7` (ES256) only; `prfSupported` optional boolean: the page asked for the WebAuthn `prf` extension and the authenticator reported it enabled (absent means not, or unknown). Registration never fails because `prf` is missing, and no `prf` output is ever read, logged or sent. Nothing acts on the flag until the vault channel's Phase 1b |
| Pairing code | Text `XXXX-XXXX`, shown on the registration page after a hand-back, typed into `basalt init` ([CLI](#cli)) | 8 characters of Crockford base32 (`0123456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ`, 40 bits): the first 5 bytes of `sha256("basalt-pairing.1\n" ‖ challenge ‖ credentialId ‖ x ‖ y)`, where `challenge` is the registration request's and `x`, `y` the 32-byte P-256 coordinates of the registered key, shown as two groups of four. Read as typed: surrounding space trimmed, any case, `-` and spaces dropped, `O` read as `0` and `I`, `L` as `1`; anything that is then not 8 Crockford characters is not a pairing code. Shared vectors, which Go and the page both run: [`pairing-code.json`](api/mcp/passkey/pairing-code.json) |

### Format 2: the compact request

Owner decisions VC-7 and VC-29; the layout details are VC-31. The engine writes approval and revert links in format 2; the pages read format 2 and format 1. Schema: [`compact-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/links/compact-request.schema.json). Shared vectors, which the contract test, the Go codec and the pages all run: [`compact-request.json`](api/mcp/links/compact-request.json).

`#2.<b64url(deflate-raw(json))>`, where the JSON is `[shapes, texts]` or `[shapes, texts, credentialIds]`:

- **`texts`**: the binding text first, then the lines in order: the preview lines of an approval, or the revert lines (a large plan's sample) of a revert. At least 2 texts, at most 51 for an approval and 1001 for a revert. There is no separate id: it is the binding's `approvalId` or `revertId`.
- **`credentialIds`** (optional, M4.1): the database's active passkey credential ids (`basalt_meta.agent_passkeys`, at most 100), base64url without padding, each 1 to 1023 bytes and listed once. The engine and `basalt approve`/`basalt revert` write them whenever the database has any. They are not signed: the page passes them, as it passes an engine answer's `credentialIds`, as `allowCredentials` on every approval, revert, widen and reveal prompt, so the browser offers only this database's passkeys (every local database's pages share RP ID `localhost`, so without them the picker offers the person's passkeys for every database). A list naming a passkey the database does not hold only makes the prompt find none; Postgres checks the signature either way. When the prompt offered some and ends without a signature, the page says the device may hold none of this database's passkeys.
- **A text** is a JSON string, the text as it is, or `[shape, value, ...]`: the index of a shape in `shapes` and one value per slot of that shape.
- **`shapes`**: the key layouts the texts share, each listed once and used by at least one text, never more than there are texts. A shape is 1 to 64 entries in text order. An entry is `"key"`, which takes one slot, or `["key", ["column", ...]]` for a key whose value is an object (`key`, `old`, `set`, `now`), which takes one slot per column (at most 1600), in text order.
- **A value** is a string, `true`, `false`, `null`, an integer of magnitude at most 2^53 − 1 that is not `-0`, an array of values (nested at most 32 deep), or `{"raw": "<text>"}`: the value's `jsonb` text as it is, for anything else (other numbers, nested objects). Numbers are read as JavaScript's `JSON.parse` reads them, so `1.0` is the integer 1.

**Rebuild.** A text `[shape, value, ...]` is `{`, then its entries joined by `, `, then `}`. An entry is `"key": value`, or for an object entry `"key": {`, then `"column": value` for each column joined by `, `, then `}`. An array is `[`, its values joined by `, `, then `]`; an integer is written in decimal; `{"raw": text}` is `text`. Keys, columns and strings are quoted as Postgres's `escape_json` quotes them: `\"`, `\\`, `\b`, `\f`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t`, any other character below U+0020 as `\u00xx` (lowercase hex), everything else as it is. That is `jsonb`'s own text layout, so the texts rebuild to the binding text and the lines byte for byte. The engine checks each text rebuilds exactly and sends any text that does not as a string.

**Limits.** The page first repairs the fragment ([The pages repair what they can](#the-pages-repair-what-they-can): line breaks, spaces and the other characters a client adds are dropped), then decodes it. It refuses the repaired fragment unless: its payload is at most 1 MiB of base64url characters and nothing else (a decoder given an unrepaired fragment, as the Go codec is, refuses a line break rather than skipping it); it inflates to at most 1 MiB of UTF-8, with nothing after the end of the deflate stream (the page finds that end itself, without relying on the browser's decompressor); that is one JSON value the schema allows, with nothing after it; and the rebuilt texts total at most 1 MiB. Then it checks the rebuilt request exactly as a format 1 request: the binding's id against the id in the page's path, which the approval and revert pages require (a page whose path has no id refuses to run: a format 2 request has no id of its own), the kind, the number of lines, and `rowsHash` (or `sampleHash`). A wrong rebuild fails closed: the lines no longer hash to the binding's `rowsHash`, and a binding text that differs from the one Postgres built has a different challenge, which Postgres refuses.

A page first repairs the fragment and, when it still does not decode, offers to read the link from the pasted message ([Showing links](#showing-links), planned M3.5). A page whose fragment does not decode as base64url, deflate-raw or JSON says the link looks cut short and to open it with `basalt approve --open` (or `basalt revert ... --open`), which builds the full link itself. The approval page checks `sha256(lines joined by "\n")` equals the binding's `rowsHash` and refuses to ask for a signature otherwise. Registration asks for `userVerification: "required"`, `residentKey: "required"`, `attestation: "none"`, ES256 only. `basalt init` verifies the registration in Go: type `webauthn.create`, challenge, origin, `rpIdHash`, the UP, UV and AT flags, the attested credential id equals `credentialId`, and a P-256 key.

### Checks in Postgres

`submit_approval` accepts an assertion only if: `type` is `webauthn.get`; the challenge is the approval's binding; `origin` and `rpIdHash` match the passkey's stored RP ID and origin; UP and UV are set; the signature counter increased (0 is accepted while the stored count is 0); the ES256 signature over `authenticatorData ‖ sha256(clientDataJSON)` is valid; the approval is not expired and not yet approved. Applying with `basalt.approval_id` commits only if the approval is approved, unused, unexpired, of this agent and run, and the statement, parameters, row count and `rowsHash` recomputed at commit match. Failures are `BS005`.

### Rows hidden from the agent

Owner decisions HR-1 to HR-4. Applies to approvals and to reverts ([Revert](#revert) links here). A redacted line (`"redacted": true`: a table the agent role cannot `SELECT`, cascaded rows above all, and revert lines redacted for row-level security) keeps its values from the agent. The person can see them before signing, on the page the engine serves (loopback or a [Vault channel](#vault-channel) session), after a fresh passkey proof Postgres verifies. Nothing new is stored, no view or tool result an agent role reads changes, and the engine holds the values for one request.

- **When.** The engine's `GET /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}` and `GET /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}` carry `revealNonce`, the item's single-use nonce (16 bytes, base64url), read with `reveal_nonce` ([version 29](#version-29-reveal-hidden-revert-rows)); an approval's only once the guard has [version 30](#version-30-reveal-hidden-approval-rows), which serves its reveal. The page shows a "Show values with passkey" button above the hidden rows (after a reveal, "Hide values" hides them again on the page only, without a prompt) when it has `revealNonce` and at least one redacted line; it never prompts on its own (HR-4). A long link carries no nonce, so its page shows the values hidden and says to open the short link to see them. The CLI cannot reveal.
- **Reveal key.** For each reveal the page makes `revealSecret`, 16 random bytes (`crypto.getRandomValues`), and sends it only to the engine, with the proof (over loopback, or inside the end-to-end encrypted session), never shown. The engine derives `revealKey = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm = the 16 decoded bytes of revealSecret, salt = empty, info = "basalt-reveal-key.1", length = 32)` and passes Postgres only that key, never the secret; Postgres refuses unless `sha256(revealKey)` is the hash the proof signed. So a reveal proof alone, pasted or leaked, is worthless to the agent role, as a widen proof is without its `newSecret`. The engine never logs or stores the secret or the key.
- **Challenge.** `sha256(utf8("basalt-reveal.1\n" + kind + "\n" + id + "\n" + revealNonce + "\n" + hex(sha256(revealKey)) + "\n" + rowsHash))`, in `clientDataJSON.challenge` as base64url without padding. `kind` is `APPROVE` or `REVERT`, `id` the `approvalId` or `revertId` in canonical lowercase form, `revealNonce` as the request carries it, hex lowercase, `rowsHash` the binding's (a large plan's too, never its `sampleHash`). The prefix keeps a reveal proof from verifying as an approval or revert signature (their challenges hash a JSON binding) or a widen proof (`basalt-rescope.1`), and `rowsHash` makes it worthless after a rescope. Postgres checks it with `_verify_assertion`, as `submit_approval` does. The page never shows a reveal proof as a code to paste.
- **Request.** `POST /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}/reveal` or `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/reveal` ([Local pages](#local-pages)), body [`reveal-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/approval/reveal-request.schema.json): `{ "proof": { "credentialId", "authenticatorData", "clientDataJson", "signature" }, "revealSecret" }`, the proof an assertion as in an approval response without `approvalId`.
- **Revert.** One call, `reveal_revert_rows(revertId, proof, revealKey)`: checks the proof and key, replaces the nonce, appends `ROWS_REVEALED`, rebuilds each redacted line the request carries (every line, or a large plan's sample) from the ledger and the live row, and returns the answer, or that the rows changed. Both outcomes commit, so a proof that passed is used up either way.
- **Approval.** The values exist only while a preview transaction is open, and a `PREVIEW` transaction cannot commit, so two transactions: `open_reveal(approvalId, proof, revealKey)` checks the proof and key, replaces the nonce, appends `ROWS_REVEALED` and returns a fresh 128-bit ticket good for 2 minutes (Postgres keeps only its `sha256`); then the engine re-runs the held statement with its parameters in a `PREVIEW` transaction, as at the hold, records it with `finish_change` as an apply does, calls `reveal_approval_rows(approvalId, ticket)` there and rolls back. The agent role has no ticket: it exists only in the engine's memory and as a bind parameter. The re-run fires the app's triggers again and locks the rows briefly, as the hold's preview did (M3-7).
- **Answer.** [`reveal-response.schema.json`](api/mcp/approval/reveal-response.schema.json): `{ "rows": [{ "line", "row", "secretColumns"? }] }`, one entry per redacted line, in line order. `line` is the 0-based index of the line in the request's `lines`. `row` is the exact text of `jsonb_build_array(key, old, set)` (approval) or `jsonb_build_array(key, now, set)` (revert), the text that line's `rowHash` hashes. For a row without `secretColumns` the page checks `sha256(utf8(row))` equals the line's `rowHash` before it shows any value; a row that fails is an error, never values. So for those rows neither the engine nor the Vault can show the person values other than the ones bound into what they sign. The engine answers with `Cache-Control: no-store`, keeps nothing after the response, writes nothing to `stateDir` and logs no value; the ticket and the reveal key never reach the MCP channel or the engine's log. A Postgres server that logs bound parameters (`log_statement = 'all'`, or `log_min_duration_statement` with parameters) records the ticket and the reveal key in the server log; only the database admin can turn that on, and either is worthless after its request (the ticket after 2 minutes, the key without a fresh proof).
- **`SECRET` columns** ([PII classification](#pii-classification), HR-3). Never returned: Postgres sets every `SECRET` column to `null` wherever it appears in `row` and names it in `secretColumns`. Such a row cannot be checked on the page: `rowHash` hashes the whole row text, so no per-column hash lets the page rebuild it, and an engine or Vault could add `secretColumns` to any row and change its other values. The page therefore accepts the row only when every value of the named columns is `null`, shows those columns as "secret · not shown", and says under the table "Row <key> has a secret column, so this page can't check it against what you sign.", never that it is checked. `IDENTIFIER` and `FREE_TEXT` values are shown as they are: the page is the person's, and only the wire to the model is tokenized.
- **Refusals.** Every proof, key, nonce or ticket failure (no proof, a proof over another item, nonce, key or `rowsHash`, an approval or revert signature, a widen proof, a replay, an expired or wrong ticket, `reveal_approval_rows` outside `PREVIEW` or for another statement or parameters) is `APPROVAL_INVALID` or `REVERT_INVALID` with reason `REVEAL_REFUSED` (the Postgres detail) and one fixed message, and changes nothing. Hidden rows that changed since the hold or the prepare are reason `ROWS_CHANGED` and return nothing: an approval then can no longer apply (it would fail with `APPROVAL_INVALID` at commit), so the page tells the person to ask the agent to try the write again, and for a revert to prepare it again.
- **Single use.** A proof that passes consumes the nonce and Postgres sets a new one, whatever the outcome (synced passkeys report a signature counter of 0, so the counter cannot stop a replay). A refused proof changes nothing.
- **Ledger.** `ROWS_REVEALED` (detail `kind`, `id`, `credentialId`, `rows`, and `rowsChanged: true` on a revert whose rows changed: hashes and counts, no value), appended when the proof passes. It records the person's request: for an approval at `open_reveal`, before the re-run.
- **Limits** (as for a widen). Over the Vault on `RELAY`, whoever holds the link secret and gets the Vault to route the session to their own engine can read what the page reads (VC-42). In same-user mode an agent running as the person's OS user can read the person's screen. What the reveal guarantees is that the agent's database role learns nothing new. Out of scope: lines of a large plan beyond its sample, approval lines of rows RLS filters on a table the agent can `SELECT` (they are not redacted today), and the CLI.

## Revert

M3. Owner decisions M3-1 to M3-14. This section fixes the rules and the bytes. JSON Schemas for every payload are in [`api/mcp/revert/`](api/mcp/revert/).

- **Scopes:** `CHANGE` (one change, a revert's own change included), `RUN`, `TASK` (every agent's changes with that task id). `APPLIED` and `REVERTED` changes that wrote rows count (a reverted one shows its rows as `ALREADY_REVERTED`). Planned (RS-1 to RS-3, [version 20](#version-20-revert-scope)): what a revert covers is its [scope descriptor](#the-scope-descriptor), a scope narrowed to some agents, runs and tables; an agent role proposes only its own work, and only the person widens it, on the signing page ([Rescope, widen and reject](#rescope-widen-and-reject)).
- **A revert can be reverted** (owner decision AE-14). The ledger is append-only and a revert is itself a recorded write, so reverting a revert's change brings the reverted values back (a redo). People still win and the person still signs. An agent may request it for a revert of its own change or run (`request_revert` with the revert's `changeId`).
- **A revert is a new change.** It restores old values and never edits or deletes ledger rows. It is recorded as one change record with `op: REVERT`, `actor.kind: PERSON` and `revertId` (its `agent` is the role that relayed it), one `ROW_CHANGE` per restored row (detail `revertOf`), and a `REVERTED` ledger row with the credential id, the binding hash and every skipped row (table, key or row hash, reason; no values). Each covered change gets `revertedBy`; its status becomes `REVERTED` only when none of its rows was skipped. Every agent whose changes a revert covered sees the revert's change record.
- **Only a person.** A revert runs only inside `apply_revert`, after it verifies a passkey assertion over the revert binding with the same checks as `submit_approval`. Agents may preview and prepare (from `request_revert` the agent prepares one for the person and hands them the link); they cannot sign. No MCP tool applies a revert without the person's signature: `request_revert` may deliver the person's signed code, which `apply_revert` verifies (N12-R8).
- **Row limits do not apply** to a revert, and no line cap does: a revert of up to 1000 lines is applied in one call; a larger one is a plan applied in batches (see [Large plans](#large-plans)). The intent comes from the person (`--intent`, or through `request_revert` the agent's wording of the person's reason, which the page shows before the person signs).
- **Expiry.** A prepared revert must be signed (and, up to 1000 lines, applied) within 1 hour. A signed large plan must be finished within 24 hours of the signature.
- **Window.** A row whose old values were pruned (30 days) is skipped as `VALUES_PRUNED`.

### What is restored

Per row, BasaltDB takes the first and the last in-scope ledger row, following primary-key changes, and restores the row to its state before the first in-scope change: an inserted row is deleted, an updated row gets its old values back (key included), a deleted row is re-inserted, and a row inserted and deleted in scope gets no line. Cascaded rows (deleted or set to null by an FK cascade) are restored like any other row. Generated columns are not written; identity `ALWAYS` columns are re-inserted with `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE`; columns added after the change keep their current value or default.

### Conflicts (people win)

A row is restored only if nobody wrote it after the last in-scope change. Otherwise it is skipped, reported, and never overwritten; the other rows are still restored.

| Reason | When |
|---|---|
| `CHANGED_AFTER` | The row's `xmin` is not the version the last in-scope change left, or its values differ from that change's new row, or someone wrote it between two in-scope changes. Covers people, the app, other agents and later changes outside the scope |
| `ROW_GONE` | The row the revert would update or delete no longer exists, or the guard cannot see it |
| `KEY_TAKEN` | The revert would re-insert a row, or move a key back, and a row with that key exists |
| `ALREADY_REVERTED` | The row was last written by a revert of this change |
| `VALUES_PRUNED` | The old values are past the 30-day window |
| `SCHEMA_CHANGED` | The table, a column of the old row, or the primary key changed |
| `CASCADE` | The inverse write would cascade into a row that is not part of the revert |
| `CONSTRAINT` | The inverse write fails a constraint on trial; the line carries `sqlState` |

### Lines, binding and codes

**Revert line.** One per row, the `jsonb` text Postgres returns for

```json
{"op": "UPDATE", "key": {"id": 7}, "now": {"id": 7, "status": "merged"}, "set": {"id": 7, "status": "open"}, "table": "public.leads", "action": "RESTORE", "cascaded": false, "conflict": null, "sqlState": null, "changeIds": ["…"]}
```

`op` is the inverse write (`DELETE`, `UPDATE`, `INSERT`); `now` the row as it is now (`null` if absent); `set` the values written (`null` for `DELETE`); `action` `RESTORE` or `SKIP`; `conflict` a reason above on `SKIP` lines. A row of a table the preparing role cannot `SELECT` is redacted to `{"op", "table", "action", "rowHash", "cascaded", "conflict", "redacted": true, "changeIds"}` with `rowHash` = lowercase hex of `sha256(jsonb_build_array(key, now, set)::text)`. Lines are ordered by their text `COLLATE "C"`; clients never re-serialise them. Planned: on the revert page the engine serves, the person can reveal a redacted line's values with their passkey ([Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent)).

**Binding text.** The `jsonb` text Postgres returns for

```json
{"kind": "REVERT", "rows": 11, "scope": "RUN", "agents": ["bot"], "intent": "…", "scopeId": "…", "skipped": 1, "database": "app", "revertId": "…", "rowsHash": "<hex>", "expiresAt": "2026-11-11T07:02:11.123456Z", "requestedBy": "basalt_agent_bot"}
```

`rowsHash` = lowercase hex of `sha256(utf8(lines joined by "\n"))` over every line, `RESTORE` and `SKIP`, so the person signs both what is restored and what is left alone. **Challenge** = `sha256(utf8(binding text))`, as for approvals. A large plan's binding also has `"batched": true` and `"sampleHash"` (see [Large plans](#large-plans)); a one-call revert's binding has neither key.

From [version 20](#version-20-revert-scope) (RS-3), the binding carries the scope as descriptors instead of `scope`, `scopeId` and `agents` ([`revert-binding.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-binding.schema.json)); there is no compatibility shim:

```json
{"kind": "REVERT", "rows": 14, "intent": "…", "select": {…descriptor…}, "skipped": 1, "widened": true, "database": "app", "proposed": {…descriptor…}, "revertId": "…", "rowsHash": "<hex>", "agentRows": {"basalt_agent_bot": 11, "basalt_agent_sync": 4}, "expiresAt": "…", "requestedBy": "basalt_agent_bot"}
```

- `select`: the scope the person signs. `proposed`: what the agent asked for, **omitted when it equals `select`** (every revert nobody rescoped).
- `widened`: `true` when `select` covers any change not made by `requestedBy` (its `agents` is `null` or names another role).
- `agentRows`: lines per agent role, `RESTORE` and `SKIP`, for the page's headline; present exactly when `widened` is `true`.
- `database` stays, `current_database()` for display. `rows` may be 0 only for a task proposal with no line of the agent's own, which cannot be signed (the page disables signing; `apply_revert` answers `NOTHING_TO_REVERT`).

The challenge is unchanged: `sha256(utf8(binding text))`, so the signature covers the scope.

| Payload | Carried as | JSON |
|---|---|---|
| Revert request | URL `<base>/reverts/<revertId>#2.<b64url(deflate-raw(json))>` ([Format 2](#format-2-the-compact-request)) | `[shapes, texts]` or `[shapes, texts, credentialIds]`: the binding text, then the revert lines in order, then the database's active credential ids |
| Revert request, format 1 (read only) | URL `<base>/reverts/<revertId>#1.<b64url(deflate-raw(json))>` | `{ "revertId", "binding", "lines", "credentialIds" }`; the JSON the local pages serve at `GET /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}`, which also carries `rescopeNonce` and (planned) `revealNonce` ([Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent)); a long link carries neither |
| Revert response | Text `basalt-revert.1.<b64url(json)>` | `{ "revertId", "credentialId", "authenticatorData", "clientDataJson", "signature" }` |

The revert page checks `rowsHash` (or, for a large plan, `sampleHash`), refuses a binding whose `kind` is not `REVERT`, and shows restored rows (current and restored values) and skipped rows (reason) before asking for the passkey.

**Lines on tables with row-level security** (N12-8). On a table where RLS applies to the preparing role (RLS enabled, and the role neither has the owner's privileges nor `BYPASSRLS`), a line keeps its values only when it is a `RESTORE` line, not cascaded, and every change it names was made by the preparing role; any other line there is redacted as above, and the person can reveal it like any redacted line.

### Large plans

N12. A revert with more than 1000 lines is a **batched plan**:

- `prepare_revert` stores every line in the plan, numbered `1..N` in execution order (the order the trial's writes succeeded, then the `SKIP` lines), and builds one binding over all of them: `rowsHash` over every line in signed order, as above, plus `batched: true` and `sampleHash`.
- **Sample** (N12-4, amended by RSS-1): 1000 lines that keep room for both kinds. It takes up to 500 `SKIP` lines and up to 500 `RESTORE` lines; when one kind has fewer, the other fills the rest, up to 1000 in all: `skips = min(skipped, max(500, 1000 - rows))`, `restores = min(rows, 1000 - skips)`, with `skipped` and `rows` the binding's. The sample lists the first `skips` `SKIP` lines, then the first `restores` `RESTORE` lines, each kind in signed order. So 1500 `SKIP` and 50 `RESTORE` lines give 950 and 50; 2000 and 2000 give 500 and 500; 10 and 3000 give 10 and 990. `sampleHash` = lowercase hex `sha256(utf8(sample joined by "\n"))`. `prepare_revert` returns the sample as `lines`, and the revert request carries it. The page (and `revert.Request.Check` in Go) checks `sampleHash` and that the sample splits as the binding's `skipped` and `rows` give, then says how many of the plan's lines it shows and that the signature covers all of them.
- **What the page counts.** Every count the page shows comes from signed data: the totals from the binding's `rows` and `skipped`, the shown counts from the sample lines, which hash to `sampleHash`. Hidden rows are `rows` less the `RESTORE` lines shown and `skipped` less the `SKIP` lines shown. The request carries no other count, and the page shows no per-table or per-column count of hidden rows: nothing signed gives them.
- **Signature:** `apply_revert` with the person's code verifies it, opens the revert's change record, marks the `SKIP` lines skipped and records `REVERT_SIGNED`; it writes no row of the person's tables. The plan can then be applied for 24 hours, only by the role that prepared it.
- **Batches:** `apply_revert_batch` (MCP tool above) applies up to 200 pending lines of the plan. Each line runs at most once; a line whose row changed after the plan was built is skipped (people win). Every batch is one transaction with its `ROW_CHANGE` rows under the revert's change and a `REVERT_BATCH` ledger row; a failed batch leaves nothing, so the agent resumes from the pending lines.
- **Finish:** when no line is pending, the plan is `APPLIED`, a `REVERTED` ledger row is appended, and each covered change becomes `REVERTED` only if every line naming it was restored.

### Checks in Postgres

`apply_revert` runs the revert only if the assertion passes every `submit_approval` check with the challenge taken from this revert's binding, and the revert is neither expired nor applied. It then locks the rows, recomputes the lines for the preparing role, and refuses with `REVERT_INVALID` unless their `rowsHash` equals the signed one: a row changed after the preview fails the whole revert and nothing is written. Writes run as `<prefix>_guard` with all constraints immediate; a failure the trial did not predict aborts the revert. Failures are `BS005`. For a large plan `apply_revert` only records the signature; `apply_revert_batch` checks the caller, the signature state, the expiry and every line number, then re-checks each line against the signed text with its row locked.

From [version 20](#version-20-revert-scope): `apply_revert` and `apply_revert_batch` lock the revert row (`FOR UPDATE`) before any check, so a rescope and a signature cannot interleave; `apply_revert` recomputes the lines from the stored scope descriptor and refuses with `REVERT_INVALID` when the binding's `select` is not the stored one, and refuses a `REJECTED` revert. From [version 21](#version-21-widen-proof-rescope-and-reset), with `rescope_revert` and `reset_revert`: a rescope or reset after the signature is refused, and a rescope before it changes the binding, so an earlier signature fails. A `REJECTED` revert cannot be rescoped or reset. From [version 23](#version-23-reject-a-revert), `reject_revert` marks a pending, unsigned revert `REJECTED`.

### The scope descriptor

RS-1, RS-3, RS-4, from [version 20](#version-20-revert-scope) ([`revert-select.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-select.schema.json)). One JSON object, stored with the revert (`reverts.selection`) and carried in the binding as `select` and `proposed`:

```json
{"runs": null, "scope": "TASK", "agents": ["basalt_agent_bot", "basalt_agent_sync"], "tables": ["public.invoices", "public.leads"], "scopeId": "0193…"}
```

- **`scope`, `scopeId`**: `CHANGE`, `RUN` or `TASK` and its id. A person's widen may turn a `RUN` proposal into the run's `TASK` when the run has a task id.
- **`agents`**: the roles whose changes are in the selection. An agent's proposal has exactly one, itself. A person's widen may send `null`: every agent with changes in the scope.
- **`runs`**: `null` for every run of the selected agents in the scope; otherwise sorted run ids, each in the scope and of a selected agent. Only for `TASK`. At most 20.
- **`tables`**: `null` for every table; otherwise sorted `schema.table` names, each a guarded table written in the scope by a selected agent. At most 20. It filters lines, not changes: a change that wrote two tables can be half selected, and the trial run (M3-7) reports what that breaks (`CONSTRAINT`, `CASCADE`, `KEY_TAKEN`) before the person signs.
- **`store`** (RS-4): reserved for multi-database work, absent today. Postgres refuses a descriptor that carries it (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`). How a database is identified is decided after M5; `database` in the binding stays a display key.
- **Normalized in Postgres.** Every key present, lists sorted by their text `COLLATE "C"` and deduplicated, `null` for all, kept as the `jsonb` text Postgres returns, so the same choice always gives the same text. The widen proof signs that text.
- **Own work.** A descriptor is inside an agent role's own work when `agents` is exactly `[that role]`, its change or run is the role's own or the role ran in its task, and `runs` and `tables` lie inside that work.

**Link budget (VC-28, VC-30).** A proposal's descriptor adds about 100 characters before compression; a list of 20 run ids about 760. A rescope never travels on a long link (it needs the page talking to the engine). An agent's filtered proposal may make a long link go over budget, which follows the existing over-budget rule.

### Rescope, widen and reject

RS-2, RS-5; plan sections "Person side" and "Links after a widen". The Postgres side (`rescope_revert`, `reset_revert`, the redaction for the requesting agent) is [version 21](#version-21-widen-proof-rescope-and-reset); the engine serves `POST …/scope`, the link rotation and `LINK_RESET` on loopback and in Vault sessions; `reject_revert` is [version 23](#version-23-reject-a-revert), and the engine serves `POST …/reject` on loopback and in Vault sessions, which the page's Reject button posts with its note. The person agrees (signs the proposal as it is), rescopes it, or rejects it, on the revert page served by the engine (loopback, or the [Vault channel](#vault-channel) session). A long link with no engine shows the scope read only and says widening needs the page from the engine. Rescoping and rejecting go through [`POST …/scope` and `POST …/reject`](#local-pages); the engine calls `rescope_revert` and `reject_revert` with the credential of the role that prepared the revert.

| Select sent to `POST …/scope` | Needs | Checked in |
|---|---|---|
| Inside the agent's own work (narrow the proposal, drop the agent's own filters, or go back to the proposal), on a revert not yet widened | The link secret | Engine (the link), Postgres (own work) |
| Wider than the agent's own work | A **widen proof** and the **new link secret** it names | Postgres (`rescope_revert`) |
| Anything after a widen: inside the widest select the person proved (narrow, or back to the agent's own work) | The **scope token** of that proven call, which the engine adds (or a new widen proof) | Postgres (`rescope_revert`) |

- **Widen proof.** A WebAuthn assertion by the person's passkey ([`revert-scope-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-scope-request.schema.json), `proof`) with challenge `sha256(utf8("basalt-rescope.1\n" + revertId + "\n" + nonce + "\n" + hex(sha256(rescopeKey)) + "\n" + normalized select))`, where `nonce` is the revert's `rescopeNonce` (base64url, as the revert request carries it), `rescopeKey = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm = the 16 decoded bytes of newSecret, salt = empty, info = "basalt-rescope-key.1", length = 32)`, and hex is lowercase. The prefix keeps a widen proof from ever verifying as an approval or a revert signature (their challenges hash a JSON binding).
- **`newSecret`.** The link secret the revert has after the widen: 16 random bytes the page makes (`crypto.getRandomValues`), sent only to the engine (over loopback, or inside the end-to-end encrypted session), never shown. The engine derives `rescopeKey` and passes Postgres only that key, never the secret; `rescope_revert` refuses unless `sha256(rescopeKey)` is the hash the proof signed. A proof pasted into the chat is worthless without the secret.
- **Nonce.** 16 random bytes per revert (`reverts.rescope_nonce`), carried in the revert request the engine serves (it reads it with the requesting role's `revert_nonce`, [version 22](#version-22-the-rescope-nonce)), and in the `409` the engine answers for a widened revert whose lines it no longer holds. Every proven widen consumes it and sets a new one; `reset_revert` sets a new one. A proof is good for one widen: synced passkeys report a signature counter of 0, so the counter cannot stop a replay.
- **Checks.** `rescope_revert` verifies the proof with the same checks as `submit_approval` (`_verify_assertion`: a registered, unrevoked passkey, RP ID and origin, the counter where the authenticator keeps one) and records the credential id in the `REVERT_RESCOPED` ledger row, which holds hashes only (`sha256(rescopeKey)`, old and new `rowsHash`), never a key or secret. Any proof or token failure is `REVERT_INVALID` detail `WIDEN_REFUSED`, nothing changes, and nothing is returned.
- **Check order** (so a select cannot be used to probe other agents' work). `rescope_revert` checks, in this order, and stops at the first failure:
  1. **Shape only**, from the descriptor alone: the schema (every key, types, `runs` only with `TASK`), the reserved `store` key, at most 20 runs and 20 tables. These read nothing but the request: `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`BS011` detail `SHAPE`, `STORE_RESERVED` or `FILTER_CAP`), 400 on the pages API.
  2. **Own work.** Is the select provably inside the requesting role's own work (`agents` exactly `[requestedBy]`, the change or run its own or a task it ran in, every listed run its own run in the scope, every listed table one it wrote in the scope)? This test only answers yes or no; it raises nothing itself.
  3. **Proof or token**, for every select that is not provably own work, and for every select at all once the revert has a ceiling (below): a valid widen proof with its key, or the scope token with the select inside the ceiling. Every failure, whatever it is (no proof, a bad proof, a used nonce, a wrong key, no token, a wrong token, a select outside the ceiling, and a select naming another agent's run or table, an unknown run or table, or an agent with no changes), is `REVERT_INVALID` detail `WIDEN_REFUSED` with one fixed message, 409 on the pages API. So an agent that sends `{"agents": [itself], "scope": "TASK", "runs": [X]}` learns nothing about `X`: another agent's run and a run that does not exist read alike.
  4. **Scope membership**, only for a select that passed 3 or was own work: each listed agent, run and table must lie in the scope (`BS011` detail `FILTER` or `DESCRIPTOR`, `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, 400). Only the person, proven in 3, or the requesting role about its own work, reaches this step.
- **After a widen the token is required.** Once a revert has a ceiling (`reverts.ceiling IS NOT NULL`), `rescope_revert` (any select, own work included) and `reject_revert` refuse a call without the scope token (`REVERT_INVALID` `WIDEN_REFUSED`), or without a new valid proof for `rescope_revert`. Checking `session_user` is not enough there: the requesting agent role can call both functions directly with its own credential. The agent role's only way back is `reset_revert` (its `reissueLink`), which needs no token (owner, Q8), drops the person's scope and is recorded as such; after it the revert has no ceiling, so the agent role can then also call `reject_revert` without a token.
- **Scope token.** A proven widen returns a fresh 128-bit token; Postgres stores only its hash (`reverts.scope_token_hash`) and the proven select as the ceiling (`reverts.ceiling`). The engine keeps the token in memory with the rotated link and adds it to later narrowing calls that carry that link's secret. A narrowing select must lie inside the ceiling; no new prompt. A new proven widen replaces token and ceiling; a reset clears them.
- **What a proven call returns.** Only a call with a valid proof and key, or with the token, gets the full lines (every agent's values, `set` included) and the **catalog**: the agents with changes in the scope, their runs (id, agent, start, change count), the tables written (row counts) and, from [version 32](#version-32-the-agent-of-each-change-in-the-catalog), `changeAgents`, the agent role of every change in the scope by change id ([`revert-scope-response.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-scope-response.schema.json)). No other function returns the catalog and no view an agent role reads holds it. The engine keeps those lines and the catalog in memory only, keyed by the rotated link, until the revert expires; after an engine restart `GET` with the rotated link returns the redacted view and the page asks the person to widen again. Nothing with other agents' values is written to `stateDir`.
- **The agent of each row** (owner decision Q-11). A line names its `changeIds`, not their agents. `changeAgents` maps each change in the ceiling to the role that made it, so it covers every change id the lines of any select inside the ceiling name. It is part of the catalog, so it has the catalog's gate and no other: only a call with a valid widen proof and key, or with the scope token of one, gets it. An own-work rescope (no proof, no ceiling), a refused call, `GET /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}`, the long link, every MCP tool and every `basalt_meta` view get no map, so the requesting agent never learns which other agents changed which rows. The page tags each row (a "Changed by" cell) with the agents its `changeIds` map to (short names, as the headline), amber when any of them is not `requestedBy`; a row whose change ids are not all in the map gets no tag rather than a guessed one. Without a catalog (before a widen) or without `changeAgents` (a guard older than version 32) the page tags rows only when the select names exactly one agent, as before.
- **Every rescope** rebuilds the lines in Postgres (with the trial run), replaces the binding, lines, totals, `rowsHash` and expiry, keeps `proposed`, and restarts the one-hour expiry; the revert keeps its `revertId`. A rescope that runs longer than 100 seconds (N12-R9) is `QUERY_TIMEOUT` reason `TIME_LIMIT` (504 on the pages API), nothing changes, and the page says: "Changing the scope took longer than 100 seconds and was stopped. Nothing changed. Choose fewer agents, runs or tables, or try again." A widened revert costs two passkey prompts (the proof, then the signature); signing as proposed, or narrowing, costs one. When the scope has 0 lines to restore the page disables signing and says there is nothing to revert in this scope.
- **Reset.** `reissueLink: true` (from `request_revert`, `revert_plan` or `list_pending`) on a widened revert resets it to the agent's proposal through `reset_revert` (own lines, new binding, ceiling and token cleared, new nonce, `REVERT_RESET` in the ledger) and issues a link for that. The page holding the person's rotated link then gets `NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK_RESET` ([Link secrets](#link-secrets)) and says: "The agent reset this revert to its own work. Ask it for the new link, and widen again if you still want to include other agents."
- **Reject.** The page's Reject (optional note up to 500 characters, which the page says the agent will read; in the decision panel on a wide screen, in the bottom bar with a note sheet on a phone) posts `POST …/reject` under the current link secret. `reject_revert` marks the revert `REJECTED`, appends `REVERT_REJECTED` with the note and the path it came by, and the engine revokes the link; the agent's wait returns `REJECTED` at once. No passkey: rejecting can only stop a revert. Before a widen the path is `LINK_OR_AGENT`: the call carried no token, so Postgres cannot tell the person on the link from the agent holding the link, or the agent role calling `reject_revert` itself. After a widen `reject_revert` requires the scope token, which only the engine holds, and adds it only for a request carrying the rotated secret; the path is then `SCOPE_TOKEN`. This does not make a widened revert's reject the person's alone: the agent role can still call `reset_revert` (no token, owner Q8), which drops the person's scope and ceiling, and then `reject_revert` with no token. The ledger then shows `REVERT_RESET` followed by `REVERT_REJECTED` with `via` `LINK_OR_AGENT`, and the page holding the person's rotated link reads `NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK_RESET`. So `SCOPE_TOKEN` proves the person rejected; `LINK_OR_AGENT` never proves who did. Whether a widened revert's end should be the person's alone is an open owner decision. After an engine restart the token is gone (it lives in memory): the person widens again to reject, or lets the revert expire. From a long link with no engine the page says "Close this page to decline; nothing happens unless you sign"; the revert expires in an hour.
- **Two codes, two labels.** The page labels the revert signature "You can give this to the agent" and never shows a widen proof as a code to paste: the widen happens only between the page and the engine (owner, Q14; no CLI widen).

**What the requesting agent can read** (RS-5). Every line naming a change not made by the requesting role is redacted in every view that role reads, whatever its grants (the signed lines themselves do not change):

| Where | Not widened | After a widen |
|---|---|---|
| `request_revert` wait form | As today, plus `select`; `REJECTED` | Status, totals, `widened`, a large plan's `progress`; no scope, `select`, lines or link |
| `GET /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}` with the agent's link | The request | `404 NOT_FOUND`: the link was rotated |
| `agent_reverts` | As today, plus `selection` | Status, totals, `widened`, expiry and progress; `binding_text`, `selection`, `lines` `NULL` |
| `agent_revert_lines`, `revert_plan` | As today | Line number, `outcome`, `conflict`, `sql_state`, `batch` for every line; `line` text only where every change it names is the caller's |
| `list_pending` | The headline from the binding | "Revert widened by the person: N rows to restore." and no scope or link (LP-3) |

## Page links

Owner decisions, Decided Nov 12. Every link is built from one pages base URL (`pagesUrl` in `engine.json`; one Go constant `links.Portal` and one JS constant `PORTAL` for the portal): the engine's local pages `http://localhost:8787` by default until hosting exists, or the portal `https://portal.basaltvault.app`. Only the origins in [Page origins](#page-origins) are accepted. The paths are RESTful and the same on both:

| Page | Path |
|---|---|
| Approve a held write | `/approvals/<approvalId>` (long link: `#2.<request>` appended) |
| Sign a revert | `/reverts/<revertId>` (long link: `#2.<request>` appended) |
| Register a passkey | `/passkeys/register#1.<request>` |
| Check which passkeys a device has (M4.1) | `/passkeys/check#1.<request>` |
| Forget a removed passkey (M4.1) | `/passkeys/forget#1.<request>` |
| A run (reserved, later) | `/runs/<runId>` |


### Page origins

Chat-only operator CO-8. The origins a link may point at, and that the engine and the CLI accept for passkeys, are a closed list compiled into the binary (Go: `links.Portal` and the checks of `internal/engine/config`; JS: `relyingParty` in `web/lib/codec.js`):

- `https://portal.basaltvault.app`, always (RP ID `basaltvault.app`).
- `http://localhost:<port>` (the host is `localhost` exactly: a passkey's origin, and so the page a link opens, is `localhost` only, RP ID `localhost`, so a `pagesUrl`, `approvalPage` or `--pages-url` on `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` or another spelling is refused, not rewritten, with `the local pages run only at localhost; use http://localhost:<port>`), only when `operatedBy` is `PERSON`: the engine runs on the person's own machine, so the loopback page is theirs.

Neither `engine.json`, a flag, nor the bot can add an origin. `*.pages.dev` (Cloudflare's preview and branch URLs, which serve the same files but whose passkeys would not belong to `basaltvault.app`) and every other host are refused in both modes. Where the list is checked:

- **`pagesUrl`**, when `engine.json` is loaded: an origin not in the list is refused, naming the setting and saying `use https://portal.basaltvault.app or a local http://localhost:<port>`. With `"operatedBy": "AGENT"`, a local `pagesUrl` (also the default `http://localhost:8787` when `pagesUrl` is absent) is refused with `pagesUrl <value>: an agent-operated engine uses the portal's pages (https://portal.basaltvault.app); local pages would be on the agent's machine`, unless `devLocalPages` is `true`.
- **`--passkey-origin`** (`basalt init`) and **`--pages-url`** (`basalt approve`, `basalt revert`, `basalt passkey check` and `remove`, `basalt service install`): an origin not in the list is a usage error (exit `2`). `basalt init --operated-by=agent` with a local `--passkey-origin` is a usage error unless `--dev-local-pages` is given.
- **The pages** (`web/lib/codec.js`) run only at `https://portal.basaltvault.app` (no port) or `http://localhost:<port>`, and refuse anything else (`this page only runs at https://portal.basaltvault.app or http://localhost:<port>`) before they touch a passkey.

**`--dev-local-pages`** (development only, design section 5.2). `basalt init --operated-by=agent --dev-local-pages --passkey-origin http://localhost:<port>` lets the owner test `AGENT` mode against loopback pages on their own laptop. Init writes `"devLocalPages": true` to `engine.json` and saves `--dev-local-pages` in `initArgs`; running init again without it removes the key. It is a usage error without `--operated-by=agent`, and with a `--passkey-origin` that is not local. A config with `devLocalPages` set is refused when it is loaded if `operatedBy` is not `AGENT`, if `pagesUrl` is not local, or if `pagesAddr` is `"off"`: the exception holds only while the engine serves the pages on a loopback address. While it is set, the warning `DEV_LOCAL_PAGES: these pages are on the agent's machine; never use this with a bot you do not control` is carried:

- by every `personMessage` the engine returns to the agent (tool errors, results, warnings and `status` problems, the `DEV_LOCAL_PAGES` problem's own included, once) as its first line, followed by an empty line, and not counted in its 500 characters, so every link the agent hands over comes with it;
- by `status` (the tool and `basalt status`) as the problem `DEV_LOCAL_PAGES` (`WARNING`, [Next steps](#next-steps)). Its one step is a `PERSON` `RUN_COMMAND` on the engine's machine even though `operatedBy` is `AGENT`: the exception exists only on the owner's own laptop, so the [agent-operated variants](#next-steps-on-an-agent-operated-engine) do not apply to it;
- on stderr by `basalt init` when it writes the key, and in the engine's log (level `WARN`) when it starts.

## Local pages

M4.1. The engine serves the approval and revert pages itself, so a held write reaches the person as a short link that works out of the box, and the page relays the signature without a pasted code. The signed data does not change: the page fetches the same request the long link carries, checks it the same way (binding, `rowsHash` or `sampleHash`), and the person signs the same binding; Postgres verifies the signature as before. The long-fragment links stay supported (hosted page, phone).

- **Listener.** `pagesAddr` in `engine.json`: a loopback `host:port`. Default: when the pages base URL is local (`http://localhost:<port>`, the default `http://localhost:8787`), `127.0.0.1:<port>`; for the portal, `off`. `"off"` disables it. A non-loopback address is refused when the config is loaded: this listener has no TLS or plaintext opt-in. If the port cannot be bound when the engine starts (another process holds it), the engine refuses to start, naming the address and how to find the process (`lsof -nP -iTCP:<port> -sTCP:LISTEN`); it never falls back to the long link silently. To run without the pages, set `pagesAddr` to `"off"`. The listener applies the `websec` Host check (loopback names only), Origin check and security headers.
- **Origin.** The page origin is the pages base URL (scheme, host, port), so the passkey's RP ID and origin are the ones it was registered with. Short links: `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#s=<secret>` and `<base>/reverts/<revertId>#s=<secret>` (see [Link secrets](#link-secrets)).
- **Assets.** The files under `web/` are embedded in the binary (`go:embed`); to try an edit to a page, rebuild the binary and restart the engine.

| Method and path | What |
|---|---|
| `GET /healthz` | `200 ok`; the CLI's probe |
| `GET /approvals/{approvalId}`, `GET /reverts/{revertId}` | The approval or revert page. A fragment `#1.<request>` is the long link: the page reads the request from it and checks its id equals the path's. A fragment `#s=<secret>` is the short link: the page reads the request from the engine, sending the secret in the `basalt-link-secret` header |
| `GET /passkeys/register` | The registration page (`basalt init`'s link). Its hand-back route, `POST /api/v1/passkeys/register`, is served only by `basalt init` while it waits ([CLI](#cli)), never by the engine |
| `GET /passkeys/check`, `GET /passkeys/forget` | The passkey management page (M4.1, [Passkey management](#passkey-management)); it reads its request from the fragment and refuses one whose mode is not its path's |
| `GET /lib/*.js`, `GET /style.css` | The pages' scripts and style; nothing else under `web/` is served |
| `GET /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}` | The approval request, the JSON the long link carries: [`approval-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/approval/approval-request.schema.json), with `columns` for display only (see below the table). Needs the link's secret |
| `POST /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}/approve` | Relay. Body: [`approval-response.schema.json`](api/mcp/approval/approval-response.schema.json), its `approvalId` equal to the path's. The engine calls `submit_approval` with the credential of the agent that holds the write. `200 { "approvalId", "status": "APPROVED", "receipt" }`, `noReceipt` in place of `receipt` when none can be made ([Receipts](#receipts)). Needs the link's secret |
| `GET /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}` | The revert request: [`revert-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-request.schema.json), with `columns` for display only (see below the table). Needs the link's secret |
| `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/apply` | Relay. Body: [`revert-response.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-response.schema.json). The engine calls `apply_revert` with the credential of the agent whose role prepared it. `200` with the result `basalt revert --apply --json` prints, plus `receipt` or `noReceipt` ([Receipts](#receipts)). Needs the link's secret |
| `POST /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}/links`, `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/links` | Issue a short link for the CLI (`basalt approve --show`/`--open`, `basalt revert`). Body `{ "proof" }`, see [Link secrets](#link-secrets). `200 { "url" }` with a new secret; the item's earlier link stops working |
| `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/scope` | RS-2, [Rescope, widen and reject](#rescope-widen-and-reject). Body: [`revert-scope-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-scope-request.schema.json), `{ "select", "proof"?, "newSecret"? }`. The engine calls `rescope_revert` with the credential of the role that prepared the revert. With a proof and `newSecret`: a widen; on success the engine rotates the revert's link to `newSecret` (the old one stops working) and answers `200` [`revert-scope-response.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-scope-response.schema.json) with the new request and the catalog. Without: a narrow, inside the proposal, or inside the ceiling when the engine holds the token for this link; `200` with the new request (and the catalog when under a token). Needs the link's secret. There is no `GET …/scope`: the catalog only comes back from a proven `POST` |
| `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/reject` | RS-2, [Rescope, widen and reject](#rescope-widen-and-reject). Body: [`revert-reject-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/revert/revert-reject-request.schema.json), `{ "note"? }`. The engine calls `reject_revert` with the preparing role's credential, adding the scope token when it holds one for this link (a widened revert), and revokes the link. `200 { "revertId", "status": "REJECTED", "rejectedAt" }`. Needs the link's secret |
| `POST /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}/reveal`, `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/reveal` | HR-1, [Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent). Body: [`reveal-request.schema.json`](api/mcp/approval/reveal-request.schema.json), `{ "proof", "revealSecret" }`; the engine derives the reveal key from the secret. The engine calls `open_reveal` then re-runs the held write in a `PREVIEW`, records it with `finish_change` and calls `reveal_approval_rows` (approval), or `reveal_revert_rows` (revert), with the credential of the agent that holds the item. `200` [`reveal-response.schema.json`](api/mcp/approval/reveal-response.schema.json) with `Cache-Control: no-store`. Needs the link's secret |

**Display-only fields.** The engine's `GET` answers for the pages (and the request in the scope route's answer) may carry fields that are never signed and never in a long link; the Go parsers and the pages refuse them in a link, even empty or `null`. They change how the page shows the rows, never which rows or values:

- `columns` (approvals and reverts): each table the lines name, spelled as the lines spell it, to its column names in table order, read by the agent's engine from `pg_attribute` (by `attnum`) as the agent role sees the catalog. Rows arrive as `jsonb`, which reorders their keys; the page orders each table's columns by this list, shows any column the rows carry that it does not list after them in the rows' order, and never hides one. A long link has no engine, so its page keeps the rows' order. Optional: an engine that cannot read the catalog leaves it out.

- **Which agent.** The engine looks the id up with each configured agent's credential (the ledger views show a role only its own approvals and reverts) and acts with the one that finds it. When none does: `404`. The secret is checked before the lookup, so a missing or wrong secret is answered the same as an unknown id.
- **Errors.** JSON `{ "code", "message" }` with a code from [Error codes](#error-codes): `NOT_FOUND` 404; `APPROVAL_EXPIRED` or `REVERT_EXPIRED` 410; `APPROVAL_INVALID` or `REVERT_INVALID` 409 (already used, already signed, or refused by Postgres, with its detail); `INVALID_ARGUMENT` 400 (a bad id or body, or ids that differ); 415 for a body that is not `application/json`; 413 over 16 KiB; 403 for a missing or foreign `Origin`. Planned ([Next steps](#next-steps)): the body also carries `personMessage` and the `PERSON` steps of the code's entry, which the page shows; a link without its secret or replaced is `NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK`. Revert scope (RS-2): a link revoked by a reset is `NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK_RESET`; `POST …/scope` follows the [check order](#rescope-widen-and-reject): a descriptor that fails on its shape alone (the schema, `store`, over 20 runs or tables) is `400 INVALID_ARGUMENT`; then anything that is not provably the agent's own work, and anything at all after a widen, without a valid proof or token is `409 REVERT_INVALID` with one fixed message, whatever the reason (a run or table of another agent, or unknown, included); only after that is a run, table or agent outside the scope `400 INVALID_ARGUMENT`; a rescope over 100 seconds is `504 QUERY_TIMEOUT` reason `TIME_LIMIT`; `POST …/scope` on a signed, rejected or expired revert answers as `apply` does (409 or 410), and so does `POST …/reject`. `GET` of a widened revert whose lines the engine no longer holds (it restarted since the widen) is `409 REVERT_INVALID` whose body also carries `rescopeNonce`, and the page asks the person to widen again with it. Postgres wins over the engine's memory: when the engine holds a scope token for the link but the revert is no longer widened with the binding it holds (the agent role reset it in Postgres directly), the engine drops what it held, revokes the link as a reset does, and answers `404 NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK_RESET`. A widen whose `newSecret` is the link's current secret is `400 INVALID_ARGUMENT`. `POST …/reveal` (HR-1): every proof, key, nonce or ticket failure is `409 APPROVAL_INVALID` or `409 REVERT_INVALID` with `reason` `REVEAL_REFUSED` and one fixed message; hidden rows that changed since the hold or the prepare are `409` with `reason` `ROWS_CHANGED`; an expired item is `410`, a used, signed or rejected one `409` as on `apply`; a guard older than [version 30](#version-30-reveal-hidden-approval-rows) (approvals) or [29](#version-29-reveal-hidden-revert-rows) (reverts) is `503 UNAVAILABLE` reason `GUARD_OUTDATED`. The engine serializes the reads, rescopes and resets of one revert, so it never serves an older rescope than Postgres stored.
- **CSRF.** A `POST` must carry an `Origin` equal to the page origin and `Content-Type: application/json`. The listener sends no CORS headers, so another site's page can neither read a preview nor make a JSON post. The relay only carries a signature: Postgres refuses anything the person's passkey did not sign.
- **Who can read a preview.** Only a holder of the short link (its secret), or of the long link, which carries the preview itself. The id alone is not enough: ids appear in tool results, logs and the CLI.
- **The page.** It shows the same review as the long link. After the passkey signs, it posts the response and shows "Approved. The agent will continue." (approval) or the revert result. If the relay fails, it shows the error and the code to paste into `basalt approve` or `basalt revert --apply`.

### Link secrets

Owner decisions, Decided Nov 12: short links carry a per-link secret.

- **The secret.** 16 random bytes (128 bits), base64url without padding (22 characters), in the fragment: `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#s=<secret>`. The fragment is never sent in an HTTP request, so the secret stays out of server and proxy logs and `Referer`; the page reads it from `location.hash` and sends it to the same-origin API in the `basalt-link-secret` header. A custom header also makes any cross-origin request need a CORS preflight, which this listener never grants.
- **Storage.** The engine keeps only `sha256(secret)` per held item, with the item's expiry (an approval after 24 hours, a revert after its hour), per agent in `<stateDir>/<agent>.links.json`: mode `0600`, owned by the engine's OS user, written atomically on every change and pruned of expired items. Short links survive an engine restart. It compares in constant time. A file it cannot read is logged and replaced: those links stop working and are issued again as below.
- **One live link per item.** Issuing a link for an item revokes its earlier one. Links are issued by: `write` when it holds a write (`approvalUrl`); `revert_plan` while the plan is `PENDING` and not widened; `request_revert` when it prepares a revert, and in its wait form only with `reissueLink: true`; `list_pending` only when called with `reissueLink: true` (then every item it returns gets a new link and the old one stops working; the agent hands the person the new one); the CLI through `POST …/links`, for `basalt revert` (a freshly prepared revert has no link yet) and for `basalt approve --show`/`--open` whenever the engine serves the pages (`--reissue` is accepted and means the same), which revokes the link the agent handed over; the agent still applies the write by its approval id. Without `reissueLink`, `list_pending` touches no link: `approvalUrl` is the long link and `personMessage` points back to the link already given (on an engine that serves no pages, the link the budget chooses, which over the budget is the hash link with `requestFile`: [The request file and pasted blocks](#the-request-file-and-pasted-blocks)). A waiting `write` that times out issues none and returns the hold's own answer, with the link it issued.
- **Issuing for the CLI.** `POST /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}/links` (and `/reverts/{revertId}/links`) with `{ "proof": "<hex>" }`, where `proof` = lowercase hex of `sha256(utf8("basalt-link.1\n" + binding text))`. Only a holder of the binding text can issue: a role that can read the item in `basalt_meta` (the CLI with the agent credential) or a holder of the long link, both of whom can already see the whole preview. The proof is not derivable from a signed response (whose challenge is `sha256(binding text)`). No `Origin` or a page-origin `Origin`, `Content-Type: application/json`; a wrong proof is answered as an unknown id.
- **Refusals.** A missing or wrong secret, or a link revoked by a newer one, gets `404 NOT_FOUND` with the same body as an unknown id, on both the request and the relay. The page then says the link is incomplete or replaced and how to get a new one (`list_pending`, `basalt approve --open`).
- **A widened revert** (RS-2 and RS-5, [Rescope, widen and reject](#rescope-widen-and-reject)). A proven widen rotates the revert's link to the page's `newSecret` and revokes the old one, as reissuing does: the link the agent handed over then reads `404 NOT_FOUND` (the same body as an unknown id) on `GET`, every relay route and a new Vault session. The engine stores the rotated link like any other (`sha256`, `linkToken`, `psk`, expiry). No route issues a link to a widened revert for the agent: `reissueLink: true` (`request_revert` and `list_pending`; planned for `revert_plan`) resets the revert to the agent's proposal through `reset_revert` and issues a link for that, and `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/links` on a widened revert is refused as an unknown id (its proof hashes a binding text the agent role can no longer read). The engine keeps the `sha256` of a link revoked by a reset until the revert expires and answers it `404 NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK_RESET` instead of `LINK`, so the page can say what happened (it keeps the hash in `<agent>.links.json` with the live links). A rejected revert's link is revoked: the page holding it then reads `404 NOT_FOUND`, and in a Vault session the engine sends the reject's answer first, then the session ends with `LINK_REFUSED`. Over the Vault a reset revokes the person's relay link like any reissue: its open sessions end with `LINK_REFUSED`.
- **Not affected.** The long-fragment links (`#2.<request>`, or `#1.` before it; the hosted form) carry the preview themselves and need no secret. The passkey pages, `/passkeys/register#1.<request>`, `/passkeys/check#1.<request>` and `/passkeys/forget#1.<request>`, are static: their whole request travels in the fragment, so the link is already the bearer of everything the page shows, and the engine has no data or relay endpoint for them to gate. They carry no `#s=` secret. A passkey page that ever reads from or posts to the engine gets a short link with a secret and gates its routes as above. The one exception is not the engine: the registration page of the pages `basalt init` serves itself posts the passkey back to init, with the `handBack` secret its request carries ([CLI](#cli), `basalt init`).
- **Over the Vault (M3.5).** On the portal the same short link opens an end-to-end encrypted session through the Vault: the engine also keeps the link's `linkToken` and `psk`, derived from the secret, and the secret travels only inside the session ([Vault channel](#vault-channel)).

### Handing a held action to the person

Every result that names a held action gives the agent what to show the person, so the person needs nothing else: `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` (`write`) and `list_pending` items carry `headline`, `approvalUrl`, `approveCommand` and `personMessage` (above). `revert_plan` for a plan not yet signed (`status` `PENDING`) and not widened carries `revertUrl` (the short link when the pages are served, else the long link, or the hash link when that is over the link budget) and `personMessage`; so does `request_revert` when it prepares a revert, or reissues its link. Wherever `approvalUrl` or `revertUrl` is a hash link, `requestFile` is beside it ([The request file and pasted blocks](#the-request-file-and-pasted-blocks)). An agent that lost a link calls `list_pending` again (for a revert, `request_revert` with `revertId` and `reissueLink: true`).

## Showing links

Planned (M3.5, Vault channel Phase 0, owner decision VC-7). Long links rarely fail on a hard length limit. They fail because chat and terminal clients format them: they hard-wrap the line, stop link detection at a line break, take a trailing `.` or `)` into the URL, or read `_` and `*` as emphasis. These rules keep a link whole from the engine to the person's browser. They apply to every link the engine, the CLI or a page hands over: approval, revert and passkey pages, short or long.

Terms: a **short link** carries `#s=<secret>` ([Link secrets](#link-secrets)); a **long link** carries the whole request, `#1.<request>` or `#2.<request>` ([Codes](#codes)); a **hash link** carries only a check of the request, `#h=<h>`, and the request travels beside it as a file or text blocks ([The request file and pasted blocks](#the-request-file-and-pasted-blocks)). Format `2.` is the compact request encoding (design section 3.2, item 1), laid out in [Format 2](#format-2-the-compact-request) (owner decision VC-29). The pages read both `1.` and `2.`; the engine emits `2.`.

### The link budget

- **`linkBudget`** in `engine.json`: the longest long link, in characters of the whole URL (base, path and fragment; every link is ASCII), the engine hands an agent as the way to open a page. A positive integer with no other minimum; default **1800** (owner decision VC-30). A value that is not a positive integer is refused when the config is loaded.
- **Short links are never measured.** They are about 110 characters plus the base, so they are always the first choice when one exists.
- **What the engine hands over** for a held write or a revert, in this order:
  1. A short link, when one exists: the engine's [local pages](#local-pages), or the portal's short link over the Vault (Phase 1a).
  2. Else the long link, when it is at most `linkBudget` characters.
  3. Else the long link is **over budget** and is not handed over. The engine hands over the hash link `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#h=<h>` (or `/reverts/<revertId>#h=<h>`), about 110 characters, with the request beside it as `requestFile` ([The request file and pasted blocks](#the-request-file-and-pasted-blocks); chat-only operator design, section 8.3, options B and C). This ends the interim hand-over of the over-budget long link (owner decision VC-28): no result hands over a long link over the budget (a result that only restates an item's link while the engine serves the pages keeps the long link in its field, below).
- **Fields.** `approvalUrl`, `revertUrl` and a step's `url` carry whichever link the engine handed over (short, long or hash). `requestFile` is added beside the link field exactly when it is a hash link. Revert links almost always go over: a revert carries up to 1000 lines.
- **Only MCP results are budgeted.** The CLI prints the link it has (with `--open` it opens it instead), and the pages show what they are given.

### The request file and pasted blocks

Chat-only operator design, section 8.3, options B (the request as a file) and C (the request as pasted text). When no short link exists and the long link is over the budget, the request reaches the page beside the link instead of inside it. Nothing about what is signed changes: the page decodes the same request the long link would carry and checks it the same way (its id against the path, `rowsHash` or `sampleHash` against the binding), the person signs the same binding, and Postgres recomputes the rows at apply and refuses any difference. `h` is a transfer check (a wrong, partial or altered file), not a trust anchor: the bot made both the link and the file.

- **The request text.** The long link's fragment without its `#`: `2.<request>` ([Format 2](#format-2-the-compact-request)). ASCII, with no whitespace.
- **`h`.** base64url, without padding, of the first 16 bytes of `sha256(utf8(request text))`: 22 characters. The hash link is `<base>/approvals/<approvalId>#h=<h>` or `<base>/reverts/<revertId>#h=<h>`. It is not a secret.
- **`requestFile`** ([`request-file.schema.json`](api/mcp/links/request-file.schema.json)), beside `approvalUrl` (in `approval` of `APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, and in a `list_pending` item) or `revertUrl` (`revert_plan`, `request_revert`, a `list_pending` revert item), present exactly when that link is a hash link:
  - `fileName`: `basalt-approval-<approvalId>.txt` or `basalt-revert-<revertId>.txt`.
  - `text`: the request text, the whole content of the file (no line break at its end).
  - `path` (optional): the absolute path of a file on the agent's machine that already holds exactly `text`, mode `0600`, owned by the agent's OS user, so the agent attaches it without copying `text` (a model that retypes tens of kilobytes of base64url gets one character wrong, and the page then refuses the file). `basalt mcp`, which runs as the agent's OS user, writes it under `<user cache dir>/basalt/requests/` (on Linux `$XDG_CACHE_HOME` or `~/.cache`, on macOS `~/Library/Caches`) as `fileName`, and adds `path` to the result it passes on; it removes files there older than 24 hours when it writes one. When it cannot write the file it passes the result on without `path`. The engine never sets it.
  - `blocks`: the request text cut into consecutive pieces of exactly **1800** characters (the last one 1 to 1800), in order, as numbered blocks the person can paste. Block `i` of `n` is the line `basalt-request <i>/<n> <h> <length>`, where `length` is the piece's length in characters, a line break (`\n`), and the piece. Present when `n` is at most **10**; absent above (a request over 18 000 characters goes as a file only: a 1000-line revert runs to tens of kilobytes). 1800 keeps a block under the smallest common chat message limit (Discord's 2000 characters) with its header.
- **What the agent does** (`AGENT` `SEND_REQUEST`, the first step whenever `requestFile` is present): send the person the file as an attachment in the chat: the file at `path` when it is set, else a file named `fileName` holding exactly `text`. When it cannot send files and `blocks` is present, send each block unchanged in a code block, as a message of its own, in order (outside a code block, a chat's Markdown can read `_..._` in a piece as emphasis and drop the underscores; the page reads through the fence). When it can send neither (no files, and `blocks` absent), it does not show the link: it tells the person the request is too large to paste in this chat and asks how to go on (for a revert, a narrower one with `runs` or `tables`, or `basalt` on a machine that serves the pages). Otherwise it then shows `personMessage` as usual. It skips the send when it already sent that item's file or blocks in this conversation. The step's `text` says all of this; it names no file content.
- **`personMessage`.** Says the request comes as a file or text blocks from the agent, before the link line: "Approve it with your passkey: open this link, then open the request file the agent sends you (or paste its text blocks). The page then shows a code for the agent:" (for a revert, "Sign it with your passkey: ..."), then the hash link on its own line, then the command when the result has one and the expiry. When `blocks` is absent it names only the file.
- **The page** (approval and revert). A fragment `#h=<h>` shows a box with two ways to give it the request; the rest of the page waits for it:
  1. **Open the file**: a file picker (`<input type="file">`, any type). The page reads the file locally (it is never uploaded) as UTF-8, removes a leading byte order mark (U+FEFF) and every `White_Space` character, and refuses a file over **4 MiB** before reading it.
  2. **Paste the text blocks**: a text box that takes one or more blocks per paste, in any order, over several pastes. The page reads the paste line by line (`\r\n`, `\n`, `\r` or U+2028), each line with every `>`, `*`, `\` and `` ` `` removed and `White_Space` trimmed (so a code fence on the same line as a header or a piece is dropped).
