A header is a line that is `basalt-request <i>/<n> <h> <length>` (with `1 <= i <= n <= 10` and `1 <= length <= 1800`), or ends with it after other text and a space (a chat's sender name). The lines after a header make its piece: each line is taken while it is not empty, holds only base64url characters and `.`, and is not a header, until the piece has `length` characters; the first line that is not ends it, and characters past `length` are dropped. A block whose piece has fewer than `length` characters is refused as cut short. So sender names, timestamps and prose a chat app puts between or after copied messages are never read into a piece. A block whose `h` differs from the link's, or whose `n` differs from the first block's, is refused with a message saying it belongs to another request; a repeated `i` replaces the earlier one. The page shows how many of `n` blocks it has and which are missing, and joins the pieces in order once it has all of them.

  Either way, when the text's `h` differs from the link's the page says the file or blocks do not match this link, loads nothing and lets the person try again (a wrong file, a cut one, or blocks a chat app altered). When it matches, the page decodes the text as the fragment `#<text>` of a long link, so format `1.` reads too, though the engine writes only `2.`: every check of a long link applies, including that the request's id is the path's.
- **A result that restates a link** without handing over a new one, while the engine serves the pages (`list_pending` without `reissueLink`, a wait that ran out): the person already has the short link, so the link field keeps the long link as before, `personMessage` points back to the link already given, its `OPEN_LINK` step carries no `url` (the person opens the short link they have), and there is no `requestFile` and no `SEND_REQUEST`. On an engine that serves no pages the hash link is the one the person has: `list_pending` without `reissueLink`, and `write`'s wait that ran out (it returns the same approval body), carry it with `requestFile` like any other, and the agent skips a file it already sent. `request_revert`'s wait that ran out follows its own rules (`request_revert`, Wait, under [MCP tools](#mcp-tools)): the link on its `OPEN_LINK` step only, with no `revertUrl` and no `requestFile`. A refusal that sends the person back to sign (`APPROVAL_INVALID` `NOT_SIGNED`, `BAD_SIGNATURE`, `PASSKEY_REVOKED`; `REVERT_INVALID` `BAD_SIGNATURE`, `PASSKEY_REVOKED`) has no link field: it restates the link the person has, never a long link over the budget, with no `requestFile` and no `SEND_REQUEST` (the agent sent the file with the request). While the engine serves the pages its `OPEN_LINK` step carries no `url`. Without them the step carries the link the hold or the request handed over, and an `APPROVAL_INVALID`'s `personMessage` and `links` show that link too (naming the request file already sent for a hash link). A `REVERT_INVALID`'s `personMessage` names no link.
- **When the page refuses the blocks** (the person says so), the agent sends them once more, each in a code block; when they fail again, it follows the "neither" path above.
- **The CLI** is unchanged: it prints or opens the link it has, never a hash link ([The link budget](#the-link-budget): only MCP results are budgeted).
- **Shared vectors** ([`request-file.json`](api/mcp/links/request-file.json)): request texts with their `h`, hash link, file name and blocks, and pasted texts with the request the page rebuilds from them (or the refusal). The contract test checks them against these rules; the engine and the pages run them.

### Links in `personMessage`

Every `personMessage` follows these rules. They replace "one plain line" in [Next steps](#next-steps).

- **Lines.** Plain text in lines separated by `\n` (never `\r`).
- **Each URL is a paragraph of its own.** Its line holds the URL and nothing else: no label, no leading or trailing space, and nothing after it (no `.`, `,`, `;`, `:`, `)`, `>`, quote or backtick). The line before it and the line after it, when there is one, are empty. So the URL ends at the end of its line in plain text, and stands alone in Markdown.
- **No Markdown.** The engine never writes `[label](url)`, `<url>`, a code span or emphasis around a URL, and never escapes characters in it. A client that renders Markdown uses `links` (below).
- **Text from the agent or the database** (an intent, a table name) never reads as a link. It is one line with no control or format character; full-width forms and lookalike slashes and dots (U+2215, U+2044, U+29F8, U+3002 and the like) fold to ASCII; scheme separators (`//`, `\\`), `www.` and link fragment prefixes are removed; a dot between two labels of a host name becomes a middle dot (`evil.io` shows as `evil·io`, `public.leads` as `public·leads`), so a chat app does not link a bare host; so do the dots of a four-part dotted number (`10.0.0.1` shows as `10·0·0·1`); a port gets a space before its colon when a path follows it or a letter precedes it (`localhost:8080/x` shows as `localhost :8080/x`); and `](` becomes `] (`, so it is no Markdown link.
- **Length.** At most 500 characters, not counting URL lines (the URL and its line break). URLs are bounded by the link budget, not by this limit. JSON Schema cannot express this, so the schemas carry no `maxLength` for `personMessage`; an engine test checks every message the engine builds (with the engine change that applies the budget). When any message offers a command (`basalt approve --agent <name> --config <path> ...`, `basalt revert ...`, `basalt access ...`) and would go over the limit because `--config` is a long path, the message says "the command the agent shows you" in its place (a held write's: "Or run the approve command the agent shows you."); the full command stays in the `PERSON` `RUN_COMMAND` step, and for a held write also in `approval.approveCommand`.
- **`links`.** Next to every `personMessage`, and present exactly when that `personMessage` holds a URL: an array of `{ "label", "url" }` ([`link.schema.json`](api/mcp/links/link.schema.json)), one entry per URL in the order they appear, each `url` byte for byte the URL in `personMessage`. `url` follows the URL rule of [Terminal hyperlinks](#terminal-hyperlinks): `https`, or `http` for a loopback host, and only RFC 3986 characters. `label` says what opening the link does, in plain text of 1 to 80 characters with no URL, no control character (C0, DEL or C1, U+0080 to U+009F), no line break, no bidirectional control (U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069), and none of `[`, `]`, `(`, `)`, `<`, `>`, `` ` ``, `*`, `_` (`Approve the held write`, `Review and sign the revert`). A client that renders Markdown may show `[label](url)`.

For example (`\n` shown as line breaks):

```text
Held for your approval: Update 27 rows in basalt_demo·leads.
Approve it with your passkey here:

http://localhost:8787/approvals/0193a1b2-7c4d-7e8f-9a0b-1c2d3e4f5a6b#s=q3Jv8sLk2mT0xYp1aBcD9w

Or run: basalt approve --agent demo_bot --open 0193a1b2-7c4d-7e8f-9a0b-1c2d3e4f5a6b
Expires 2026-11-12 07:02 UTC.
```

with `links: [{ "label": "Approve the held write", "url": "http://localhost:8787/approvals/0193a1b2-7c4d-7e8f-9a0b-1c2d3e4f5a6b#s=q3Jv8sLk2mT0xYp1aBcD9w" }]`.

A `PERSON` step's `url` is shown under the same rules: on its own line, unchanged.

### What the agent is told

The engine's MCP `initialize` result carries, in its `instructions`, this text unchanged:

> When you show the person a link from Basalt, put it on its own line exactly as Basalt gave it, or as a Markdown link [label](url) with the label from links. Never put a link in a code block or inline code, never split, wrap, shorten, encode or rewrite it, and put nothing after it on its line. Show personMessage verbatim and keep its line breaks.

The tool descriptions say the same where they mention `personMessage`.

It also carries the rule that keeps the admin credential away from the agent ([Agents and the admin credential](#agents-and-the-admin-credential)), unchanged:

> Never ask the person for, or accept, their database admin connection string or password, and never run basalt init without --remote yourself: to set up or change your access, follow nextSteps or run basalt init --remote and give the person the setup code it prints. If the person pastes an admin password, tell them to change it now.

It also carries one sentence that points the agent to the public docs, unchanged:

> For error codes and troubleshooting, read https://docs.basaltvault.app/llms.txt (each page is also Markdown at its path with .md in place of the trailing slash, e.g. https://docs.basaltvault.app/reference/errors.md).

### The pages repair what they can

Before decoding, the approval, revert and passkey pages repair the fragment (`location.hash`):

1. Take the text after the first `#`.
2. Remove every `%20`, `%09`, `%0A` and `%0D` (hex digits in either case).
3. Remove every whitespace character (Unicode `White_Space`: spaces, tabs, line breaks, U+00A0, U+2028 and the rest; in JavaScript `/\p{White_Space}/u`, not `\s`, which differs) and every `>`, `*` and `\`.
4. When the text starts with `1.` or `2.`, keep that prefix and the base64url characters (`A` to `Z`, `a` to `z`, `0` to `9`, `-`, `_`) right after it, up to the first other character; drop the rest (a trailing `.`, `)`, quote or text). When it starts with `s=` or `h=`, keep that prefix and the 22 characters after it when all 22 are base64url. Anything else is left as it is and fails to decode.

`_` and `-` are never removed: they are base64url. Repair never makes a wrong request pass: the page still checks the request's id against the path and `rowsHash` (or `sampleHash`) against the binding, and refuses to sign otherwise.

When the repaired fragment still does not decode, or is missing, the page shows a box, "Paste the whole message from your agent", and reads the link out of the pasted text:

1. Split the text into lines (`\r\n`, `\n`, `\r` or U+2028). In each line remove every `>`, `*` and `\`, then trim leading and trailing `White_Space`.
2. Find the first line holding `#1.`, `#2.`, `#s=` or `#h=`. Take the prefix and the base64url characters right after it on that line.
3. When they reach the end of the line, append each following line while it is not empty and holds only base64url characters; stop at the first line that does not. A short link or a hash link stops at 22 characters.
4. Decode the result as a repaired fragment (above). The empty line the engine puts after every URL is what ends a wrapped link.

**Only the fragment is taken from the paste.** The page keeps its own origin and path: it uses the pasted text only for the fragment, ignores the scheme, host and path in front of it, never navigates to a pasted URL and never fetches from one. For a long link the decoded request must still carry the id of the page's own path. A short link (`s=`) carries no request and no id: the page sends the pasted secret with its own path's id, and the engine checks the secret against that id, so a secret pasted from another item's link is answered `NOT_FOUND`, as a wrong secret is.

This covers terminal clients that hard-wrap and indent a long link: the person copies the whole block. Shared cases in [`api/mcp/links/link-repair.json`](api/mcp/links/link-repair.json) pin each rule (`fragments`: a fragment and its repair; `pastes`: a pasted message and the fragment read from it, or `null` when there is none); the contract test checks them against these rules, and the pages' tests run them.

### Terminal hyperlinks

The CLI prints every link on its own line, with nothing else on it.

- **The URL rule.** Every link the CLI prints, and every pages base it builds one from, is `https://` followed only by RFC 3986 characters (`A` to `Z`, `a` to `z`, `0` to `9` and `-._~:/?#[]@!$&'()*+,;=%`), or `http://` for a loopback host (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`) followed by an optional port and the same characters. A pages base is narrower: its local form is `localhost` only ([Page origins](#page-origins)). [`link.schema.json`](api/mcp/links/link.schema.json) carries the same rule as `url`'s pattern. Every byte of such a URL is between `0x21` and `0x7E`.
- **Bases are checked when read.** `pagesUrl` in `engine.json` and `--pages-url` must follow the URL rule, with no query or fragment. A config that breaks it is refused when it is loaded, and a flag that breaks it is a usage error (exit `2`). Either way the message names the setting and the first bad character's position, never echoes the raw byte, and says what to do: set it to an `https://` URL, or `http://localhost:<port>`, of printable characters. A base that follows the rule must also be in the closed list of [Page origins](#page-origins).
- **Never a control byte on a terminal (refuse, not escape).** The CLI never writes a byte below `0x20` or between `0x7F` and `0x9F` (as a byte or as the UTF-8 encoding of U+0080 to U+009F) to a terminal as part of a link. A URL that breaks the URL rule anyway (from an older config, or from the database) is not printed: the CLI exits `1` with the same message, naming the item's id and the command to print its link again after the fix. The CLI's tests cover this: a base with `ESC`, `BEL`, `\n`, `0x7F` and U+009B in it is refused at load and at `--pages-url`, and a URL that breaks the rule at print time writes no part of the URL and no control byte to the terminal.
- **Hyperlinks.** When stdout is a terminal (and `TERM` is not `dumb`), the CLI prints the link as an OSC 8 hyperlink whose visible text is the whole URL: `ESC ] 8 ; ; <url> ESC \` `<url>` `ESC ] 8 ; ; ESC \`. The URL rule keeps the URL from ending the escape sequence or starting another. A terminal that supports OSC 8 makes the whole link clickable however it wraps; one that does not shows the plain URL. Otherwise (a pipe, a file, `--json`), the plain URL.
- **The stream.** The terminal check is made on the stream the link is written to (stdout for a link on stdout, stderr for one the CLI writes to stderr), so a link is never an escape sequence in a pipe or file.

## Vault channel

Planned (M3.5, Vault channel Phase 1a; owner decisions VC-1 to VC-42). An engine with no inbound port reaches the person's browser through the hosted Vault (`relay.basaltvault.app`, VC-20). The engine dials out, the portal page dials in, and the two run an end-to-end encrypted session keyed by the short link's secret, inside which the page speaks the same pages API the engine serves on loopback. The Vault pairs sockets and forwards opaque bytes.

**The Vault is untrusted.** Neither the engine nor the page depends on it being honest. A Vault that drops, delays, duplicates, reorders, replays, truncates, reflects or alters any message can make a session fail, never make either side accept something. It never holds a key that opens a session (VC-4). The Vault service is closed source (VC-13, VC-18): this section is the whole protocol it must speak, and its internals (hosting, storage, its own rate numbers) are not part of the contract. Schemas, the close-code registry and the vector format: [`api/mcp/vault/`](api/mcp/vault/).

**Scope (VC-1, VC-17).** Link sessions only, for the approval and revert pages. Pairing, box keys in use, envelopes, device codes, the pending list, notifications and accounts are after M4 (their contract is VC2-1's). The direct transport (`DIRECT`, M4.1) runs this same session over the person's own tunnel; its route, the pinned engine address and revocation of an engine in `basalt_meta` are in [Direct transport](#direct-transport) (VCT-1).

**The exception (VC-1).** "No hosted service before M4" has one narrow exception: this rendezvous. It stores no plaintext, no credentials and no ledger, has no accounts, and approvals work when it is down (long links, below).

### Primitives and encodings

- **Algorithms (VC-5).** P-256 (ECDH and ECDSA), SHA-256, HMAC-SHA-256, HKDF-SHA-256 (RFC 5869) and AES-256-GCM: all in WebCrypto and in Go's standard library (`crypto/ecdh`, `crypto/ecdsa`, `crypto/hkdf`, `crypto/hmac`, `crypto/cipher`). No third-party crypto in the engine or the pages, and no primitive of our own: the handshake is a Noise pattern and every other step is a signature, an HKDF or an AEAD call.
- **Public keys.** In a handshake message: the 65-byte uncompressed SEC1 point (`0x04 ‖ X ‖ Y`). As an identity: the 91-byte DER SubjectPublicKeyInfo (`id-ecPublicKey`, `prime256v1`, uncompressed point), base64url without padding (122 characters). Any other encoding (a compressed point, explicit curve parameters, trailing bytes) and any point not on the curve is refused.
- **ECDH output** is the 32-byte x-coordinate (WebCrypto `deriveBits` with 256 bits; Go `ecdh.PrivateKey.ECDH`).
- **Signatures** are ECDSA P-256 with SHA-256, encoded as 64 bytes `r ‖ s` (IEEE P1363, WebCrypto's form; Go converts from ASN.1), base64url without padding (86 characters). A verifier refuses any other length. A high-S signature (`s` above half the group order) is **accepted**: verifiers use plain ECDSA verification (WebCrypto `verify`, Go `ecdsa.Verify`), which accepts both `s` and `n − s`, and nothing uses a signature's bytes as an identifier, so the second form gains nothing.
- **Bytes** in JSON are base64url without padding unless a field says otherwise; in test vectors, lowercase hex.
- **Signing contexts.** The engine's signing key signs only these, each input starting with its context line, so no signature made for one can be used as another (VC-37). A new context is a contract change.

| Context line | Signed by | Over |
|---|---|---|
| `basalt-vault-engine.1\n` | Engine signing key | The Vault's challenge and the engine's Vault host ([engine socket](#the-engines-socket-to-the-vault)) |
| `basalt-vault-session-sig.1\n` | Engine signing key | The handshake hash `h` after `e, ee` ([Handshake](#handshake)) |
| `basalt-receipt.1\n` | Engine signing key | A receipt's payload bytes ([Receipts](#receipts)) |
| `basalt-engine-box-key.1\n` | Engine signing key | The engine id and the box key's public key |
| `basalt-engine-rotate.1\n` | The previous signing key | The new keys ([Engine keys](#engine-keys), rotation) |

### Engine keys

- **Two keys (VC-5).** A **signing key** (ECDSA P-256) and a **box key** (ECDH P-256, used from Phase 1b; made now so the key file keeps its shape). Neither does the other's job. The box key's public key is signed by the signing key (`basalt-engine-box-key.1\n` + `engineId` + `\n` + the box key's SPKI as its base64url text, as `channel.json` holds it).
- **Engine id** = base64url of the first 16 bytes of `sha256(signing key SPKI DER)`: 22 characters. It is self-certifying (anyone holding the key can check it; nobody can claim another engine's id) and a key fingerprint, not a UUIDv7. **Shown to people** as its first 16 characters in four groups of four, separated by spaces (`AbCd EfGh IjKl MnOp`), next to the agent name: in `status`, `basalt status`, and on every portal page with a session, so the person can compare.
- **When made (VC-36).** On the engine's first start, whatever `vault` says: receipts need the signing key on every path, the local pages and the CLI included. A missing file is created.
- **Storage.** `<stateDir>/channel.json` ([`channel-key-file.schema.json`](api/mcp/vault/channel-key-file.schema.json)): mode `0600`, owned by the engine's OS user (as `<agent>.links.json` and the credential file), written atomically (a temporary file in the same directory, `fsync`, rename). Private keys are PKCS#8 DER. No key material goes anywhere else: not to logs, `engine.json`, `status`, the ledger or the Vault. No OS keychain (as M51-1).
- **Refused, never replaced (VC-36).** A key file that grants group or other access, is owned by another user, cannot be read, fails its schema, whose `engineId` is not its signing key's or whose `boxKeySig` does not verify is left as it is. The engine then serves MCP and the local pages as usual, does not connect to the Vault, hands over long links instead of portal short links, signs no receipt (the result says why), and `status` reports `vault.state` `KEY_ERROR`. The fix is to correct the file's owner and mode, or `basalt engine rotate-key`. (The links file is replaced instead, because a new link costs nothing; new keys change the engine id the person may have compared, and from M4.1 pinned.)
- **Who can read it.** Whoever can read the agent's credential file: in same-user mode (`GUARD_ONLY`) and with `operatedBy` `AGENT`, the agent. A leaked key lets its holder pose as the engine to the Vault and sign receipts in its name. It reads no session without that session's link secret and approves nothing: only the person's passkey signs, and Postgres verifies it.
- **Rotation (VC-40).** `basalt engine rotate-key [--config PATH] [--yes]` makes both keys again (a new engine id). When the old file is valid, it signs `basalt-engine-rotate.1\n` + old `engineId` + `\n` + new signing key SPKI + `\n` + new box key SPKI + `\n` + `at` (each SPKI as its base64url text) with the old key and keeps it as `rotatedFrom` (the latest rotation only; the old private key is deleted). Then it revokes every outstanding short link of every agent (their `psk` sat next to the old key), unregisters their tokens, closes open sessions with `ENGINE_SHUTDOWN` and reconnects under the new id. An agent gets new links with `list_pending` and `reissueLink: true`. Rotation is never automatic. A `KEY_ERROR` file is rotated without a statement.
  - `rotatedFrom` proves only that whoever held the old key agreed to the new one. After a leak the thief holds the old key too and can sign a statement of their own, so a Phase 1b device must not trust a new key on `rotatedFrom` alone: it also needs the person's own check (the fingerprint `status` shows) and, from M4.1, the engine key recorded in `basalt_meta` and not revoked.
  - The old key is not dead at the Vault: whoever holds it can still authenticate as the old engine id, and revoking the key in `basalt_meta` (M4.1) does not change that, since the Vault never reads the person's database. It gives them the old id's routing only: the old id's links were revoked, and no new link uses it. What revocation stops is the honest engine and the person's trust: the portal pin and the record in `basalt_meta` ([Direct transport](#direct-transport)).
- **Recorded and revoked by the person (M4.1, VC-21).** Laptop init records the signing key in the person's database with the pinned address ([Version 33](#version-33-engine-keys-and-the-pinned-address)); `basalt engine revoke` revokes it. What a revoked engine does, per agent database: [Direct transport](#direct-transport), Revocation by the person. In short, today: it reports the revoke in `status`, and on `DIRECT` stops handing over direct short links for that agent and closes and refuses its sessions with `ENGINE_REVOKED`. The relay half (revoking short links, unregistering tokens, leaving the Vault) is planned, see the Planned paragraph there.

### Link secrets in a session

The short link is unchanged ([Link secrets](#link-secrets)): `https://portal.basaltvault.app/approvals/<approvalId>#s=<secret>` and `…/reverts/<revertId>#s=<secret>`. The page tells the transport by its own origin: same-origin `fetch` on the engine's local pages, the Vault on the portal (VC-25: the portal never calls `localhost`). With `s` the 16 bytes the 22-character secret decodes to (VC-9):

- `linkToken` = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm `s`, salt empty, info `basalt-vault-link.1`, 16 bytes), base64url: 22 characters. The Vault's routing key for the link.
- `psk` = HKDF-SHA-256(ikm `s`, salt empty, info `basalt-vault-psk.1`, 32 bytes): the session's pre-shared key.

Neither reveals `s` and neither gives the other. The engine keeps `linkToken` and `psk` per item in `<agent>.links.json` next to `sha256(secret)`, with the same expiry, the same one-live-link rule, and constant-time comparison. The page derives both from `location.hash` and never sends `s` or `psk` to the Vault: the socket path carries `linkToken` only, and the secret itself travels only inside the encrypted session, as `basalt-link-secret`.

### Handshake

`Noise_NNpsk0_P256_AESGCM_SHA256`, from the Noise Protocol Framework (revision 34), with an engine signature in the second payload (VC-32; design 6.1). The page is the initiator, the engine the responder.

- **Functions.** `DH`: P-256. Noise's `DHLEN` is the length of both a public key and a DH output; here they differ: an `e` token writes, reads and hashes the 65-byte uncompressed point, and `DH` returns the 32-byte x-coordinate (both meet Noise's minimum of 32). `HASH`: SHA-256. `HKDF`: Noise's (HMAC-SHA-256; the same as RFC 5869 with empty info, and implemented with HMAC so a zero-length input key works in WebCrypto). Cipher: AES-256-GCM with Noise's nonce, 4 zero bytes followed by the 64-bit big-endian counter. The protocol name is 31 bytes, so `h` starts as the name padded with zero bytes to 32, and `ck` = `h`. Because the pattern has a `psk` token (Noise section 9.2), every `e` token is followed by `MixKey(e.public_key)` as well as `MixHash(e.public_key)`, on both sides and for both ephemeral keys. A Noise message, which here is everything after a message's 2-byte header (and, in message 1, the 16-byte `linkToken`), is at most 65 535 bytes.
- **Prologue** = UTF-8 of `basalt-vault-prologue.1\n` + transport (`RELAY` or `DIRECT`) + `\n` + `linkToken` as its 22-character base64url text (not the raw bytes), with nothing after it. Each side builds it from what it knows itself (the page from its transport and its link; the engine from the socket the session came on and the token it looked up), so a session moved to another link or transport fails.
- **Message 1, page to engine** (`psk, e`): exactly 99 bytes, `0x01` (version) ‖ `0x01` (type `HELLO`) ‖ `linkToken` (16 raw bytes) ‖ `e` (65 bytes) ‖ `EncryptAndHash` of the empty payload (the 16-byte tag). The payload is always zero bytes: `EncryptAndHash` of it is the tag alone, and the fixed length refuses anything else. `linkToken` travels here as its 16 raw bytes. With `psk0`, that tag is under a key derived from `psk`: the page proves it holds the link before the engine does any public-key work.
- **The engine on message 1**, in order: the version byte (`UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`), then the type and the exact length (`PROTOCOL`); look `linkToken` up (on `RELAY` it must equal `SESSION_OPEN`'s); the [limits](#limits); check the tag (the `e` token hashes and mixes the 65 bytes as they are, so this needs no point arithmetic); only then check `e` is a valid point. A token it does not hold, a link expired or revoked, a different `SESSION_OPEN` token, a failing tag and an invalid point all close with `LINK_REFUSED`, alike, as a wrong secret answers today, so a probe cannot tell a live token from a dead one by the answer.
- **Message 2, engine to page** (`e, ee`): `0x01` ‖ `0x02` (type `WELCOME`) ‖ `e` (65 bytes) ‖ `EncryptAndHash` of the payload, at most 1 024 bytes in all. The payload is UTF-8 JSON, [`handshake-payload.schema.json`](api/mcp/vault/handshake-payload.schema.json): `{ "engineKey": <SPKI, base64url>, "sig": <base64url> }`, where `sig` signs `basalt-vault-session-sig.1\n` followed by the 32 raw bytes of `h` after the `e` and `ee` tokens (before the payload is hashed in).
- **The page on message 2.** Check the version byte (`UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`), the type (`PROTOCOL`) and the size (`TOO_LARGE`), then that `e` is a valid point, decrypt, parse the closed schema, verify `sig` over the same `h` with `engineKey`, and compute the engine id from `engineKey`. On `DIRECT`, compare it with the pinned key (VCT-1). Any failure closes with `HANDSHAKE_FAILED` and shows nothing from the engine.
- **Engine identity on `RELAY` is not pinned (VC-42).** The link carries no engine id and the page keeps nothing between sessions, so on `RELAY` the page accepts whatever key a holder of the link's `psk` signs with. The engine's identity there is trust on first use, checked only by the person comparing the fingerprint the page shows with the one `status`, `basalt status` or the agent's earlier messages gave. Only someone who holds the link secret (the engine itself, or whoever saw the link) and gets the Vault to route the token to their own engine (a malicious Vault, or the registration race in [The engine's socket to the Vault](#the-engines-socket-to-the-vault)) can present another key. What that gains them: a fake fingerprint on the page, fake receipts in the page, and denial of service (taking the person's signature and not relaying it). Not a forged approval: the page builds the binding from the rows it shows and checks `rowsHash`, and Postgres accepts a signature only over a real pending item's binding, so the most such an engine can obtain is the person's signature over the real item they were shown, which approves only that item. Reading the preview is no gain: the link already lets its holder read it. On `DIRECT` the key is pinned (VCT-1).
- **Split.** `Split()` gives `k1` (page to engine) and `k2` (engine to page), each with its own counter from 0. Both sides erase the ephemeral private keys and `ck`. The final `h` may be logged as the session id; keys never are.

What this gives against the Vault and anyone on the path:

- **Confidentiality and integrity.** Every key depends on `psk` (from the link secret, which the Vault never sees) and on the ephemeral-ephemeral DH. The Vault cannot read or change a session or pretend to be either side.
- **Authentication by the link.** Only holders of `s` (the engine that issued the link, and whoever holds the link) can make message 1's tag or a message 2 the page accepts. The signature binds the session to the engine's identity key, for the fingerprint on the page, receipts and `DIRECT` pinning; on `RELAY` nothing but the person's comparison checks that key (above).
- **Transcript binding.** `h` covers the prologue (version, transport, `linkToken`), both ephemeral keys and both payloads; the signature covers `h`; `Split` derives from the whole chain.
- **Forward secrecy.** A later leak of the link secret, the links file or the engine's key does not open a recorded session.
- **Replay.** A replayed message 1 meets a fresh engine ephemeral key and leads nowhere: the replayer lacks the page's ephemeral private key, so it can neither read message 2 nor send a frame. A replayed message 2 fails against the page's fresh key. Within a session, implicit counters refuse replays ([Frames](#frames)); across sessions, Postgres refuses a used approval or revert ([Replay and expiry](#replay-and-expiry)).

### Frames

After the handshake, every binary WebSocket message either way is one frame: `0x01` (version) ‖ `0x03` (type `FRAME`) ‖ AES-256-GCM ciphertext, made with Noise's `EncryptWithAd` under the direction's key and counter, with the two header bytes as associated data (VC-33). The nonce is the receiver's own count, never sent, so a frame dropped, repeated, reordered, reflected to its sender or taken from another session does not decrypt: the receiver closes with `FRAME_INVALID` and drops the session. There is no rekey; the limits end a session long before a counter could wrap. A text message, or a binary message with another header, closes with `PROTOCOL` (an unknown version with `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION`). A receiver checks a frame in this order: its size ([limits](#limits)), the version byte, the type, then decrypts and unpads.

**Padding (VC-34; design 6.2).** The plaintext is `len` (4 bytes, big-endian) ‖ content (`len` bytes) ‖ zero bytes up to `max(1024, the next power of two ≥ 4 + len)` bytes, at most 32 768 (content at most 32 764 bytes per frame, so every Noise message stays under Noise's 65 535-byte limit). A receiver refuses (`FRAME_INVALID`) a plaintext of any other size for its `len`, or with a non-zero padding byte. Sizes on the wire are then 1 024, 2 048, … 32 768 bytes plus 18: one frame leaks a bucket, not a row count. What a whole session still leaks is in [What the Vault sees](#what-the-vault-sees).

**Content** is UTF-8 JSON, one of [`frame-message.schema.json`](api/mcp/vault/frame-message.schema.json):

- `REQUEST`, page to engine: `{ "type", "id", "method", "path", "headers", "body", "more" }`.
- `RESPONSE`, engine to page: `{ "type", "id", "status", "headers", "body", "more" }`.
- `CONTINUE`, either way: `{ "type", "id", "body", "more" }`, the next piece of a body.

`body` is text (the pages API speaks JSON). A body too large for one frame continues in `CONTINUE` frames, appended in order up to the one with `more: false`. A message is acted on only once that frame arrives, so a truncated message is never used: the page times out and shows nothing. `id` is 1 for a session's first request and one more for each next one; a response and its continuations carry the request's `id`. One request is in flight at a time: a `REQUEST` before the previous response ended closes with `PROTOCOL`.

**Requests.**

- **Routes.** Only these are dispatched; any other method or path is answered `404 NOT_FOUND` with the same body as an unknown id:
  - `GET /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}` and `POST /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}/approve`;
  - `GET /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}` and `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/apply`;
  - `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/scope` and `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/reject` (revert scope, RS-2, [Local pages](#local-pages); a reject revokes the revert's link, so its session ends with `LINK_REFUSED` once the answer went out);
  - HR-1, [Rows hidden from the agent](#rows-hidden-from-the-agent): `POST /api/v1/approvals/{approvalId}/reveal` and `POST /api/v1/reverts/{revertId}/reveal`;
  - reserved, each when its own contract lands: the passkey check and forget routes (a passkey page that reads from the engine gets a short link, [Link secrets](#link-secrets)), and after M4 (VC-27) `GET /api/v1/setups/{setupId}` and `POST /api/v1/setups/{setupId}/done`.

  Never the `…/links` issuing routes, `/healthz`, the pages or their scripts.
- **One item per session.** The path's id must be the item of the session's link, and `basalt-link-secret` must be that link's secret (its `sha256` and its `linkToken` both match). Otherwise `404 NOT_FOUND`. Revert scope: when a widen in the session rotates the revert's link, the session stays open under its keys and the revert keeps its id, so one item per session holds; its later requests must carry the new secret (`newSecret`, whose `sha256` the engine now holds for the item), and the old one is answered `404 NOT_FOUND`. The engine moves the session to the new `linkToken`, ends every other session of the old one with `LINK_REFUSED`, and registers the new one, so a session opened with the new secret works and one opened with the old secret is refused by the engine at message 1 (`LINK_REFUSED`). It does not send `UNREGISTER_LINK` for the old token, which would close the rotating session's page socket; the Vault drops the old token at its `expiresAt`.
- **Headers**, lowercase. A request carries `basalt-link-secret` and `origin` (`https://portal.basaltvault.app`), and on `POST` `content-type: application/json`. A response carries `content-type`. Any other header in a request is answered `400 INVALID_ARGUMENT`.
- **Dispatch.** The engine hands the request to the same handlers as on loopback, with the same checks, statuses and JSON bodies ([Local pages](#local-pages)): `NOT_FOUND` 404, `APPROVAL_EXPIRED` or `REVERT_EXPIRED` 410, `APPROVAL_INVALID` or `REVERT_INVALID` 409, `INVALID_ARGUMENT` 400, 413 over 16 KiB, 415, 403 for a wrong `origin`, and `QUERY_TIMEOUT` reason `TIME_LIMIT` 504 for a rescope over 100 seconds. The `approve` and `apply` responses also carry `receipt`, or `noReceipt` ([Receipts](#receipts)).

### Receipts

A statement signed by the engine of what a relayed signature did (VC-22, mitigation 5; built in VC1-17; VC-38). Token: `basalt-receipt.1.<payload>.<sig>`.

- `payload`: base64url of UTF-8 JSON, [`receipt.schema.json`](api/mcp/vault/receipt.schema.json): `format` (1), `kind` (`APPROVAL` or `REVERT`), `itemId`, `status` (`APPROVED`; `APPLIED` or `APPLYING`), `headline`, `rowsHash` (the signed binding's, from Postgres's record, so the receipt names exactly the rows the passkey signed), `agent`, `engineId`, `at`, and `changeId` when there is one.
- `sig`: over `basalt-receipt.1\n` followed by the payload's decoded bytes. Signing exact bytes means no one needs canonical JSON.
- **Checking one needs an expected engine key from outside the receipt.** On `DIRECT`, the key pinned for that address; otherwise the engine the person already knows: the engine id or fingerprint `status` and `basalt status` report, or an earlier message of the same agent. The verifier checks the signature against that key, then parses the payload, then checks that `engineId` is that key's id and that the payload passes the closed schema; a receipt signed by any other key is refused. A receipt checked only against a key that came with it (the session's own `engineKey`, or a key the Vault or the agent hands over with it) proves only that whoever holds that key signed it, not which engine that is.
- **In the page, a receipt proves nothing beyond the session's own key.** The page can verify it only against the session's `engineKey`, which on `RELAY` is not pinned ([Handshake](#handshake)), and the page's code is what a compromised portal would change. So the page shows it with the fingerprint and asks the person to compare; the check that counts is the one in the chat, against the engine the person knows (VC1-17).
- `headline` is built from Postgres's record of the item, never from what the page sent, so a page that made the person sign another item than the one shown is caught when the receipt names the real one.
- **Where (VC1-17).** In the relay responses, on loopback and in a session: `POST …/approve` answers `{ "approvalId", "status": "APPROVED", "receipt" }` and `POST …/apply` the result `basalt revert --apply --json` prints plus `receipt` ([Local pages](#local-pages)). In the tool results that report a signed item, whoever relayed the signature (the page, the CLI, `write`'s `approval` or `request_revert`'s `signature`): `write` when it applies a held write (`approvalId`), and `request_revert` with `revertId` when it answers `APPLIED` or `APPLYING`. Each tool result with a receipt also carries the receipt line first in its `personMessage`, which the agent shows as it is. A receipt is made for each answer, so a later wait on the same item returns a new receipt naming the same item.
- **Built from Postgres, after the signature.** The engine signs a receipt only once Postgres accepted the signature, and builds the payload from its own read of the item after that, with the credential of the agent that holds it: `itemId`, `headline` (an approval's from its preview lines, as `APPROVAL_REQUIRED`'s `approval.headline`; a revert's from its binding and totals, as `list_pending`'s), `rowsHash` (from the binding text Postgres holds), `agent` (the agent's name), `status` and `changeId` (a revert's own change record, once set; an approval receipt has none). An approval's receipt says `APPROVED`, also once the write is applied; a revert's says `APPLIED` or `APPLYING` as Postgres shows it. `at` is when the engine made the receipt (UTC, whole seconds). Nothing the page, a code or the agent sent goes into a receipt except through Postgres, and only the engine's signing key signs one ([Engine keys](#engine-keys)); the agent cannot make one.
- **The receipt line.** One sentence built from the same payload, at most 300 characters: `Approved: <headline> (approval <itemId>), receipt from engine <fingerprint>.` for an approval, `Signed: <headline> (revert <itemId>), receipt from engine <fingerprint>.` for a revert. `<fingerprint>` is the engine id's fingerprint as `status` and `basalt status` show it (its first 16 characters in four groups of four, [Engine keys](#engine-keys)), for the person to compare character by character. The headline's final full stop is dropped first; then, when the line would pass 300 characters, only the headline is shortened, ending in `...` (the id and fingerprint are never cut). The rest of the `personMessage` follows it and the whole stays within the [Links in `personMessage`](#links-in-personmessage) rules.
- **No receipt.** The signature is applied whether or not a receipt can be made; the answer then carries `noReceipt` with the reason instead of `receipt` (never both), and the receipt line says why. `KEY_ERROR`: the engine key file is refused ([Engine keys](#engine-keys)); the line is `No receipt: the engine's key file is refused (basalt status shows KEY_ERROR), so nothing confirms what was signed.` `WIDENED` (provisional, an open owner question): a revert whose scope was widened on the signing page; the agent role cannot read its binding (RS-5), so the engine cannot name its rows from Postgres. The line gives the totals the agent may see and asks the person to speak up: `No receipt: the revert's scope was changed on the signing page (<rows> rows restored). If you did not change it, tell the agent at once.` (`<rows> rows to restore in batches` while `APPLYING`). Both forms of a relay response (`approve`, `apply`) carry `noReceipt` the same way.

### The engine's socket to the Vault

`wss://relay.basaltvault.app/v1/engines/<engineId>`, compiled into the binary (as the page origins are, CO-8). `vaultUrl` in `engine.json` overrides it for development and tests only, and only with a `ws://` URL on a loopback address (`127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` or `localhost`); anything else is refused when the config is loaded. The engine connects only when `vault` is `ON` and `pagesUrl` is the portal: a passkey with RP ID `localhost` cannot sign on the portal.

1. **Open**, with the header `basalt-engine-key: <signing key SPKI, base64url>`. The Vault refuses (`AUTH_FAILED`) a key that is not a valid P-256 SPKI or does not hash to the path's `engineId`.
2. **Challenge.** The Vault sends `CHALLENGE` with 32 random bytes: fresh per socket, single use, valid 30 seconds.
3. **Auth.** The engine answers `AUTH` with `version` 1 and `sig` over `basalt-vault-engine.1\n` + challenge (as sent, base64url) + `\n` + host, where host is the engine's own Vault host (`relay.basaltvault.app`, or the override's `host:port`), never one the Vault names. A bad or late answer: `AUTH_FAILED`.
4. **Ready.** The Vault sends `READY`. One live socket per engine id: a newer authenticated socket replaces the older, which is closed with `REPLACED`. After every `READY` the engine registers every live link again (registering is idempotent for the same engine), so a Vault that lost its state recovers.

Control messages are text messages, JSON per [`control-message.schema.json`](api/mcp/vault/control-message.schema.json), at most 4 KiB. Session bytes are binary `ROUTE` messages: `0x01` ‖ `0x10` (type `ROUTE`) ‖ `sid` (16 bytes) ‖ the page's message exactly as received, or the engine's message for the page.

| Message | Direction | Fields | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `REGISTER_LINK` | engine to Vault | `ref`, `linkToken`, `expiresAt` | Route page sockets for this token to me until `expiresAt` (at most 24 hours and 5 minutes ahead) |
| `UNREGISTER_LINK` | engine to Vault | `ref`, `linkToken` | Stop routing it, and close its page sockets with `LINK_UNKNOWN`. Only the engine the token is bound to can: another engine gets `LINK_CONFLICT` and the binding stays. A token that is not registered (or expired) answers `OK` |
| `OK` | Vault to engine | `ref` | Request `ref` succeeded |
| `ERROR` | Vault to engine | `ref` (when it answers one), `code` | `LINK_CONFLICT` (another engine registered the token), `LINK_LIMIT` (too many live links), `INVALID_EXPIRY`, `UNKNOWN_SESSION`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `PROTOCOL` |
| `SESSION_OPEN` | Vault to engine | `sid`, `linkToken` | A page connected for the token; its message 1 follows as a `ROUTE` |
| `SESSION_CLOSE` | either way | `sid`, `code` | The session ended (1000, or a [close code](#close-codes)); the Vault closes the page's socket with the same code |

`ref` is a positive integer the engine picks, unique among its requests waiting for an answer; `sid` is 16 random bytes the Vault picks per page socket (base64url in JSON). **No control message is trusted:** a forged `SESSION_OPEN` costs one refused handshake; a forged `OK` gives a link the page cannot reach (the person asks for the link again, which falls back if the Vault keeps failing); a forged `SESSION_CLOSE` or `ERROR` ends a session or a registration. None can open a session, read one or make the engine act on a request.

- **Reconnecting (VC-2).** Exponential backoff from 1 to 60 seconds with full jitter, reset after `READY`. A WebSocket ping every 30 seconds; no pong for 90 seconds closes and redials.
- **Registering a link (VC-15, design 6.5).** Before handing over a portal short link, the engine sends `REGISTER_LINK` and waits up to 5 seconds for `OK`. With no socket, an `ERROR` or no answer, it hands over the long link under the [link budget](#the-link-budget) rules instead, and the short link is not issued. `status` shows the socket's state.
- **A token belongs to one engine (VC-42).** The Vault binds each registered token to the engine id that registered it on an authenticated socket, and refuses (`LINK_CONFLICT`) a registration of a live token by any other engine id until the token's `expiresAt` or its `UNREGISTER_LINK`. It must keep that binding durably for the token's whole lifetime, across its own restarts and failovers. **Known limit:** if the Vault loses the binding anyway, an engine that also holds the link secret (someone who saw the link) could register the token first while the real engine reconnects. The impact is that of an unpinned `RELAY` identity ([Handshake](#handshake)): a fake fingerprint, fake receipts in the page, or denial of service, never a forged approval. Mitigations: the engine registers every live link at once after each `READY`; on `LINK_CONFLICT` for a link it holds, it revokes that link and logs it, so the item's next link (`list_pending` with `reissueLink: true`) has a new secret whose token nobody else knows; and the person compares the fingerprint.
- **Unregistering.** Reissuing a link unregisters the old token first, without waiting for its answer: an `ERROR` `LINK_CONFLICT` there (a rival engine holds the old token) does not stop the reissue, which registers the new token as usual, so a rival that took the old token cannot block it; a link that expires or is revoked is unregistered then, and its open sessions are closed with `LINK_REFUSED`. The Vault also drops a token at its `expiresAt` by itself.

### The page's socket

`wss://relay.basaltvault.app/v1/links/<linkToken>`, opened by a portal page (the Vault refuses another `Origin` with `PROTOCOL`; a check against other sites' pages, not a security boundary). The Vault closes with `LINK_UNKNOWN` when no engine registered the token or it expired, and with `ENGINE_OFFLINE` when its engine has no socket. Otherwise it sends `SESSION_OPEN` to the engine and forwards every binary message both ways unchanged and in order, as `ROUTE` messages on the engine's side. A text message from the page closes with `PROTOCOL`. When one side ends, the Vault closes the other with the same code. When the engine's socket ends (it dropped, or a newer one `REPLACED` it), the Vault closes that socket's page sockets with `ENGINE_OFFLINE`: sessions do not move to the engine's next socket. `SESSION_CLOSE` carries 1000 or a registry code only: a page that leaves with another code (a browser's 1001) or without a close frame is reported as 1000, and an engine's `SESSION_CLOSE` with any code but 1000 or one whose "Sent by" names the engine (so never `AUTH_FAILED`, `REPLACED`, `LINK_UNKNOWN`, `ENGINE_OFFLINE` or `HANDSHAKE_FAILED`) is malformed: the Vault closes the engine's socket with `PROTOCOL`.

The page sends nothing but handshake message 1 and frames; it keeps no state between sessions (no storage in Phase 1a) and never holds a key past its session.

### Close codes

WebSocket close codes, with the name as the close reason. Registry: [`close-codes.json`](api/mcp/vault/close-codes.json), checked against this table by the contract test. A code that comes from the Vault only decides what the page shows; nothing else trusts it (VC-39). What the page shows: link gone (the link expired, was replaced or is incomplete, and how to get a new one, as the loopback `NOT_FOUND` reason `LINK`), engine offline (ask the agent to check `status`, or use the long link), broken (nothing was accepted; open the link again), or try later.

| Code | Name | Sent by | When | The page shows |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4000 | `PROTOCOL` | page, engine, Vault | A message out of place or malformed (text for binary, unknown type, content that fails its schema, a second request in flight, a foreign `Origin`) | broken |
| 4001 | `UNSUPPORTED_VERSION` | page, engine, Vault | A version byte or `AUTH` version other than 1 | broken |
| 4002 | `AUTH_FAILED` | Vault | Engine socket: a bad `basalt-engine-key`, or a bad or late `AUTH` | (engine only) |
| 4003 | `REPLACED` | Vault | Engine socket: a newer authenticated socket for the same id | (engine only) |
| 4004 | `LINK_UNKNOWN` | Vault | No engine registered the token, it expired, or it was unregistered | link gone |
| 4005 | `ENGINE_OFFLINE` | Vault | The token's engine has no socket | engine offline |
| 4006 | `LINK_REFUSED` | engine | Message 1 for a link the engine does not hold, expired, revoked, with another token than `SESSION_OPEN`'s, a failing tag, or an invalid ephemeral key, all alike; on `DIRECT`, also a link whose agent's database does not record the session's address; also open sessions of a link revoked or expired | link gone |
| 4007 | `HANDSHAKE_FAILED` | page | An invalid engine ephemeral key; message 2 that does not decrypt, fails its schema or its signature; on `DIRECT`, an engine key other than the pinned one | broken |
| 4008 | `FRAME_INVALID` | page, engine | A frame that does not decrypt with the next counter, or with non-canonical padding | broken |
| 4009 | `TOO_LARGE` | page, engine, Vault | A message over its [limit](#limits), or a response body over 16 MiB | broken |
| 4010 | `RATE_LIMITED` | engine, Vault | Too many handshakes for a link, requests in a session, or connections at the Vault | try later |
| 4011 | `SESSION_LIMIT` | engine, Vault | Too many open sessions for the link or the engine | try later |
| 4012 | `TIMEOUT` | page, engine, Vault | The handshake took over 10 seconds, the session was idle 5 minutes, or it reached 30 minutes or its link's expiry; on `DIRECT`, the engine could not read its record in 5 seconds | try later |
| 4013 | `ENGINE_REVOKED` | engine | The engine's key is revoked in the database of the session's agent (M4.1, [Direct transport](#direct-transport)), on `DIRECT` (on `RELAY` planned) | engine offline; on `DIRECT` also that the engine may be revoked, with the pin left as it is ([Direct transport](#direct-transport)) |
| 4014 | `ENGINE_SHUTDOWN` | engine | The engine is stopping, or rotated its keys | engine offline |

A page that ends a session normally closes with 1000. A refusal inside a session that leaves it usable (`404`, `409`, `410`, `413`) is an HTTP status in a `RESPONSE`, not a close.

### Limits

Enforced by the engine and the page (VC-35). The Vault must carry messages up to these sizes, and may apply lower rates of its own (private numbers), never lower sizes. Rate checks come before any public-key operation.

| Limit | Value | Over it |
|---|---|---|
| Handshake message 1 | exactly 99 bytes | `PROTOCOL` |
| Handshake message 2 | at most 1 024 bytes | `TOO_LARGE` |
| Frame | at most 32 786 bytes (32 768 of padded plaintext, 2 of header, 16 of tag) | `TOO_LARGE` |
| `ROUTE` message on the engine's socket | at most 32 804 bytes (a frame plus 18) | `TOO_LARGE` |
| Control message | at most 4 KiB | `TOO_LARGE` |
| Request body | at most 16 KiB, as on loopback | `413` |
| Response body | at most 16 MiB | `TOO_LARGE` (page) |
| Handshake time | 10 seconds from `SESSION_OPEN` (engine) or from opening the socket (page) | `TIMEOUT` |
| Idle session | 5 minutes without a frame | `TIMEOUT` |
| Session lifetime | 30 minutes, and never past the link's expiry | `TIMEOUT` |
| Requests per session | 64 | `RATE_LIMITED` |
| Open sessions per link | 4 | `SESSION_LIMIT` |
| Open sessions per engine | 64 | `SESSION_LIMIT` |
| Handshakes per link | 20 per 10 minutes | `RATE_LIMITED` |
| Live links the Vault accepts per engine | at least 1 000 | `ERROR` `LINK_LIMIT`; the engine hands over the long link |

### Replay and expiry

- Session keys are fresh per session; counters refuse replays within one ([Handshake](#handshake), [Frames](#frames)).
- An approval is single-use in Postgres, a revert is applied once, and both expire (24 hours; one hour for an unsigned revert). A replayed signature is refused by Postgres (`APPROVAL_INVALID`, `REVERT_INVALID`) whatever carried it (design 6.3).
- A reissued link revokes the old one at the engine (its `psk` deleted, its token unregistered, its sessions closed with `LINK_REFUSED`). An expired link goes the same way, and the Vault drops its token at `expiresAt` on its own.
- A restarted engine keeps its links (the links file) but no session; it registers its links again after `READY`.

### What the Vault sees

**Sees (metadata only, VC-4):** engine ids and their public keys; when each engine is connected and from which IP address; link tokens, when they are registered, used and unregistered, and their expiry; page IP addresses and user agents; how many sessions and frames, their padded sizes and timing; close codes. Padding hides one frame's exact size, not these: how many frames a message took (a large response, such as a big revert, shows as many full frames), which way each went, and the rhythm of a session. From them the Vault can usually tell that a page read an item and then posted a signature (a small request answered after Postgres's time): **that an approval or revert was probably signed, and when**, though not which item, rows or result. **Never sees:** link secrets, `psk`s or session keys; item ids, rows, statements, headlines, signatures, receipts, results; any database credential; any private key. (The portal's static host sees the page path `/approvals/<id>`, an id the contract already treats as not secret.)

**What the Vault must do** (tested against the Go fake Vault, VC1-4, and attacked by a hostile one, VC1-12): forward session bytes unchanged and in order; keep one engine's sessions apart from each other and from other engines'; route a token only to the engine that registered it first, and refuse another engine's registration (`LINK_CONFLICT`); drop tokens at their expiry; accept the sizes above; send fresh single-use challenges; never log session bytes. Retention (VC-24): connection metadata at most 7 days. Nothing in the engine or the page relies on any of these: each is about service and privacy, not about what gets accepted.

### Configuration and status

- **`vault`** in `engine.json`: `ON` or `OFF` (VC-19), set by `basalt init --vault=on|off`: `on` by default with `--operated-by=agent` or when installed through a Vault-driven setup, `off` otherwise; init prints the value it chose and why (`vault: on (default for --operated-by=agent; --vault=off to turn it off)`). `--vault=on` with a local pages origin is a usage error (exit `2`), for the passkey reason above. `vaultUrl`: the development override above.
- **`status`** gains `vault` ([`status.json`](api/mcp/tools/status.json)): `state` (`OFF`, `CONNECTING`, `CONNECTED`, `UNREACHABLE` after 30 seconds without `READY`, `KEY_ERROR`), `engineId` and `fingerprint` whenever the key file loads. `basalt status` prints the same. No state is a problem: approvals work in all of them, through long links when the Vault is not there.
- **Links** follow the [link budget](#the-link-budget)'s order: the portal short link when the Vault registered it or the agent is direct-ready ([Direct transport](#direct-transport)), else the long link.
- **`directAddr`** in `engine.json` and **`engineRecord`** in `status`: [Direct transport](#direct-transport) and below.
- **`status.engineRecord`** ([`status.json`](api/mcp/tools/status.json)): `state` `RECORDED` (the agent's database records this engine's key, not revoked), `NOT_RECORDED`, `REVOKED` or `UNKNOWN` (the guard is older than version 33, the key file is refused, or the database cannot be read); `url`, the address recorded, when there is one; `recordedAt` and `revokedAt`. `basalt status` prints the same, on an engine that is running, as a service or in the foreground (the report of an engine that is down, or whose key file the CLI's user cannot read, has none). The problems `ENGINE_REVOKED` (`REVOKED`) and `DIRECT_NOT_SERVED` (`RECORDED` with an address while `directAddr` is `off`) are in [Next steps](#next-steps-per-code).
- **`status.ledger`** ([`status.json`](api/mcp/tools/status.json)): `bytes`, the size of every relation in `basalt_meta` (tables, indexes and TOAST, from `pg_class` and `pg_total_relation_size`, which need no privilege on the schema); `valuesKeptDays` (30) and `recordsKeptDays` (365), the fixed retention `prune()` applies. Absent when the guard cannot be read (the engine is down, the database cannot be reached, or `basalt_meta` is missing), never `0`. A failed size read leaves it out and adds no problem. `basalt status` prints the same.

### Direct transport

M4.1 (owner decisions VC-16, VC-21, VC-25, VC-27, CO-18, OR-5, OR-6). For an agent-operated engine whose person does not use the hosted Vault: the person runs their own tunnel (Cloudflare Tunnel, Tailscale Funnel, a reverse proxy with TLS) to a loopback listener of the engine, and the portal page connects to it straight, at an address the person recorded with the admin credential and pinned on their device by hand. The session is the one above, byte for byte, with transport `DIRECT` in the [prologue](#handshake): a tunnel that ends TLS sees only ciphertext, as the Vault does. Nothing here changes the `RELAY` transport or the engine's local pages.

**The engine address.** An origin `https://<host>[:<port>]`, the address the tunnel publishes. Who checks it (`basalt init --setup-code --engine-url`, `record_engine` in Postgres, the portal's pin page) applies the same grammar, refusing (never repairing) anything else:

- scheme `https` only; no user part, query or fragment, and no path but an empty one or `/` (the written form drops it);
- host: ASCII only, lowercase, at least two labels separated by `.`, each 1 to 63 characters of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, not starting or ending with `-`; at most 253 characters; no trailing `.`, no `%`, no `[`; an internationalized name is given in its `xn--` form (a Unicode name is refused, not converted, so no two checkers can map it differently);
- the last label starts with a letter, so no IP literal and nothing a browser's URL parser reads as a number (`2130706433`, `0x7f.1`, `127.1`);
- not `localhost` or a name under it, nor a name under `.local`, `.internal` or `.home.arpa` (the portal never calls a local engine or the person's local network, VC-25), and not `basaltvault.app` or a name under it;
- port: none (meaning 443), or decimal 1 to 65535 with no leading zero, other than `443`.

The pattern, shared by [`status.json`](api/mcp/tools/status.json) and the tests: `^https://([a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?\.)+[a-z]([a-z0-9-]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?(:[1-9][0-9]{0,4})?$` (no lookahead, so Go, Postgres and JavaScript compile it alike), plus what a pattern cannot state: the host's 253 characters, the name exclusions, the port's range and `443`. Two limits no checker here can see: a browser may still refuse an `xn--` label that is not valid punycode (the pin page then cannot pin it, which fails safe), and a public name may resolve to a loopback or private address (`127.0.0.1.nip.io`); only the person records an address, so that is theirs to avoid. The written form, everywhere it is stored or compared, is the origin with no trailing `/`. A link never carries an engine address (a bot could change it).

**Where it is pinned.** Twice, by the person, and the bot can change neither:

- **In the person's database**, with the engine's identity key, by `basalt init --setup-code <code> --engine-url <address>` on the person's computer ([Version 33](#version-33-engine-keys-and-the-pinned-address)). The setup code carries the key (`engine.publicKey`, [The setup code](#the-setup-code)); the address is given on the person's computer, never in the code. This record is what the engine reads to know its address and whether it is revoked.
- **On each device**, once, on the portal's pin page (OR-6, below). This pin is what the page trusts: it holds the address and the engine key, and the page compares every session's `engineKey` with it.

**The listener: `directAddr`.** A second listener of the engine, separate from the local pages, for the tunnel to forward to. `directAddr` in `engine.json`: a loopback `host:port` (`127.0.0.1` or `::1`), or `off`, the default. It is refused when the config is loaded unless `pagesUrl` is the portal (a local `pagesUrl` has the local pages instead, and a `localhost` passkey cannot sign on the portal), when it is not a loopback IP address with a port (the listener has no TLS: the tunnel ends TLS; the [listener rules](AGENTS.md#listeners-and-local-sockets) hold), and when it is the same address as `pagesAddr`. Set with `basalt init --direct-addr <host:port|off>` (agent mode and `--remote`; saved in `initArgs`, so a re-run keeps it). An address it cannot bind stops the engine from starting, as for `pagesAddr`. It serves only these routes; any other method or path is `404` with an empty body, and it serves no page, script or `/api/v1/` route outside a session:

| Method and path | What |
|---|---|
| `GET /v1/engine` | The probe. `200` JSON, [`direct-engine.schema.json`](api/mcp/vault/direct-engine.schema.json): `{ "engineKey", "engineId" }`, the signing key's SPKI (base64url) and its id, with `Cache-Control: no-store`. With an `Origin` of `https://portal.basaltvault.app` it adds `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` with that origin and `Vary: Origin`; with no `Origin` (the CLI's check, `basalt tunnel`) it answers without CORS; any other `Origin` is `403`. It is public: the key is not a secret, and nothing trusts this answer alone (the page's pin compares it by hand, every session checks the key again) |
| `GET /v1/session` | A WebSocket upgrade for one link session. `Origin` must be exactly `https://portal.basaltvault.app` (`403` otherwise, also when absent); no subprotocol, no extension (no `permessage-deflate`, which would leak sizes). A request that is not an upgrade is `400` |

- **Host.** Both routes take the request's `Host` only when it is an address recorded for this engine's key in the database of at least one agent the engine serves, revoked or not (so a revoked engine's honest `ENGINE_REVOKED` reaches the page) (read as below), compared as host and port: `:443` is dropped from the `Host` first, so `engine.example.com` and `engine.example.com:443` are the same address, and any other port must match the recorded one. Anything else, a loopback name included, is `400` before the route runs: these routes are reached only through the tunnel. The engine checks against the addresses it last read; a `Host` not among them makes it read the records again first (at most once every 10 seconds), so an address the person just recorded works without a restart. The engine never trusts `X-Forwarded-For`, `Forwarded`, `X-Real-IP` or `X-Forwarded-Host`.
- **The probe is not a session.** `GET /healthz` stays on the local pages listener only and keeps its loopback-only `Host` check; the direct listener has no `/healthz`. (The design's `/healthz` with CORS is replaced by `/v1/engine`, because the pin page needs the key.)
- **Headers.** The `websec` security headers on every answer. No cookies are read or set.

**The socket.** After the upgrade, binary WebSocket messages exactly as on the [page's socket](#the-pages-socket) to the Vault: the page sends handshake message 1, then frames; the engine sends message 2, then frames. There is no `ROUTE` wrapping and no control message. A text message closes with `PROTOCOL`; a message over its [limit](#limits) with `TOO_LARGE`. The engine's handshake time (10 seconds) runs from the upgrade.

- **The engine on message 1** does what it does on `RELAY`, in the same order, with transport `DIRECT` in the prologue and no `SESSION_OPEN` to compare `linkToken` with. Then, once the tag and the point are good, it reads its record in the link's agent's database (with that agent's credential, through `agent_engines`, at most 5 seconds): the key revoked closes with `ENGINE_REVOKED`; no record, or the session's `Host` not the address recorded there, closes with `LINK_REFUSED`, alike; a read that fails or takes longer closes with `TIMEOUT`. Only then message 2. So a link of an agent whose database never recorded this address does not open over it, even when another agent's database did.
- **Limits.** The [limits](#limits) hold as on `RELAY`, and sessions on both transports count together towards the per-link and per-engine numbers. Rate checks the Vault makes on `RELAY` the engine makes here, before any public-key operation: at most 16 sockets at a time that have not finished the handshake (one more is answered `503` before the upgrade), and at most 600 upgrades per minute in all (`503` over it). Message 1 must arrive within 3 seconds of the upgrade (`TIMEOUT`); the request line and headers within 5 seconds, at most 8 KiB of them (`431` over it); at most 64 connections open on the listener before an upgrade or a probe's answer. The per-link handshake limit is counted before the tag ([Handshake](#handshake)), so whoever sees a `linkToken` (the tunnel, another pinned engine the page tries) can spend that link's budget for 10 minutes and tell a live token by `RATE_LIMITED`: a denial of service and one bit of metadata, never a session. The engine cannot tell clients apart behind a tunnel, so these numbers are for the whole listener.
- **Close codes.** The engine sends the codes of the [registry](#close-codes) that name it. The Vault's codes (`LINK_UNKNOWN`, `ENGINE_OFFLINE`, `AUTH_FAILED`, `REPLACED`) never occur on `DIRECT`: a link the engine does not hold is `LINK_REFUSED`, and an engine that does not answer is a failed connection.

**Which transport, and which link.** An engine hands over a portal short link for an item when the Vault registered it ([Configuration and status](#configuration-and-status)) or when the item's agent is **direct-ready**: `directAddr` is not `off`, and the agent's database records this engine's key, not revoked, with an address. Otherwise the long link, as before. The link is the same in both cases (`https://portal.basaltvault.app/approvals/<approvalId>#s=<secret>`), with no engine address and no transport hint. On the portal, the page (VCT-4):

1. Tries `DIRECT` first, over each engine this device pinned, the one that last opened a session first, one at a time: `GET <address>/v1/engine` (`credentials: "omit"`, `cache: "no-store"`, 5 seconds), and only when its `engineKey` equals the pin, `wss://<host>/v1/session`. An engine that closes with `LINK_REFUSED` does not hold this link, and one that closes with `ENGINE_REVOKED` may be revoked: either way the page tries the next pin. At most 8 pins per device.
2. Then `RELAY`: planned, not built. The portal has no relay transport yet, so the page skips this step.
3. When neither opens a session, the page says the link could not reach the engine, and how to go on: add this engine on this device (the pin page), ask the agent for the link again, or the long link (`basalt approve`, `list_pending`).

`linkToken` is a routing value, not a secret ([Link secrets in a session](#link-secrets-in-a-session)), so trying it on another engine of the same person gives that engine no session: it cannot answer message 1 without the link's `psk`. It does learn that the person is opening a link, and the token, with which it can spend the link's handshake budget (above).

**Pinned key on `DIRECT` (VC-42).** On `DIRECT` the page compares the engine id computed from message 2's `engineKey` with the pinned one before it shows anything from the engine; a different key closes with `HANDSHAKE_FAILED`, and the page says this address now answers with a different engine and to check with the person's own computer (`basalt engine list --admin`) before pinning again. So on `DIRECT`, unlike `RELAY`, the engine's identity is checked by the page, and receipts in the page are checked against the pinned key ([Receipts](#receipts)). A revoked engine's honest answer is `ENGINE_REVOKED`. A close code travels outside the session and anyone on the path can forge one (VC-39), so the page changes no stored state on it: it shows that the engine may be revoked, says to check with `basalt engine list --admin` on the person's own computer, and offers to remove the pin, which it does only when the person confirms.

**The pin page** (VCT-4, OR-6). `https://portal.basaltvault.app/engines/add`: the person types the address; the page checks it by the rules above, fetches `/v1/engine`, refuses an `engineId` that is not the hash of `engineKey`, and shows the address and the [fingerprint](#engine-keys). The person compares the fingerprint with the one `basalt init --setup-code` printed on their own computer (or `basalt engine list --admin`), then confirms. The page keeps `{ "address", "engineKey", "engineId", "addedAt", "lastUsedAt" }` per pin in the portal origin's `localStorage` (and, under the provisional CSP below, the addresses alone in a cookie); `https://portal.basaltvault.app/engines` lists the pins and removes one. Nothing about a pin goes to the Vault, the engine or a link. A pin is per device and per browser profile: each device pins by hand, once (OR-6).

**CSP (OR-5, TP-1).** A portal page that may run `DIRECT` (`/approvals/<id>`, `/reverts/<id>`) is served with `connect-src 'self'` plus, for each pinned address, that origin (`https://<host>[:<port>]`) and its WebSocket origin (`wss://<host>[:<port>]`), and nothing broader (never `https:` or `wss:`); the relay transport adds `wss://relay.basaltvault.app` to the base when it lands (VC1-9). Decided by the owner on Nov 14, 2026 (TP-1):

- **The cookie.** After a pin is added or removed, and on every portal page that finds it stale, the page writes the pinned addresses to a first-party cookie `__Host-basalt-engines` on `portal.basaltvault.app`: the addresses in their written form, separated by `|` (a cookie value holds no comma, semicolon or space, and no address holds a `|`), the one that last opened a session first, at most 8; `Secure; Path=/; SameSite=Lax` (not `Strict`: a link clicked in a web chat is a cross-site navigation, and the edge must see the cookie on it; the cookie carries no authority), no `Domain` (the `__Host-` prefix makes the browser refuse it otherwise, so no other `*.basaltvault.app` host can set it), `Max-Age` 400 days, and `Max-Age=0` when no pin is left. Only the page sets it; nothing else on the portal reads or writes cookies. A page whose served policy (its `<meta>` `connect-src`, which the edge rewrites with the header) does not list every pin, or whose cookie does not, rewrites the cookie and reloads once per tab, so its policy lists them.
- **The edge function.** A Cloudflare Pages Functions middleware in front of the portal (`functions/_middleware.js`, logic in `web/edge/portal.js`, run only for `/approvals/*`, `/reverts/*` and `/engines/add` by `_routes.json`) reads the cookie (its name matched exactly, trimming only spaces and tabs; two cookies of that name in one request, which only another host can plant, count as none) and adds an origin only when it is an address in its written form by the [grammar](#direct-transport) (the same cases as `internal/engineaddr`, `internal/engineaddr/testdata/addresses.json`); anything else is dropped, and with nothing left the strict default of `_headers` is served unchanged. It adds the same sources to the `connect-src` of the page's own `<meta>` policy (a browser enforces both), sets the other security headers of `_headers` itself, and never answers a conditional request with `304`.
- **Per person, never shared.** Every answer of the function carries `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Vary: Cookie`, is built for its request alone, and leaves the static asset it starts from unchanged, so one person's policy is never served to another (`web/edge/portal.test.mjs`).
- **The pin page.** `/engines/add?address=<address>` (the address URL-encoded, in its written form, given once) is served with `connect-src 'self' <address>` (the `https` origin only, for the probe) and nothing from the cookie; `/engines/add` without it, and `/engines`, keep the default.
- **Defence in depth.** The CSP limits where a page that is already ours can connect. It is not what makes signing safe: that rests on subresource integrity on every script (VC1-18), the engine record pinned in Postgres ([Version 33](#version-33-engine-keys-and-the-pinned-address)) with the pinned key checked in every handshake, and the person's passkey, verified in Postgres.

With the cookie, the portal's host sees every pinned address of the device: metadata, like the Vault's. The pages of the engine's own listeners keep the `internal/websec` policy, and `basalt portal verify` compares the portal's header with a cookieless request's (the default). When the person has a Vault account, the allow list will come from the server-side record instead of the cookie (follow-up with the Relay and Vault milestone).

**Revocation by the person.** `basalt engine revoke` on the person's computer, with the admin connection string ([CLI](#cli)), calls `revoke_engine` ([Version 33](#version-33-engine-keys-and-the-pinned-address)). Revocation is per database: `engines` has no agent column, so it applies to every agent of that database. On `DIRECT` the engine reads its record per agent database at every session (after message 1's tag and point checks), before answering each request of an open session and every minute for the agents with one open, so an open session is closed at its next request, and within a minute of a revoke while the reads succeed. When a database revokes its key, the engine, for every agent in that database, on `DIRECT` today: stops handing over direct portal short links (long links still work: the person's passkey signs and Postgres checks, whatever carries it) and closes the agent's open direct sessions and refuses new ones for the agent's links with `ENGINE_REVOKED`. On either transport it reports `engineRecord.state` `REVOKED` and the problem `ENGINE_REVOKED` in `status`. A failed read on `DIRECT` refuses a new session, as above, and a failed re-read during an open session keeps it open (only a revoke ends it). A revoked key is never recorded again: after `basalt engine rotate-key` the new key is recorded with a new setup code. A revoked engine on a compromised server can ignore all this; it still cannot sign anything a person did not, and the record tells the person not to open its links.

**Planned, with the relay transport (not built in M4.1).** The relay half of revocation is not built. With the Vault connected, a revoked key does not stop the engine handing over a relay short link, and the engine does not revoke the agent's live short links, unregister their tokens at the Vault or close relay sessions with `ENGINE_REVOKED`. It does not read its record at start, at every heartbeat or at a relay session, and it does not leave the Vault when every database it serves has revoked the key. When the relay work lands, a revoked database will do all of that for its agents, and on `RELAY` a failed read will keep the last state the engine read. Until then a person who revokes the engine should treat any relay short link it handed over as live.

**What the tunnel sees.** The tunnel provider sees what the Vault sees ([What the Vault sees](#what-the-vault-sees)), minus link registrations: the engine's address, page IP addresses and user agents, `/v1/engine` probes, the number, sizes and timing of frames. It never sees a link secret, an item id, a row or a signature. A wrong or hijacked address can deny service, not read or forge: the page checks the pinned key in the handshake. The listener's routes face the internet through the tunnel, gated by 128-bit link secrets, by the [limits](#limits) and by the `Host` and `Origin` rules above.

### Test vectors

Shared files under `api/mcp/vault/testdata/` (VC1-2: `link-derivation.json`, `engine-id.json`, `padding.json`, `handshake.json`, `frame.json`, `signature.json`, `receipt.json`), each one kind, in the format of [`vectors.schema.json`](api/mcp/vault/vectors.schema.json): `LINK_DERIVATION`, `ENGINE_ID`, `PADDING`, `HANDSHAKE`, `FRAME`, `SIGNATURE` (every context above) and `RECEIPT`. Each case says what a receiver must do (`ACCEPT`, `REFUSE`, or a close code), so the refusals are pinned too: a tampered tag, a wrong `psk`, an invalid point, a compressed key, a reflected, replayed, reordered or spliced frame, non-zero padding, a signature from another context. Go tests and `node --test` read the same files; a mismatch fails `make ci`. Private keys are fixed 32-byte scalars so the bytes are reproducible (WebCrypto imports one as PKCS#8 without the optional public key and computes it); ECDSA signatures are randomized, so a vector pins one valid signature and a writer under test uses it instead of signing.

The Vault service's own vectors and tests live with it, privately.

## `basalt_meta`

- Everything BasaltDB installs lives in the `basalt_meta` schema and is removed with `DROP SCHEMA basalt_meta CASCADE`.
- The agent role has no direct `INSERT` on `basalt_meta` tables; ledger rows go through validating `SECURITY DEFINER` functions.
- Session settings use the `basalt.` prefix and are set transaction-local with `set_config(name, value, true)` (`SET LOCAL`), never session-level `SET`: `basalt.intent` (required on every agent write), `basalt.run_id`, `basalt.task_id`, `basalt.mode` (`APPLY` default, `PREVIEW`), `basalt.approval_id` (M2). The engine and `basalt revert` also set `TimeZone`, `DateStyle`, `IntervalStyle`, `extra_float_digits`, `lc_monetary` and `bytea_output` this way in every transaction ([Wire format](#wire-format), Timestamps), so row values print the same from any session; the guard's row trigger and the revert's row read and restore set them on themselves (versions 34 and 35).
- Every BasaltDB connection to Postgres (engine and CLI) works through a transaction-mode pooler: it sends unnamed statements in one round trip (pgx `QueryExecModeExec`, `internal/pgdial`), never named prepared statements, unless the connection string sets `default_query_exec_mode`.
- Schema changes to `basalt_meta` are forward-only and versioned; the installed version is recorded in `basalt_meta.schema_version`. Agent roles may `SELECT` it: the engine reads it on every agent tool call and compares the recorded versions with the ones its build installs (to an agent that cannot read the table every version is missing). AE-11: each migration is flagged **breaking** (the default) or **non-breaking**; marking one non-breaking needs a test that runs the engine against a guard without that migration.
